Unit 3  Home & Neighbourhood
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
In this unit you will…3.1  describe a home3.2  say where things are3.3  talk about your neighbourhood3.4  ask for and give directions3.5  describe things (size, shape, colour, material)3.6  make and accept offers3.7  Review & Mediate: tell a friend about a flat for rent

New in this unitLama Nasser (19) – Maya's younger sister. She is starting university in Damascus (English literature) and needs a flat.Abu Nabil – a landlord in Baramkeh.   ·   Mr Bassam – receptionist at Al-Yasmin Language Centre.

3.1  There's a big kitchen
Function: describing a home   ·   Grammar: there is / there are; a / an / some / any   ·   Pronunciation: /ð/ there, this and /θ/ bathroom, third
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What rooms and furniture are there in a home? What do flat ads say? Your teacher writes the class's ideas on the board.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
There's a big kitchen. | FLAT FOR RENT – 2 bedrooms
 | 
 | 
b  Which room? Write the room for each thing: a bed · a fridge · a sofa · a shower · a desk · a cooker
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Who is looking for a flat?   A Maya   B Maya's sister   C Lina
b  Listen again. 1 How many bedrooms are there?   2 Is there any furniture in the living room?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 3.1  A flat in Baramkeh
Abu Nabil | Come in, come in. This is the flat.
Maya | Thank you. Lama, look – it's very bright.
Lama | Yes, it is! How many bedrooms are there?
Abu Nabil | There are two bedrooms. There's a big bed in this one and there's a desk in the small one.
Lama | Great – I need a desk for my studies. Is there a balcony?
Abu Nabil | Yes, there is. It's next to the kitchen. There are some plants on it.
Maya | And the kitchen? Is there a fridge?
Abu Nabil | Yes, there's a fridge and a cooker, but there isn't a washing machine, I'm sorry.
Lama | Are there any chairs in the living room?
Abu Nabil | There's a sofa, but there aren't any chairs. And the bathroom is here – there's a shower.
Maya | And it's ten minutes from the university. What do you think, Lama?
Lama | I love it!

Language boxGrammar – there is / there are; a / an / some / any | singular | plural
+ | There's (there is) a fridge. | There are some plants.
– | There isn't a washing machine. | There aren't any chairs.
? | Is there a balcony? – Yes, there is. / No, there isn't. | Are there any chairs? – Yes, there are. / No, there aren't.
Use some in positive sentences and any in negatives and questions (plural or uncountable): There's some water. Is there any milk?
⚠ Say There are two bedrooms – not It has two bedrooms when you describe what is in a place. (Both are possible, but practise there is / are!) And not There is two bedrooms.
Practise: Complete with There's, There are, Is there, Are there, There isn't, There aren't.  1 ______ a big window in the kitchen.  2 ______ any shops near the flat? – Yes, there are.  3 ______ three chairs on the balcony.  4 ______ a garden. – No, there isn't.  5 ______ any pictures on the walls – they're empty.  6 ______ a lift in the building, so we use the stairs.
Useful phrases: Come in. · It's very bright. · What do you think? · I love it! · It's ten minutes from…
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 3.2 /ð/ (voice on): there, this, the, they · /θ/ (no voice): bathroom, third, thanks, three. Put your tongue between your teeth – don't say /z/ or /d/! Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is the landlord, B wants to rent. Use the skeleton and a card. Then swap.
A | Come in. This is the flat.
B | Thank you. How many bedrooms are there?
A | There are / There's __________.
B | Is there a __________?
A | Yes, there is. / No, there isn't, I'm sorry.
B | Are there any __________?
A | Yes, there are some __________. / No, there aren't any __________.
B | What do you think? / I love it!
Card A – flat in Mezzeh | Card B – house in Jaramana
1 bedroom · balcony ✓ · washing machine ✗ · chairs ✓ (4) · lift ✓ | 3 bedrooms · garden ✓ · fridge ✓ · beds ✗ · shops near ✓
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform without the book. Listener task: tick ✓ or cross ✗ for each thing you hear about.
☐ bedrooms | ☐ balcony | ☐ garden | ☐ fridge | ☐ washing machine | ☐ chairs
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Draw a simple plan of your home (or a home you know well – you can invent!). Don't show it. Describe it to your partner. Your partner draws it. Compare the two drawings. Then change partners and describe it again – faster.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner's home:  There are three bedrooms in Fadi's house. There's a big garden with some orange trees, but there isn't a garage.
Listener task: who has got the biggest balcony or garden?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the ad for the flat in Baramkeh. Answer the questions.
FLAT FOR RENT – BARAMKEHBright two-bedroom flat on the third floor, 10 minutes' walk from Damascus University. There's a living room with a sofa, a kitchen with a fridge and a cooker, and a bathroom with a shower. There's a small balcony with some plants. There isn't a washing machine, but there's a laundry next to the building. Quiet street with some shops and a bakery.Rent: 900,000 SYP a month (water included).   Call: Abu Nabil, 0955 300 214 (after 4 pm).
1  Which floor is the flat on?
2  What can you do without a washing machine?
3  When can you call Abu Nabil?
Writer's boxA flat ad: headline (FLAT FOR RENT + area) → size + location → rooms and furniture (There's… There are…) → one problem + solution (There isn't…, but…) → the street / area → rent + contact.Use adjectives to sell the flat: bright, quiet, big, modern, sunny.Use with: a kitchen with a fridge.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
FLAT FOR RENT – ______________   ______________ flat on the ______ floor, ______ minutes from ______________. There's a ______________ with ______________ and ______________. There are ______________. There isn't ______________, but ______________. ______________ street with ______________.   Rent: ______ a month.   Call: ______________.
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
there is / there are – singular and plural correct | 
a / some / any used correctly | 
All parts: headline, rooms, problem + solution, area, rent, contact | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Two or three students read one sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write an ad for your home or your dream home (50 words or more). Make it sound great – but be honest about one problem!







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's ad as someone who wants to rent. ★ I like…  ✓ Can you add the rent?  ? Is there a…?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Put the ads on the wall. Walk around. Put a sticky note on the home you want to rent: I want this flat because…
HomeworkWrite an email to the person whose ad you chose (30+ words). Say you are interested and ask two questions: Is there a lift? Are there any schools near the flat?

3.2  Where are my keys?
Function: saying where things are   ·   Grammar: prepositions of place; something / anything / somewhere / anywhere   ·   Pronunciation: word stress: beTWEEN, beHIND, OPposite
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What things do people often lose at home? Where do you keep them? Make two lists.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
My keys are on the table. | Your keys are in the drawer.
 | 
 | 
b  Draw a box on the board. Your teacher puts a pen in, on, under, next to, behind, in front of the box. Say where it is.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What is Rami looking for?   A his phone   B his keys   C his wallet
b  Listen again. 1 Where does Rami usually put them?   2 Where are they in the end?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 3.3  Late for work
Rami | Omar! Have you seen my keys? I can't find them anywhere!
Omar | Aren't they on the shelf next to the door? You usually put them there.
Rami | No, they aren't. There's nothing on the shelf.
Omar | Are they in your jacket?
Rami | No… there's something in my pocket, but it's only a pen.
Omar | What about under the sofa? Things often fall there.
Rami | Hmm… no, there isn't anything under the sofa. Only dust!
Omar | Look in the kitchen. You were there this morning. Are they behind the coffee pot?
Rami | Wait… yes! They're between the coffee pot and the bread. Thanks, Omar!
Omar | You're welcome. Now go – you're late!

Language boxGrammar – prepositions of placein | on | under | next to | between | behind | in front of | opposite
in the drawer | on the shelf | under the sofa | next to the door | between the pot and the bread | behind the pot | in front of the TV | opposite the bakery
something / anything / nothing; somewhere / anywhere
+  There's something in my pocket. · They're somewhere in the kitchen.
–  There isn't anything under the sofa. · I can't find them anywhere.  (= There's nothing under the sofa.)
?  Is there anything in the box? · Are my keys anywhere in the car?
⚠ Don't use two negatives: There isn't nothing → There isn't anything or There's nothing.
Practise: Choose the correct word.  1 Is there (something / anything) in the fridge?  2 I'm sure my phone is (somewhere / anywhere) in this room.  3 There's (anything / nothing) on the shelf.  4 I can't see my glasses (somewhere / anywhere).  5 There's (something / anything) under the bed – a shoe!
Note: Have you seen…? – learn this as a phrase (Unit 1). We study it in Book 2.
Useful phrases: I can't find… anywhere! · What about…? · Look in… · Wait… yes! · Now go – you're late!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 3.4 Stress: beTWEEN · beHIND · OPposite · NEXT to · in FRONT of. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A has lost something. B helps. Use the skeleton and a card. Then swap.
A | I can't find my __________ anywhere!
B | Aren't they / Isn't it __________? You usually put it / them there.
A | No, there's nothing __________.
B | What about __________?
A | Hmm… no, there isn't anything __________.
B | Look __________.
A | Wait… yes! It's / They're __________. Thanks!
Card A – glasses | Card B – phone charger
usually: on the desk · not: in the bag, under the bed · found: behind the TV | usually: next to the bed · not: in the drawer, on the sofa · found: between the books on the shelf
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: where is the thing in the end? Write it.
 4   Your turn – hide and find   (9 min)
Draw a room with ten things in it (a table, a lamp, a bag, books, a cup…). Your partner draws the same room empty. Tell your partner where everything is. They draw it. Then hide one small thing (a key, a coin) in your picture – your partner asks Is it in / on / under…? to find it.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class where your partner's hidden thing was and how you found it:  Hiba's coin was under the cup. There wasn't anything in the bag, and there was nothing on the shelf. (Learn was / wasn't as phrases here.)
Listener task: who found the hidden thing in the fewest questions?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Omar leaves a note for Rami before a trip to Aleppo. Read and answer.
Rami,I'm in Aleppo until Sunday. A few things: the spare keys are in the small drawer next to the fridge. The electricity bill is on my desk, under the green folder – can you pay it on Thursday? There's some kibbeh in the freezer for you (you're welcome!). Please don't touch anything on my desk – my computer parts are everywhere. If there's a problem, I'm on WhatsApp.Omar
1  Where are the spare keys?
2  What does Omar want Rami to do on Thursday?
3  Why can't Rami touch anything on the desk?
Writer's boxA note to a housemate or family member: name, → why you write → where things are (one thing per sentence) → a request → contact → your name.Be exact: in the small drawer next to the fridge, not just in the kitchen.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
,   I'm ______________ until ______. A few things: the ______________ is / are ______________. The ______________ is ______________ – can you ______________? There's some ______________ in ______________. Please don't ______________. If there's a problem, ______________.   
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
Prepositions of place are exact | 
something / anything / nothing used correctly | 
All parts: reason, where things are, a request, contact | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
You're going away for a week. Write a note to a family member or friend who is staying in your home (50 words or more). Tell them where four important things are and ask them to do one thing.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's note as the person staying in the home. ★ Very clear!  ✓ Can you add…?  ? Where is the…?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two notes are read aloud. The class says: "I can find everything!" or asks a question.
HomeworkWrite a reply text message to your partner's note (20+ words): No problem! I found the keys in… But I can't find… anywhere!

3.3  Is there a bakery near here?
Function: talking about your neighbourhood   ·   Grammar: adjective + noun; near / far from   ·   Pronunciation: /v/ and /f/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What places are there in a neighbourhood? Which are important for you? Make a class list.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Is there a pharmacy near here? | There's a good bakery on my street.
 | 
 | 
b  Where do you go to…? buy bread → ______  pray → ______  post a parcel → ______  buy medicine → ______  play football → ______
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Lama meets…   A a new classmate   B a new neighbour   C a shop owner
b  Listen again. 1 Where is the bakery?   2 Is there a gym near the building?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 3.5  On the stairs
Umm Ziad | Hello! Are you the new girl in Flat 7?
Lama | Yes, I am. I'm Lama. I'm a student at the university.
Umm Ziad | Welcome! I'm Umm Ziad, from Flat 5. Do you need anything?
Lama | Thank you. Is there a bakery near here?
Umm Ziad | Yes, there's a very good bakery on the corner, opposite the mosque. Go early – the bread is still hot at seven!
Lama | Great. And is there a supermarket?
Umm Ziad | There's a small supermarket next to the bakery, and there's a big one on the main street, five minutes from here.
Lama | Is there a gym in the area? I like running.
Umm Ziad | No, there isn't a gym near here, but there's a lovely park ten minutes away. Lots of young people run there in the evening.
Lama | That's perfect. Thank you so much!
Umm Ziad | You're welcome. Knock on my door any time!

Language boxGrammar – adjective + noun; near / far fromThe adjective comes before the noun: a good bakery · a small supermarket · a lovely park.  ⚠ Not a bakery good. Adjectives never take -s: two big parks, not bigs parks.With be, the adjective comes after: The bread is hot. The park is lovely.Distance: It's near here. · It's far from my house. · It's five minutes from here. · It's ten minutes away. · It's on the corner / on the main street.Practise: Correct the mistakes.  1 There's a restaurant cheap on my street.  2 There are two schools bigs near my house.  3 The park is near from here.  4 My office is far my home.  5 There's a mosque beautiful opposite the bakery.Useful phrases: Are you the new… ? · Welcome! · Do you need anything? · Go early. · That's perfect. · Knock on my door any time!Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 3.6 /v/ (teeth on lip + voice) and /f/ (no voice): very / ferry · van / fan · five · lovely · office · favourite. Listen and repeat. Don't say /f/ for /v/!
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is new in the building, B is a neighbour. Use the skeleton and the map card. Then swap.
B | Hello! Are you the new __________ in Flat __________?
A | Yes, I am. I'm __________.
B | Welcome! Do you need anything?
A | Thank you. Is there a __________ near here?
B | Yes, there's a __________ __________ on / opposite / next to __________.
A | And is there a __________?
B | No, there isn't, but there's a __________ __________ minutes from here.
A | That's perfect. Thank you!
Map card – Al-Ward Street | 
On the corner: a small bakery (very good bread) | Opposite the bakery: a pharmacy (open late)
Next to the pharmacy: a cheap restaurant | No supermarket – a big one is 10 minutes away
No gym – a sports club is 15 minutes by bus | At the end of the street: a beautiful old church
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the adjective you hear with each place: a ______ bakery, a ______ restaurant, a ______ church.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Draw a simple map of the street or area where you live (or a street you know well). Your partner is a new neighbour. They ask about four places. Answer with adjectives and near / far from / opposite / on the corner. Then change partners.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner's neighbourhood:  Tareq lives in a quiet area in Dummar. There's a big park near his house, and there's a good falafel restaurant on the corner. But the supermarket is far from his building.
Listener task: which neighbourhood do you want to live in? Why?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Lina writes about her neighbourhood for the class page. Read and answer.
My neighbourhood – Lina HaddadI live in Shaalan, in the centre of Damascus. It's a busy area with lots of shops, cafés and restaurants. My building is on a small street near the main road. There's a great juice shop on the corner – I have a fresh orange juice there every Saturday. There's also an old bookshop opposite my building. The owner, Abu Samir, knows every book in the shop! The only problem is the traffic: it's very noisy in the evening. But I love my neighbourhood because everything is near.
1  Where is Shaalan?
2  What does Lina do every Saturday?
3  What is the problem in her neighbourhood?
Writer's boxDescribing a place: where it is → general description (a busy / quiet area) → two or three special places (with adjectives and locations) → one problem → why you like it.Use also to add an idea and but for a contrast: The only problem is… But I love…

There's also ______________. The only problem is ______________. But I love my neighbourhood because ______________.
 6P   Check together   (5 min)

Capital letters (places!) and full stops |

there is / there are correct |

Read one sentence before and after the fix.

Write "My neighbourhood" (60 words or more). Describe three places with adjectives and say where they are. Include one problem.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)

3.4  Turn left at the pharmacy
Function: asking for and giving directions   ·   Grammar: imperatives for directions   ·   Pronunciation: stress on the important words
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  How do people give directions? What do we say? What do we write in a message? Make two lists.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Go straight on. | Take the first street on the left.
 | 
 | 
b  Draw arrows for: turn left · turn right · go straight on · go past · cross the road · it's on your right
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
Route map – from the bus stop on Mezzeh HighwaySTART: the bus stop on Mezzeh Highway. Walk along Al-Ward Street to Al-Shifa Pharmacy on the corner.Straight on along Al-Ward Street (don't turn):  the bakery → the mosque (at the end of the street).Turn LEFT at the pharmacy into Al-Jalaa Street and walk ↓On your LEFT | Al-Jalaa Street ↓ | On your RIGHT
the bank | traffic lights | the café
a school |  | the supermarket
AL-YASMIN LANGUAGE CENTRE |  | the post office (opposite Al-Yasmin)

a  Books closed. Listen. Who is lost?   A Lama   B Sara, a new student   C a tourist
b  Listen again. 1 Where does Sara turn left?   2 What is opposite the centre?
c  Books open. Listen and read. Follow the route on the map.
🔊 Track 3.7  Sara is lost
Sara | Hi Maya, it's Sara. Sorry, I'm lost! I'm at the bus stop on Mezzeh Highway. How do I get to Al-Yasmin?
Maya | No problem, it's easy. Can you see a pharmacy on the corner?
Sara | Yes, I can – Al-Shifa Pharmacy.
Maya | That's my pharmacy! OK, walk to the pharmacy and turn left into Al-Jalaa Street.
Sara | Turn left at the pharmacy. OK.
Maya | Then go straight on. Go past the traffic lights and the bank. Don't turn right at the lights!
Sara | Go past the traffic lights… and then?
Maya | The centre is on your left, next to a school. It's opposite the post office. It's about five minutes from the bus stop.
Sara | On the left, opposite the post office. Thanks, Maya!
Maya | See you in class!

Language boxGrammar – imperatives for directionsGo straight on. · Turn left / right at the pharmacy. · Take the first / second street on the left. · Go past the café. · Cross the road. · Don't turn right!Where is it? It's on your left / right. · It's at the end of the street. · It's on the corner of X Street and Y Street.Asking: Excuse me, how do I get to…? · Where's the nearest…? · Is it far? – No, it's about five minutes on foot.Practise: Look at the map. Start at the bus stop. Where are you?  1 Turn left at the pharmacy. Go straight on to the traffic lights. It's on your left. → ______  2 Walk along Al-Ward Street. Go past the pharmacy and the bakery. It's at the end of the street. → ______  3 Turn left at the pharmacy. Go past the traffic lights and the supermarket. It's on your right, opposite Al-Yasmin. → ______Useful phrases: Sorry, I'm lost! · How do I get to…? · It's easy. · Can you see…? · (repeat to check) Turn left at the pharmacy. OK.Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 3.8 Stress the important words: Turn LEFT at the PHARmacy. · Go PAST the CAfé. · It's on your RIGHT. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. Use the map. A is at the bus stop and asks for a place. B gives directions. A repeats to check. Then swap.
A | Excuse me, how do I get to the __________?
B | It's easy. Walk to __________ and turn __________.
A | Turn __________ at __________. OK.
B | Then go __________. Go past __________.
A | And then?
B | The __________ is on your __________, next to / opposite __________.
A | Thanks a lot!
A asks for… | B asks for…
the post office · the mosque | the café · the supermarket
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: follow the route on the map with your finger. Do you arrive at the right place?
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Think of a real route: from this centre to your home, to a good restaurant, or to a bus stop. Give your partner directions. Your partner draws the route as you speak. Then check: is the drawing right? Change partners.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class your partner's route:  From here to Fadi's favourite shawarma place: turn right, go straight on to the big roundabout, take the second street on the left. It's next to a bank.
Listener task: can you guess the place? Do you know it?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Lama sends her friend directions to her new flat. Read and answer.
Hi Rasha! Here are the directions to my flat for Thursday. Take the minibus to Baramkeh and get off at the university gate. Cross the road and walk towards the big hotel. Take the first street on the right – it's next to a juice shop. Go straight on for about 200 metres. My building is on the left, opposite a small mosque. It's the blue door. I'm on the third floor, Flat 7. Don't take the lift – it's broken! Call me if you get lost: 0938 551 207. See you at 5!
1  Where does Rasha get off the minibus?
2  What is opposite Lama's building?
3  Why can't Rasha take the lift?
Writer's boxDirections message: transport → start point → steps in order (one step per sentence) → landmarks (next to, opposite) → the building / door / floor → a warning → phone number.Imperatives start with the base verb: Take… Cross… Go… Use then and after that to link steps.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Hi ______! Here are the directions to ______________. Take ______________ and get off at ______________. ______________ and walk ______________. Take the ______ street on the ______. Go straight on ______________. My ______________ is on the ______, opposite ______________. It's ______________. Call me if you get lost: ______. See you at ______!
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
Each step starts with a verb (imperative) | 
Landmarks: next to / opposite / on the left | 
Steps in the right order + building details + phone | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one step before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Invite a classmate to your home (or to a place you love). Write the directions from this centre or from a well-known place (60 words or more).







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's directions and draw the route. ★ Easy to follow!  ✓ Can you add a landmark?  ? Is it far from…?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two students read their directions. The class listens and says the last landmark.
HomeworkWrite a reply to your partner's invitation (25+ words): say yes, check one step (So I turn right at the juice shop?) and say what time you can come.

3.5  It's small and made of leather
Function: describing things   ·   Grammar: adjective order (size – shape/age – colour – material); made of   ·   Pronunciation: word stress in adjectives
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  Your teacher holds up a bag, a cup, a phone. Describe them. What words do we use? Make groups.
size | shape | colour | material
big, small, long… | round, square… | black, dark blue… | leather, plastic…
 |  |  | 
 |  |  | 
b  What is it made of? a window → ______  a scarf → ______  a bottle → ______  a table → ______  a ring → ______
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What did Lina lose?   A a scarf   B a bag   C a phone
b  Listen again. 1 What colour is it?   2 What's inside it?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 3.9  At reception
Mr Bassam | Good evening, Lina. Can I help you?
Lina | I hope so! I think I left my bag in the café on Thursday.
Mr Bassam | OK, we've got some bags here. What does it look like?
Lina | It's a small brown leather bag.
Mr Bassam | Is it round or square?
Lina | It's square, with a long thin strap. And there's a small silver key ring on it – it's a star.
Mr Bassam | What's inside it?
Lina | My glasses in a red case, some keys, and a notebook.
Mr Bassam | Let me look… Is this it?
Lina | No, that one's black and it's made of plastic. Oh – that's it! Next to the umbrella!
Mr Bassam | Here you are. Can you sign here, please?
Lina | Of course. Thank you so much!

Language boxGrammar – describing thingsAdjective order: size → shape / age → colour → material → nounsize | shape / age | colour | material | noun
a small | square | brown | leather | bag
a long | old | blue | wool | scarf
a big | round | white | plastic | box
Usually use only two or three adjectives: a small brown bag · an old wooden box.
made of: It's made of leather / glass / metal / wood / plastic / cotton / wool / silver / gold.  Questions: What's it made of? What does it look like? What colour is it?
Practise: Put the words in order.  1 bag / black / big / a  2 a / ring / silver / small  3 scarf / cotton / white / a  4 a / table / round / wooden  5 an / phone / old / grey
Note: I left… / I think I left… – learn as phrases here (past simple comes in Unit 8).
Useful phrases: I hope so! · What does it look like? · Let me look… · Is this it? · That's it! · Can you sign here, please?
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 3.10 Word stress: LEAther · PLAStic · MEtal · SILver · WOOden · SQUARE · YELlow. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A has lost the thing on the card. B is the receptionist. Use the skeleton. Then swap.
B | Good afternoon. Can I help you?
A | I hope so! I think I left my __________ here.
B | What does it look like?
A | It's a __________ __________ __________.
B | What's it made of?
A | It's made of __________. And __________.
B | Is this it?
A | No, that one's __________. / Yes, that's it! Thank you!
Card A – umbrella | Card B – phone
long · black · red handle · made of plastic and metal | small · old · grey · in a green leather case · a crack on the screen
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the three adjectives you hear in the right order.
 4   Your turn – guessing game   (9 min)
Think of an object in this room or in your bag – don't say it! Describe it: size, shape, colour, material, what you use it for. Your partner guesses. Then describe something special you own (a gift, a family object) – your partner asks three questions about it.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner's special object:  Omar has got an old silver watch. It's round and it's made of silver. It was his grandfather's. He wears it on Fridays.
Listener task: ask one question: What colour is…? Is it heavy?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING

 7P   Respond   (5 min)

3.6  Shall I help you?
Function: making and accepting offers   ·   Grammar: Shall I…? / I'll… / Can I help?   ·   Pronunciation: weak shall /ʃəl/; I'll /aɪl/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  When do people offer help? (moving house, guests, a heavy bag…) What do they say? What do they write? Make two lists.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Shall I carry that? | Let me know if you need anything!
 | 
 | 
b  Match the offer to the situation.  1 Your friend has got three heavy bags.  2 Your guest looks cold.  3 Your colleague can't find a word.  →  A I'll close the window.  B Shall I check the dictionary?  C Shall I take one?
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Lama is…   A moving into her flat   B cleaning her flat   C leaving her flat
b  Listen again. 1 What does Rami offer to do?   2 Who is going to make the coffee?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 3.11  Moving day
Maya | OK, everyone, this is the last box from the car!
Rami | That box looks heavy, Maya. Shall I carry it?
Maya | Yes, please! It's Lama's books. Can you take it to the small bedroom?
Rami | Sure. I'll put it next to the desk.
Lina | Lama, shall I put the cups in the kitchen?
Lama | That's very kind of you, Lina, thank you. The cupboard next to the fridge is empty.
Lina | No problem. And I'll wash them first – they're dusty.
Lama | You're all wonderful. I'll make some coffee for everyone!
Maya | No, no, you sit down. I'll make the coffee. Where's the coffee pot?
Lama | Er… I think it's in one of the boxes!
Rami | Don't worry. I'll go to the bakery and get some ka'ak. Do you want anything else?
Lama | No, thanks. Just some ka'ak is perfect!

Language boxGrammar – offersOffer as a question | Offer as a promise | Answers
Shall I carry it? · Can I help? · Do you want me to…? (phrase) | I'll (I will) put it next to the desk. · I'll make the coffee. | Yes, please! · That's very kind of you. · Thanks, that's great.  No, thanks. I'm fine. · No, no – you sit down.
Shall I / I'll + base verb: Shall I carry…? I'll make…  ⚠ Not Shall I to carry or I'll making.
I'll = a decision now, at the moment of speaking: (The box looks heavy.) I'll carry it.
Practise: Make an offer with Shall I…? or I'll…  1 It's hot in here. → ______  2 Your friend's phone battery is dead. → ______  3 The guests are hungry. → ______  4 Your mother is tired after work. → ______  5 Your colleague doesn't understand the email. → ______
Useful phrases: That looks heavy. · That's very kind of you. · You sit down. · Don't worry. · Do you want anything else? · Just… is perfect.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 3.12 Shall I…? is weak and fast: /ʃəl aɪ/ · I'll /aɪl/ – one syllable: I'll make the coffee. I'll go. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is the host or the person who needs help. B makes offers. Use the skeleton and a card. Then swap.
B | That looks __________. Shall I __________?
A | Yes, please! Can you __________?
B | Sure. I'll __________.
A | That's very kind of you.
B | And shall I __________?
A | No, thanks. I'm fine. / No, no – I'll __________.
Card A – guests are coming | Card B – at the office
heavy shopping bags · put them in the kitchen · offer: make the salad – no (A will do it) | a big pile of papers · take them to Mr Khaled · offer: print the report – yes
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: tick the phrases you hear.
☐ Shall I…? | ☐ I'll… | ☐ Yes, please! | ☐ That's very kind of you. | ☐ No, thanks. | ☐ You sit down.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
You are hosting guests at your real home. Your partner is a guest who wants to help. They offer three things (Shall I…?). You accept two and refuse one politely. Then swap. Then do it again with a new partner – but now you offer things to your guest (I'll get you some juice. Shall I open the window?).
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class what your partner offered:  Fadi offered to wash the dishes and to bring some chairs from his flat. I said yes. He offered to cook, but I said no – I love cooking! (Learn offered to as a phrase.)
Listener task: who is the best guest in the class?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Lama puts a note under the doors of her new neighbours. Read and answer.
Dear neighbours,My name is Lama Nasser and I'm your new neighbour in Flat 7. I'm a student at Damascus University. I'm sorry about the noise on Friday – we were moving my furniture! I'd like to say hello properly, so I'll be at home on Saturday afternoon and I'll make some coffee and maamoul. Please come and visit! If you need any help with English or with your computer, just knock on my door – I'll be happy to help.Best wishes,Lama (Flat 7)
1  Why does Lama say sorry?
2  What will she do on Saturday afternoon?
3  What two things does she offer to help with?
Writer's boxA note to neighbours: Dear neighbours, → who you are → an apology or a reason → an invitation → an offer of help (I'll… / If you need…, just…) → Best wishes, → name + flat number.Use I'll for promises and offers in writing: I'll be happy to help.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Dear neighbours,   My name is ______________ and I'm your new neighbour in ______________. I'm ______________. I'm sorry about ______________. I'd like to ______________, so I'll ______________ on ______________. Please ______________! If you need any help with ______________, just ______________ – I'll ______________.   Best wishes,   ______
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
I'll / Shall I + base verb | 
An invitation and an offer of help | 
All parts: who you are, apology / reason, invitation, offer, ending | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read your offer sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Choose one: (a) you are new in a building – write to your neighbours; (b) a new family moves into your building – write a welcome note to them and offer help (50 words or more).







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's note as the neighbour. ★ What a kind note!  ✓ Can you add your flat number?  ? What time on Saturday?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two notes are read aloud. The class answers the invitation: Thank you! I'll come at…
HomeworkWrite a reply as the neighbour (25+ words). Accept the invitation or the offer, and offer something back: Welcome! I'll bring some… / Shall I show you…?

3.7  Review & Mediate: I saw a great flat!
Function: telling a friend about a rental ad   ·   Recycling: Units 1–3   ·   Exam practice: A2 Key Reading Part 3, Writing Part 6, Listening Part 3
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready – Unit 3 quiz   (6 min)
In teams. Correct the mistakes! 1 There is two bedrooms.  2 Are there some chairs?  3 I can't find my keys somewhere.  4 It's a bag leather brown.  5 The park is near from here.  6 Shall I to carry your bag?
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Read the ad on Omar's phone.
FLAT FOR RENT – MEZZEH 86One-bedroom flat, 4th floor (there's a lift). Kitchen with fridge and cooker, new washing machine. Big balcony – great view of the city! Furnished: bed, sofa, table and 2 chairs.Near: bus stop (2 min), supermarket, bakery, pharmacy.  Not near: park.Rent: 750,000 SYP a month + electricity.  Available: from 1 November.Contact: Abu Rabih, WhatsApp only, 0944 870 155.
b  Books closed. Listen. Omar tells his cousin Nabil about the ad. What is Nabil's question about the balcony?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 3.13  A flat for Nabil
Omar | Nabil, I saw a great flat for you! It's in Mezzeh 86.
Nabil | Really? How many bedrooms are there?
Omar | Just one, but it's furnished – there's a bed, a sofa, a table and two chairs.
Nabil | Is there a washing machine? I don't want to go to the laundry every week.
Omar | Yes, there's a new one. And there's a big balcony with a great view.
Nabil | Nice! Is the balcony sunny?
Omar | The ad doesn't say. You need to ask. It's on the fourth floor, but there's a lift.
Nabil | Good. And what's near it?
Omar | A bus stop – just two minutes away – and a supermarket, a bakery and a pharmacy. But there isn't a park near it.
Nabil | That's OK. How much is it?
Omar | Seven hundred and fifty thousand a month, but you pay for electricity too. It's free from the first of November.
Nabil | Perfect – I start my new job on the fifth! How can I call the owner?
Omar | You can't call him – you need to send a WhatsApp message. I'll send you the number.

Language box – telling someone about an adTurn the short ad into full sentences:4th floor (there's a lift) → It's on the fourth floor, but there's a lift.   Not near: park → There isn't a park near it.   + electricity → You pay for electricity too.If the ad doesn't answer a question, say so: The ad doesn't say. You need to ask.Useful phrases: I saw a great… for you! · It's furnished. · It's free / available from… · You pay for… too. · The ad doesn't say. · I'll send you the number.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A reads ad A, B reads ad B. Don't show it! Tell your partner about your ad. Your partner asks four questions. If the ad doesn't say, say so! Then decide together: which flat is better for a student?
Ad A – Mezzeh | Ad B – Jaramana
2 bedrooms · 1st floor · no lift · garden 🌳 · not furnished · near: school, park · rent 1,000,000 SYP (water + electricity included) · free now | studio (1 room + kitchen) · 6th floor · lift ✓ · furnished (bed, desk) · near: university bus, cafés · rent 600,000 SYP + water + electricity · free from 1 December
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the rent and one good thing about each flat. Which flat does the class prefer?
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Look for a real ad on your phone (a flat, a car, a job, a course) – or remember one. Tell your partner about it in full sentences. Your partner asks questions and decides: I'm interested because… / It's not for me because…
 4R   Report – mediation   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner's ad, in your own words:  Hiba saw an ad for a used car. It's a small white Kia, and it's ten years old. It costs…, and the owner lives in Muhajireen. The ad doesn't say…
Listener task: which ad is the best offer?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING AND UNIT CHECK
 5   Read – exam practice (A2 Key Reading Part 3)   (7 min)
Read the text. For each question, choose the correct answer A, B or C.
My street in the Old City – by Nadia HaddadI live in a small street in Bab Touma, in the Old City of Damascus. My house is two hundred years old. It hasn't got a garden, but there's a lovely courtyard in the middle with a fountain and a lemon tree. In summer we sit there every evening because it's cool.The street is too narrow for cars, so it's very quiet. There's a small grocery shop at the end of the street, and my neighbour, Abu Elias, has a workshop where he makes wooden boxes. Tourists often stop to watch him work.The only problem is that the houses are old and they need a lot of work. Last winter water came through our kitchen ceiling! But I never want to leave. My grandmother says the street is like one big family – and she's right.
1  Nadia's family sits in the courtyard in summer because   A it's near the kitchen.   B it isn't hot there.   C the garden is small.
2  The street is quiet because   A cars can't go there.   B there aren't any shops.   C tourists never come.
3  Abu Elias   A sells food.   B is a tourist guide.   C makes things from wood.
4  What happened last winter?   A The family moved.   B There was a problem with the kitchen.   C They built a new ceiling.
5  What does Nadia think about her street?   A She wants to live somewhere new.   B It's friendly.   C It's too old.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)

Near the flat there's ______, but there isn't ______. The rent is ______ a month and you pay for ______ too. It's free from ______. You need to send a WhatsApp message to ______.   ______
 6P   Check together   (5 min)

A  Grammar and vocabulary. Choose the correct answer.
1  ___ two bathrooms in the house.   a) There is   b) There are   c) It is
2  Are there ___ shops near your flat?   a) some   b) any   c) a
3  I can't find my phone ___.   a) somewhere   b) anywhere   c) nowhere

2  What does Lama need to buy?   A a desk   B chairs   C a washing machine
3  Where is the nearest supermarket?   A on the corner   B next to the bakery   C on the main street
4  What is wrong with the flat?   A It's noisy.   B The lift is broken.   C It's dark.
5  What will Maya do on Saturday?   A bring a plant   B clean the flat   C cook dinner
🔊 Track 3.14  Audio script (read after the check)
Maya | So, Lama, how's the new flat?
Lama | I love it! The bedroom is nice and the kitchen is OK, but the balcony is my favourite room. I study there every morning.
Maya | Do you need anything?
Lama | Yes – there's a sofa in the living room, but there aren't any chairs. I need two chairs for the table. The desk is fine.
Maya | And the shops? Is the supermarket far?
Lama | The small one next to the bakery is closed now, so I go to the big one on the main street.
Maya | Is everything OK with the building?
Lama | It's quiet and bright, but the lift doesn't work! I walk up to the third floor every day.
Maya | Good exercise! I'll come on Saturday and I'll bring you a plant for the balcony.
Lama | Thank you! And I'll cook dinner for us.

Unit 4  Food & Eating Out
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
In this unit you will…4.1  talk about food you like and don't like4.2  order food in a café or restaurant4.3  ask about quantities and prices4.4  give instructions for a recipe4.5  invite people and accept or refuse4.6  compliment and thank a host4.7  Review & Mediate: explain a menu to a visitor

New in this unitBeit Al-Yasmin Restaurant – a small restaurant in the Old City, near Bab Sharqi.   ·   Abu Hassan – a vegetable seller in the souq.Mr Peter Lang – a German visitor; he buys medical equipment from Lina's company.

4.1  I love olives, but I don't eat meat
Function: talking about food preferences   ·   Grammar: countable and uncountable nouns; my favourite… / I prefer…   ·   Pronunciation: /ɪ/ and /iː/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What do you eat for breakfast, lunch and dinner? What food do you love? What food do you hate? Your teacher writes your ideas on the board.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I love olives. | My favourite dish is kibbeh.
 | 
 | 
b  Put the food in groups: bread · rice · chicken · fish · cheese · eggs · olives · apples · tomatoes · beans · milk · yoghurt.   Fruit and vegetables: ______   Meat and fish: ______   Dairy (from milk): ______   Other: ______
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Where are Lina and Omar?   A at a restaurant   B in the centre café   C at Omar's flat
b  Listen again. 1 What does Lina have for breakfast?   2 What is Omar's favourite dish?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 4.1  At the centre café
Omar | You're only having tea, Lina? Aren't you hungry?
Lina | Not really. I have a big breakfast every day – bread, cheese, olives, eggs and some tea.
Omar | That's a good breakfast! I only have coffee in the morning. I eat a lot at lunch.
Lina | What's your favourite dish? Kibbeh, right?
Omar | Yes, I love kibbeh – my mother's kibbeh is the best. And I love rice with chicken. What about you?
Lina | My favourite dish is mujaddara – rice and lentils with onions. I don't eat much meat.
Omar | Really? Do you eat fish?
Lina | Yes, I love fish. And I eat a lot of fruit and vegetables. I prefer apples to oranges.
Omar | And sweets?
Lina | Oh, I love sweets! Especially baklava. But only on Fridays!

Language boxGrammar – countable and uncountable nounsCountable (you can count them) | Uncountable (you can't count them)
an apple – two apples · an egg – six eggs · an olive – some olives · a tomato – three tomatoes | bread · rice · cheese · meat · fish · milk · water · tea · coffee · fruit · food
Use a / an, numbers, and plural -s: I eat an egg. I love olives. | No a / an, no -s: I love bread. (not a bread, not breads)
With both: some (+), any (– / ?), a lot of: some eggs / some cheese · a lot of vegetables / a lot of rice. Note: fruit and food are usually uncountable.
Talking about preferences: My favourite dish / food is… · I prefer apples to oranges. · I love / like / don't like / hate… · I don't eat (much) meat.
Practise: Correct the mistakes.  1 I have a bread and a cheese for breakfast.  2 I love apple.  3 Do you eat many rice?  4 I prefer tea than coffee.  5 There are some milks in the fridge.
Useful phrases: Aren't you hungry? · Not really. · …, right? · Especially…
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 4.2 /ɪ/ (short) and /iː/ (long): chicken · fish · milk · dish  /  cheese · meat · beans · eat. Listen and repeat. Then: He eats fish. She eats cheese.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. Use the skeleton and a card. Then swap.
A | What do you have for breakfast?
B | I usually have __________ and __________.
A | What's your favourite dish?
B | My favourite dish is __________. What about you?
A | I love __________, but I don't eat __________.
B | Do you prefer __________ or __________?
A | I prefer __________ to __________.
Card A | Card B
breakfast: foul and bread · favourite: shish barak · doesn't eat: fish · prefers tea to coffee | breakfast: yoghurt and fruit · favourite: fattoush · doesn't eat: sweets · prefers coffee to tea

4.2  I'd like the fattoush, please
Function: ordering food   ·   Grammar: I'd like… / Could I have…? / Would you like…?   ·   Pronunciation: I'd /aɪd/; Would you…? /wʊdʒə/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What does a waiter say? What do customers say? What do people write when they order food online? Make lists.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Can I have the menu, please? | Order: 2 chicken sandwiches, 1 salad
 | 
 | 
b  Put the meal in order: the bill · a dessert · a starter (mezze) · the main course · a drink
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)

🔊 Track 4.3  After class at Beit Al-Yasmin
Waiter | Good evening. Are you ready to order?

Language boxGrammar – would like / couldCustomer | Waiter
I'd like (I would like) the grilled chicken, please. · Could I have the menu / the bill, please? · Can we have some hummus? | Would you like anything to drink? · Would you like a dessert? · Are you ready to order? · Anything else?
I'd like = I want (but more polite).  ⚠ I'd like a juice – not I like a juice (= I enjoy juice in general).
Would you like…? – Yes, please. / No, thanks. (not Yes, I would like.)
Practise: Complete with I'd like, Would you like, Could I have.  1 ______ anything to drink, sir?  2 ______ the bill, please?  3 ______ a table for four, please.  4 ______ some bread with your soup?  5 ______ a glass of water, please?
Useful phrases: Are you ready to order? · Yes, I think so. · … to share · Certainly. · Not now, thanks. Maybe later. · Excuse me – …
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 4.4 I'd like /aɪd laɪk/ – don't drop the /d/! · Would you like…? /wʊdʒə laɪk/ – fast and linked. Listen and repeat.

4.3  How much are the tomatoes?
Function: asking about quantities and prices   ·   Grammar: How much / How many; a kilo of, a bottle of…; a lot of   ·   Pronunciation: weak of /əv/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What do you buy every week? Where? What do people say in the market? What do they write on a shopping list?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
How much is the cheese? | 1 kg tomatoes
 | 
 | 
b  Match: a kilo of / a bottle of / a packet of / a bag of / a piece of / a can of  →  water · rice · cheese · tomatoes · tuna · biscuits
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What is Omar buying?   A fruit   B vegetables   C meat
b  Listen again. 1 How many lemons does Omar want?   2 How much does he pay in total?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 4.5  In the souq
Abu Hassan | Good morning! What would you like?
Omar | Good morning. How much are the tomatoes?
Abu Hassan | Eight thousand a kilo. They're very fresh – from Daraa.
Omar | OK, I'd like two kilos, please. And a kilo of cucumbers.
Abu Hassan | Here you are. Anything else?
Omar | Yes. Have you got any parsley?
Abu Hassan | Of course. How much do you need?
Omar | Three bunches, please. I'm making fattoush for a lot of people.
Abu Hassan | And lemons? How many would you like?
Omar | Six, please. How much is that altogether?
Abu Hassan | Tomatoes sixteen, cucumbers seven, parsley six, lemons five… that's thirty-four thousand.
Omar | Here you are. Thanks a lot!

Language boxGrammar – How much? / How many? / containersHow many + countable plural | How much + uncountable | How much + price
How many lemons would you like? – Six. | How much parsley do you need? – Three bunches. | How much is the cheese? – 40,000 a kilo. · How much are the tomatoes? · How much is that altogether?
Containers and quantities: a kilo of tomatoes · half a kilo of cheese · a bottle of water · a packet of biscuits · a bag of rice · a can of tuna · a bunch of parsley · a piece of cake · a loaf of bread
a lot of (+ / – / ?) with both: a lot of people · a lot of rice. In questions and negatives we often use much / many: I don't buy many sweets. Do you drink much coffee?
Practise: Complete with much or many.  1 How ______ eggs do you need?  2 How ______ is a bottle of water?  3 How ______ sugar do you take in your tea?  4 We haven't got ______ bread.  5 How ______ people are coming?
Useful phrases: What would you like? · They're very fresh. · Anything else? · That's… altogether. · Here you are. · Thanks a lot!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 4.6 of is weak /əv/ and links: a kilo‿of tomatoes /ə ˈkiːləʊ əv/ · a bottle‿of water · a bunch‿of parsley. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is the seller (use the price list). B is the customer (use the shopping list). Then swap.
A | Good morning! What would you like?
B | How much is / are the __________?
A | __________ a kilo / a bottle / a packet.
B | I'd like __________, please. And __________.
A | Here you are. Anything else?
B | How much is that altogether?
A | That's __________.
Seller's price list | Shopping list A | Shopping list B
tomatoes 8,000/kg · potatoes 6,000/kg · cheese 40,000/kg · eggs 2,000 each · rice 15,000/bag · water 5,000/bottle · biscuits 4,000/packet | 2 kg potatoes · 6 eggs · half a kilo of cheese | 1 bag of rice · 3 bottles of water · 2 packets of biscuits

4.4  First, cut the bread
Function: giving instructions (a recipe)   ·   Grammar: imperatives + first, then, after that, finally   ·   Pronunciation: /tʃ/ and /ʃ/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What dishes can you cook? What do we do in a kitchen? Make a class list of cooking verbs.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
First, wash the vegetables. | Ingredients: 2 tomatoes, 1 onion…
 | 
 | 
b  Match the verb and the picture word: cut · mix · fry · boil · add · wash  →  a knife · a pan with oil · water + 100°C · a spoon · put in more · water from the tap
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What is Omar teaching Maya to make?   A kibbeh   B fattoush   C hummus
b  Listen again. 1 What do you do with the bread?   2 What do you add at the end?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 4.7  A video call from the kitchen
Maya | OK, Omar, I'm in the kitchen. What do I do first?
Omar | First, cut the bread into small squares and fry it in a little oil until it's brown. Or put it in the oven – it's healthier.
Maya | OK, the bread is in the oven. Then?
Omar | Then wash the vegetables and cut them: tomatoes, cucumbers, a little onion and some lettuce.
Maya | How small?
Omar | Not too small – about one centimetre. Put them in a big bowl. After that, chop the parsley and the mint and add them.
Maya | And the dressing?
Omar | Mix some olive oil, lemon juice, salt, sumac and a spoon of pomegranate molasses in a cup.
Maya | Do I add it now?
Omar | No! Don't add the dressing or the bread now. Wait. Finally, just before you eat, add the dressing and the bread and mix everything.
Maya | Why just before?
Omar | Because the bread needs to be crunchy!

Language boxGrammar – imperatives and sequencersImperative = base verb: Cut the bread. Wash the vegetables. Mix everything. Don't add the dressing now.Order: First, … → Then … → After that, … → Next, … → Finally, …until + result: Fry it until it's brown.  for + time: Boil it for ten minutes.Cooking verbs: cut · chop · wash · peel · mix · add · fry · boil · bake · put… in · wait · servePractise: Put the instructions for Arabic tea in order.  ___ Finally, add sugar if you like.  ___ First, boil some water.  ___ After that, wait for three minutes.  ___ Then put the tea in the pot and add the water.  ___ Next, pour the tea into small glasses.Useful phrases: What do I do first? · Then? · How small? · Not too… · about one centimetre · Just before you eat…Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 4.8 /tʃ/ (like "t" + "sh"): chop · chicken · cheese · lunch · kitchen  /ʃ/: fresh · dish · wash · sugar. Listen and repeat. Then: Chop the fresh chicken in the kitchen.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A has a recipe card. A explains the recipe step by step. B asks questions and repeats to check. Then swap with card B.
B | What do I do first?
A | First, __________.
B | __________. OK. Then?
A | Then __________. After that, __________.
B | How long / How much / How small?
A | __________.
B | And then?
A | Finally, __________. Don't __________!
Card A – Hummus | Card B – Shakshuka (eggs with tomatoes)
1 boil chickpeas for 1 hour (or use a can) · 2 put chickpeas, tahini, lemon juice, garlic and salt in a blender · 3 mix until smooth · 4 add a little cold water · 5 serve with olive oil on top · don't add too much garlic! | 1 cut 1 onion and fry it in oil for 5 minutes · 2 add 4 chopped tomatoes and salt · 3 cook for 10 minutes · 4 make 4 small holes and break an egg into each one · 5 cover and cook until the eggs are white · don't mix the eggs!
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the sequencers you hear in order.
☐ First | ☐ Then | ☐ After that | ☐ Next | ☐ Finally | ☐ Don't…
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Think of a dish you can make (it can be very simple – a sandwich, tea, an omelette, or a family dish). Teach it to your partner in five steps. Your partner writes the steps. Then check: are they right? Change partners and teach it again – without notes.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class your partner's recipe:  Hiba's grandmother's lentil soup: first, wash the lentils. Then fry an onion in oil. After that, add the lentils and water and boil them for thirty minutes…
Listener task: which recipe is the easiest? Which one do you want to try?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Omar writes his fattoush recipe for the class cookbook. Read and answer.
Omar's Fattoush (for 4 people · 20 minutes)Ingredients: 2 loaves of Arabic bread · 3 tomatoes · 2 cucumbers · 1 small onion · some lettuce · 1 bunch of parsley · a little mint · Dressing: 4 spoons of olive oil, the juice of 1 lemon, 1 spoon of pomegranate molasses, 1 teaspoon of sumac, saltMethod:  First, cut the bread into small squares and bake it in the oven until it's brown. Then wash the vegetables and cut them into small pieces. Put them in a big bowl. After that, chop the parsley and the mint and add them to the bowl. Next, mix the oil, lemon juice, pomegranate molasses, sumac and salt in a cup. Finally, just before you eat, add the dressing and the bread and mix well. Don't add the bread early – it needs to be crunchy!Omar's tip: add some fried halloumi cheese for a bigger meal.
1  How many people is the recipe for?
2  What do you mix in a cup?
3  What can you add for a bigger meal?
Writer's boxA recipe: title (+ people, time) → Ingredients (a list with quantities) → Method (steps with First, Then, After that, Next, Finally) → a warning (Don't…) → a tip.Each step starts with a verb. Use until and for for exact instructions.

4.5  Would you like to come for dinner?
Function: inviting and responding   ·   Grammar: Would you like to…? / I'd love to. / I'm afraid I can't.   ·   Pronunciation: polite intonation in invitations
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  When do you invite people to your home? What do you say? How do you say no politely? Make two lists.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Would you like to come for dinner? | You're invited! Dinner at our flat
 | 
 | 
b  Which are yes and which are no? I'd love to. · I'm afraid I can't. · That sounds great! · Sorry, I'm busy. · Thanks, I'd like that. · Maybe another time.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. When is the dinner?   A Thursday   B Friday   C Saturday
b  Listen again. 1 Why can't Maya come?   2 What does Lina offer to bring?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 4.9  An invitation
Omar | Lina, Maya – would you like to come for dinner at our flat on Friday? I'm cooking for the class.
Lina | Oh, I'd love to! Thank you. What time?
Omar | About seven o'clock. Rami is making the dessert – so don't expect too much!
Lina | Ha! Shall I bring something?
Omar | You don't need to bring anything.
Lina | I'll bring some fruit, then.
Omar | Great. Maya, can you come?
Maya | I'm really sorry, Omar, I'm afraid I can't. I'm going to Latakia this weekend – it's my father's birthday.
Omar | Oh, that's a shame. Never mind – maybe next time.
Maya | Yes, definitely. Thank you for inviting me. Have a great evening!

Language boxGrammar – invitationsInviting | Saying yes | Saying no politely
Would you like to come for dinner? · Do you want to come to…? · Can you come to…? | I'd love to. · That sounds great! · Thanks, I'd like that. · What time? | I'm (really) sorry, I'm afraid I can't. + reason · Thank you for inviting me. · Maybe next time.
Would you like to + base verb = an invitation: Would you like to come…?  ⚠ Not Would you like coming.
Compare: Would you like a coffee? (an offer of a thing) – Would you like to come? (an invitation to do something).
Always give a reason when you say no: I'm afraid I can't. I'm going to Latakia this weekend.
Practise: Complete.  1 Would you like ______ (go) to the cinema on Saturday?  2 – I'd love ______! What's on?  3 I'm ______ I can't. I'm working late.  4 Thank you for ______ (invite) me.  5 Oh, that's a ______. Maybe next time.
Useful phrases: Shall I bring something? · You don't need to bring anything. · That's a shame. · Never mind. · Definitely. · Have a great evening!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 4.10 Invitations go up at the end ↗ – they sound friendly: Would you like to come for dinner? ↗  A flat voice → sounds bored! Saying no: start high and warm: I'm really sorry… Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. Use the skeleton and the cards. A invites. B says yes to one invitation and no to the other.
A | Would you like to __________ on __________?
B | Oh, I'd love to! What time? / I'm really sorry, I'm afraid I can't. I'm __________.
A | About __________. / Oh, that's a shame. Never mind – maybe next time.
B | Shall I bring something? / Thank you for inviting me.
A | You don't need to bring anything. / Have a great __________!
Card A invites to… | Card B invites to…
lunch at my mother's house · Friday 2 pm · B: yes | a picnic in Tishreen Park · Saturday morning · A: no (working)
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: tick the phrases you hear.
☐ Would you like to…? | ☐ I'd love to. | ☐ I'm afraid I can't. | ☐ That's a shame. | ☐ Shall I bring something? | ☐ Thank you for inviting me.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Plan a real small event: a dinner, a lunch, a walk in the Old City, a football match, a coffee after class. Walk around and invite four classmates. Answer their invitations honestly – yes or no, with a real reason. Write down who is coming.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class:  I invited four people to a walk in the Old City on Saturday. Fadi and Hiba are coming, and Hiba is bringing her camera. Tareq can't come because he's working, and Rasha is visiting her aunt.
Listener task: whose event is the most popular?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Omar sends an invitation to the class WhatsApp group, and Maya replies. Read and answer.
Omar: Hi everyone! Would you like to come for dinner at our flat on Friday at 7 pm? I'm cooking fattoush, kibbeh and rice with chicken, and Rami is making the dessert (good luck to us all!). The address is Building 12, Al-Akram Street, Mezzeh, 3rd floor. Please let me know by Wednesday so I can buy enough food. Omar 🙂Maya: Thank you so much for the invitation, Omar! I'm really sorry, but I'm afraid I can't come. I'm going to Latakia on Thursday evening for my father's birthday. Can you save me some kibbeh? 😄 Have a wonderful evening, everyone!
1  What is Rami making?
2  Why does Omar need to know by Wednesday?
3  What does Maya ask Omar to do?
Writer's boxAn invitation: greeting → the invitation (Would you like to…?) → what, when, where → one fun detail → reply by… → name.A reply: thanks → yes / no → a reason (if no) or an offer (if yes) → a friendly ending.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Invitation:  Hi ______! Would you like to ______________ on ______ at ______? We're ______________. The address is ______________. Please let me know by ______. ______
Reply:  Thank you so much for the invitation, ______! I'd love to come. / I'm really sorry, but I'm afraid I can't come. I'm ______________. Shall I bring ______? / Have a wonderful ______! ______
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
Would you like to + base verb | 
What, when, where + reply date | 
Reply: thanks + yes / no + reason or offer | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read your invitation sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write an invitation to the real event you planned in Stage 4 (40 words or more). Then give it to a partner – they write a reply on the same page (yes or no – their choice!).







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read the reply to your invitation. ★ Thanks for your kind reply!  ✓ Can you tell me… ?  ? Can you come next time?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two invitations + replies are read aloud. Class: is the reply polite?
HomeworkWrite a second reply to your partner's invitation – the opposite answer this time (if you said yes, now say no politely with a reason; if you said no, now say yes and offer to bring something). 25+ words.

4.6  This is delicious!
Function: complimenting and thanking   ·   Grammar: very / really / so + adjective; What a…!   ·   Pronunciation: stress and feeling in compliments
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  You're a guest at dinner. What do you say to the host? What does the host say? What do you write after the dinner?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
This is delicious! | Thank you for a wonderful evening.
 | 
 | 
b  Food adjectives: put them in two groups, 😀 and 🙁 – delicious · tasty · salty · spicy · fresh · burnt · too sweet · amazing · cold · perfect
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What does Lina think of the dessert?   A It's burnt.   B It's too sweet.   C It's really good.
b  Listen again. 1 What does Lina want from Omar?   2 What did Rami make?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 4.11  Dinner at Omar and Rami's flat
Omar | Please, help yourselves. There's a lot of food!
Lina | Omar, this kibbeh is delicious! It's really crispy.
Omar | Thank you! It's my mother's recipe.
Lina | Can I have the recipe? My kibbeh is never so good.
Omar | Of course. I'll send it to you.
Sara | And the fattoush is so fresh. What a lovely dinner!
Omar | You're very kind. Have some more rice!
Lina | No, thank you – I'm full. Everything was perfect.
Rami | Wait – there's dessert! Muhallabiyeh. I made it myself.
Lina | (tastes it) Mmm! Rami, this is really good! You said you can't cook!
Rami | I can't cook – but I can follow a video!
Lina | Thank you both for a wonderful evening. Your flat is so nice.
Omar | Thank you for coming. Our door is always open!

Language boxGrammar – complimentsvery / really / so + adjective: This is very good. It's really crispy. The fattoush is so fresh!  (so = strong feeling)What a / an + adjective + noun! What a lovely dinner! What a beautiful flat! What an amazing view!Answering compliments: Thank you! · You're very kind. · I'm glad you like it. · It's my mother's recipe.Thanking: Thank you for + noun / -ing: Thank you for a wonderful evening. Thank you for coming / inviting me.Practise: Make compliments.  1 (the soup / hot and tasty) → This soup is really tasty!  2 (a nice garden)  3 (the cake / soft)  4 (an interesting book)  5 (your English / good)Useful phrases: Please, help yourselves. · Have some more… · No, thank you – I'm full. · Everything was perfect. · I made it myself. · Our door is always open!Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 4.12 Stress and feeling: This is deLIcious! · It's REALly good! · What a LOVEly dinner! Go high on the stressed word – show you mean it! Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is the host, B is the guest. Use the skeleton and a card. Then swap.
A | Please, help yourself.
B | This __________ is __________! It's really __________.
A | Thank you! It's __________.
B | What a __________!
A | You're very kind. Have some more __________!
B | No, thank you – I'm full. Thank you for __________.
A | Thank you for coming!
Card A – lunch at a friend's house | Card B – coffee at a colleague's flat
food: mansaf (rich, tasty) · grandmother's recipe · house: big, beautiful garden | food: homemade cake (soft, not too sweet) · from a video · flat: amazing view of Qasioun
 3R   Perform   (5 min)

 4   Your turn – the compliment circle   (9 min)
Stand in a circle. Each student shows a photo on their phone (food they cooked, their home, their city, a hobby) or talks about it. Everyone gives one compliment with really / so / What a…! The owner answers. Then pairs: tell your partner about a real meal you enjoyed at someone's home.
 4R   Report   (6 min)

 5   Read   (7 min)

2  What does she ask Omar to do?

4.7  Review & Mediate: Does this have nuts in it?
Function: explaining a menu to a visitor   ·   Recycling: Units 1–4   ·   Exam practice: A2 Key Reading Part 1, Writing Part 6, Listening Part 1
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready – Unit 4 quiz   (6 min)
In teams. Correct the mistakes! 1 I have a bread for breakfast.  2 How many sugar do you want?  3 I like a coffee, please. (in a café)  4 Would you like come to dinner?  5 First, to cut the bread.  6 Thank you for invite me.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Read the menu at Beit Al-Yasmin. Look at the symbols.
BEIT AL-YASMIN – TODAY'S SPECIALS(V) = vegetarian (no meat or fish)   (N) = contains nuts   (D) = contains dairy (milk, cheese, yoghurt)   🌶 = spicyDish | Description | Price
Muhammara (V)(N)🌶 | red pepper and walnut dip with bread | 20,000
Fattoush (V) | fresh salad with crispy bread | 18,000
Shish barak (D) | small meat dumplings in hot yoghurt | 50,000
Mujaddara (V) | rice and lentils with fried onions | 30,000
Grilled fish | today's fish from Tartus, with rice | 70,000
Muhallabiyeh (D)(N) | milk pudding with pistachios | 15,000
All our meat is halal. Ask your waiter about allergies.
b  Books closed. Listen. Lina explains the menu to Mr Lang. What two things can't he eat?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 4.13  Lunch with a visitor
Mr Lang | This menu looks wonderful, Lina, but I need your help. I'm allergic to nuts, and I don't eat meat.
Lina | No problem. Let me look… OK, the muhammara is vegetarian, but it has walnuts in it – so not for you.
Mr Lang | What's that? Walnuts?
Lina | They're a kind of nut. The fattoush is fine – it's a fresh salad with crispy bread. No meat and no nuts.
Mr Lang | Great. And a main course?
Lina | The shish barak has meat in it. But the mujaddara is vegetarian – it's rice and lentils with fried onions. It's my favourite!
Mr Lang | Do you eat fish? The fish looks good too.
Lina | I do, but you said no meat – do you eat fish?
Mr Lang | Yes, I eat fish. But today I'll try your favourite. And dessert?
Lina | The muhallabiyeh is a milk pudding, but it has pistachios on top – they're nuts too. Let's ask the waiter for a fruit plate.
Mr Lang | Perfect. Thank you, Lina – you're a great guide!

Language box – explaining a menuExplain the symbols and the dish in simple words:(N) → It has nuts in it. / It contains nuts.   (V) → It's vegetarian – there's no meat in it.   (D) → It has milk / cheese / yoghurt in it.Explain unknown words: It's a kind of… · It's like… · It's made with…Give advice: It's fine for you. · It's not for you. · Let's ask the waiter.Useful phrases: I'm allergic to… · I don't eat… · Let me look… · …, so not for you. · It's my favourite! · You're a great guide!
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is a visitor with a card. B explains the menu above and recommends a starter, a main course and a dessert. Then swap.
Visitor card A | Visitor card B
vegetarian · can't eat dairy · loves spicy food | eats everything · allergic to fish · doesn't like spicy food
 3R   Perform   (5 min)

 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Think of three real Syrian dishes a visitor might not know. Your partner is a foreign visitor (they choose: vegetarian / allergic to something / doesn't like spicy food…). Explain each dish: what it is, what's in it, and if it's good for them.
 4R   Report – mediation   (6 min)

Listener task: which dish is the most difficult to explain?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.

A  Grammar and vocabulary. Choose the correct answer.
1  I have ___ for breakfast.   a) a bread   b) breads   c) bread
2  How ___ eggs do you need?   a) much   b) many   c) lot
3  ___ the grilled chicken, please.   a) I like   b) I'd like   c) I'm like

6  Would you like ___ for dinner on Friday?   a) come   b) coming   c) to come

8  What ___ lovely dinner!   a) –   b) a   c) the

🔊 Track 4.14  Audio script (read after the check)
1  Waiter | Would you like tea or coffee?
   Woman | Hmm, no, thanks. Could I have an orange juice, please? Not water – I have some.
2  Man | How much is that altogether?
   Seller | The cheese is twelve and the olives are eight – that's twenty thousand.
   Man | Twenty. Here you are.
3  Woman | Is dinner at seven?
   Man | No, half past seven. My brother finishes work at seven.
4  Woman | We've got rice and eggs. Can you get some bread on your way home?
