Unit 5  Shopping & Money
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
In this unit you will…5.1  ask for things in a shop5.2  compare two products5.3  say which is the best5.4  pay and use services (bank, post office, pharmacy)5.5  return something that doesn't work5.6  say what people are wearing5.7  Review & Mediate: explain an online order page

New in this unitAl-Hamra Centre – a shopping centre in Damascus with clothes shops, a phone shop and a post office.Samira – a shop assistant at Moda Clothes.   ·   Ziad Haddad – Lina's brother (32, dentist). His daughter Jana is three.

5.1  Have you got this in a larger size?
Function: asking for things in a shop   ·   Grammar: this / that / these / those; possessive pronouns (mine, yours…)   ·   Pronunciation: /ɜː/ shirt, skirt, first
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What clothes do you wear to work, to a wedding, at home? What do you say in a clothes shop? Your teacher writes your ideas on the board.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Have you got this in blue? | Size: medium · Colour: black
 | 
 | 
b  Clothes or not? shirt · shoes · trousers · scarf · jacket · dress · gloves · towel · socks   Which are always plural in English?
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What does Rami want to buy?   A trousers   B a jacket   C a shirt
b  Listen again. 1 What size does he take?   2 What colour does he choose?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 5.1  At Moda Clothes, Al-Hamra Centre
Samira | Good afternoon. Can I help you?
Rami | Yes, please. I'm looking for a shirt for a wedding.
Samira | Of course. We've got these white ones here, and those blue shirts are new.
Rami | I like this one. Have you got it in a larger size? I take a large.
Samira | Let me check… Yes, here you are. Would you like to try it on?
Rami | Yes, please. Where's the changing room?
Samira | It's over there, next to those jackets.
Rami | (later) It fits well, but I'm not sure about the colour. Have you got it in light blue?
Samira | Yes, we have. Here.
Rami | That's better. I'll take this one. Oh – is this scarf yours? It was in the changing room.
Samira | No, it isn't mine. Maybe it's the customer's before you. Thank you!

Language boxGrammar – this / that / these / those | near me | not near me
singular | this shirt (this one) | that shirt (that one)
plural | these shirts (these ones) | those shirts (those ones)
Possessive pronouns: my → mine · your → yours · his → his · her → hers · our → ours · their → theirs
Is this scarf yours? – No, it isn't mine.   ⚠ No noun after a possessive pronoun: It's mine, not It's mine scarf. And no apostrophe: yours, not your's.
Always plural: trousers, jeans, shoes, socks, gloves, glasses → They're nice. · a pair of trousers
Practise: Complete.  1 I like ______ shoes here on my feet. (this / these)  2 How much are ______ jackets over there? (that / those)  3 Is this bag ______? – No, it isn't ______. (you / your / yours · my / mine)  4 Those trousers ______ very nice. (is / are)
Useful phrases: I'm looking for… · Have you got it in…? · I take a large / medium / small. · Would you like to try it on? · It fits well. · I'm not sure about… · I'll take this one.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 5.2 /ɜː/ – a long sound, mouth relaxed: shirt · skirt · first · third · work · Thursday · her. Listen and repeat. Don't say /ʃiːrt/ for shirt!
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is the shop assistant, B is the customer. Use the skeleton and a card. Then swap.
A | Good afternoon. Can I help you?
B | Yes, please. I'm looking for __________ for __________.
A | Of course. We've got these __________ here, and those __________ are new.
B | I like this one. Have you got it in __________?
A | Let me check… Yes, here you are. Would you like to try it on?
B | Yes, please. (later) It __________, but I'm not sure about __________.
A | We've got it in __________ too.
B | That's better. I'll take __________.
Card A – customer | Card B – customer
a jacket for work · size medium · doesn't like the grey one · wants dark blue | shoes for a wedding · size 42 · too small · wants black, not brown
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform without the book. Listener task: write the item, the size and the colour the customer buys.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Put three or four things on your desk (a pen, a phone, a book, a bag, a scarf…). Mix them with your partner's things. Now ask: Is this pen yours? Are these keys yours? Answer: Yes, it's mine. / No, it isn't mine – I think it's Hiba's. Then: tell your partner about the last clothes you bought. Where? What size? How much? Change partners and tell the story again.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner's shopping:  Hiba bought a long grey coat at Al-Hamra Centre. She takes a medium. It was 400,000 pounds – she says it was expensive, but it's very warm. (Learn bought / was as phrases here.)
Listener task: who found the best thing? Who paid the most?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the online ad for a jacket. Answer the questions.
MEN'S WINTER JACKET – MODA CLOTHESA warm jacket for cold days. It's made of thick cotton, with a soft wool collar. There are four pockets – two inside and two outside. It's perfect for work or for the weekend.Colours: dark blue, black, dark green   Sizes: S, M, L, XL (the sizes are small – please order one size larger)Price: 480,000 SYP.  Free delivery in Damascus for orders over 300,000 SYP.Message us on WhatsApp with your size and colour: 0955 112 340.
1  What is the collar made of?
2  What is the problem with the sizes?
3  When is delivery free?
Writer's boxA product ad: title (what + where) → description (what it's made of, details) → who it's for → colours and sizes → price and delivery → how to order.Use adjectives in the right order: a warm winter jacket · a soft wool collar (Unit 3).Give the customer a warning or tip: the sizes are small…
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
 –    A ______________ for ______________. It's made of ______________, with ______________. There ______________. It's perfect for ______________.   Colours: ______________   Sizes: ______________ (______________)   Price: ______________.  Delivery: ______________.   Message us on ______________.
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
Adjective order; made of | 
this / these used correctly if you use them | 
All parts: description, colours, sizes, price, how to order | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Two or three students read one line before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write an ad for something you want to sell (clothes, a phone, a bike, furniture) – real or invented (50 words or more). Be honest about one problem!







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's ad as a customer. ★ I like…  ✓ Can you add the size?  ? Is it new or used?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Put the ads on the wall: this is our class shop! Walk around and write your name on the thing you want to buy.
HomeworkWrite a WhatsApp message to the seller of the thing you chose (25+ words): ask two questions and say when you can collect it.

5.2  This one's cheaper
Function: comparing two products   ·   Grammar: comparative adjectives   ·   Pronunciation: weak than /ðən/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  You want to buy a phone. What is important for you? Make a class list: price, battery, camera…
We SAY… | We WRITE…
This one's cheaper than that one. | Phone A: 2,500,000 · Phone B: 3,900,000
 | 
 | 
b  Opposites: cheap – ______ · big – ______ · old – ______ · fast – ______ · heavy – ______ · good – ______
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Who is buying a phone?   A Omar   B Omar's cousin   C Lina
b  Listen again. 1 Which phone has a better camera?   2 Which phone does Nabil choose?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 5.3  In the phone shop
Nabil | Omar, you know about technology. Which phone is better?
Omar | Let me see. This one is newer and the camera is better, but it's more expensive.
Nabil | How much more?
Omar | It's one million more than that one.
Nabil | That's a lot! Is the battery better too?
Omar | Actually, no. The battery in the cheaper one is bigger. And it's lighter.
Nabil | Hmm. I use my phone for work all day, so the battery is more important for me than the camera.
Omar | Then the cheaper one is better for you. It's also easier to repair – I can fix it myself!
Nabil | And the screen?
Omar | The screen is a little smaller, but it's not bad.
Nabil | OK, I'll take the cheaper one. Thanks, Omar – you saved me a million pounds!

Language boxGrammar – comparative adjectives (+ than)Adjective type | Rule | Examples
short (1 syllable) | + -er | cheap → cheaper · small → smaller · light → lighter
ending in -e | + -r | nice → nicer · large → larger
short vowel + consonant | double + -er | big → bigger · hot → hotter · thin → thinner
ending in -y | -y → -ier | easy → easier · heavy → heavier · happy → happier
long (2+ syllables) | more + adjective | more expensive · more comfortable · more important
irregular | — | good → better · bad → worse · far → further
Use than to compare two things: This one is cheaper than that one.  ⚠ Not more cheaper, not cheaper that.
A little / much + comparative: a little smaller · much more expensive
Practise: Write the comparative.  1 big ______  2 expensive ______  3 easy ______  4 good ______  5 light ______  6 comfortable ______   Then: 7 This bag is ______ (cheap) than that one.  8 My new phone is ______ (good) than my old one.
Useful phrases: Which one is better? · Let me see. · How much more? · That's a lot! · Actually,… · It's not bad. · You saved me…!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 5.4 than is weak /ðən/ and links to the next word: cheaper‿than‿that one · bigger‿than mine. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A wants to buy; B is the friend who knows. Compare the two products on the card. Then swap.
A | Which __________ is better?
B | Let me see. This one is __________ and __________, but it's __________.
A | How much more?
B | It's __________ more than that one.
A | Is the __________ better too?
B | Actually, no. The __________ in the cheaper one is __________.
A | OK, I'll take __________.
Card A – two fridges | Card B – two cars
Fridge 1: 4,500,000 · big · new model · noisy   Fridge 2: 3,200,000 · smaller · old model · quiet, uses less electricity | Car 1: 10 years old · cheap · comfortable · uses a lot of petrol   Car 2: 4 years old · expensive · small · cheap to run
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write two comparative sentences you hear. Which product would you buy?
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Compare two real things with your partner: your city and another city, two shops you know, your phone and your partner's phone, winter and summer, two cafés. Use at least five comparatives. Then change partners and compare two different things.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class what your partner thinks:  Tareq says Suwayda is quieter and cleaner than Damascus, but Damascus is more interesting and the shops are better. He says the air is colder in Suwayda in winter.
Listener task: do you agree with your classmate? Put up your hand!   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)

Which phone for a student? Two good optionsLast week my cousin asked me to help him choose a phone, so I compared two popular models.The Nova 7 is newer and it looks nicer. The camera is much better – the photos are clearer, especially at night. But it's more expensive: it costs one million pounds more than the other phone.The Star X is cheaper and lighter. The battery is bigger, so it works all day. It's also easier to repair, and the parts are cheaper in Syria. The screen is a little smaller, but it isn't bad.For a student, I think the Star X is the better choice. The camera is less important than the battery and the price. But if you love photography, buy the Nova 7!
1  Which phone has a better camera?
2  Why is the Star X easier to repair?
3  Who should buy the Nova 7?
Writer's boxA comparison text: introduction (why you compared them) → product 1 (good points + one bad point) → product 2 → your opinion (I think… is better for…) → one exception (But if you…).Use but for a contrast and so for a result: The battery is bigger, so it works all day.Write a new paragraph for each product.

But if you ______________, buy the ______________!

Comparatives: -er / more + adjective (not both!) | 
than after the comparative | 
All parts: introduction, two products, opinion, exception |

Read one comparison sentence before and after the fix.

5.3  It's the cheapest shop in town
Function: saying which is the best   ·   Grammar: superlative adjectives   ·   Pronunciation: the before a vowel /ðiː/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  Where do you buy clothes, food, phones, presents? Which shops are good? Which are expensive? Make a class list.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
It's the cheapest shop in town. | The best shop for shoes is…
 | 
 | 
b  Make the superlative: cheap → the ______ · big → the ______ · easy → the ______ · expensive → the ______ · good → the ______ · bad → the ______
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What does Lina need to buy?   A a present for a child   B clothes for work   C a phone
b  Listen again. 1 Which shop is the most expensive?   2 Where does Lina decide to go?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 5.5  Where to buy a present
Lina | Maya, it's Jana's birthday on Saturday. She's three. Where's the best place to buy a toy?
Maya | Hmm. Al-Hamra Centre has the biggest choice, but it's also the most expensive.
Lina | What about the shops in Souq Al-Hamidiyah?
Maya | They're the cheapest, but the quality isn't always the best. For a small child, I'd buy something safe.
Lina | True. What's the most popular present for a three-year-old?
Maya | Books and wooden toys. There's a lovely little shop near the university – it's not the cheapest, but it has the nicest wooden toys in Damascus.
Lina | That sounds perfect. Is it far?
Maya | No, it's the easiest shop to find – it's opposite the main gate. Ask for Abu Yarub.
Lina | Thanks, Maya! You always know the best places.

Language boxGrammar – superlative adjectives (the …est / the most …)Adjective type | Rule | Examples
short | the + -est | cheap → the cheapest · small → the smallest
ending in -e | the + -st | nice → the nicest · large → the largest
short vowel + consonant | double + -est | big → the biggest · hot → the hottest
ending in -y | -y → -iest | easy → the easiest · happy → the happiest
long | the most + adjective | the most expensive · the most popular
irregular | — | good → the best · bad → the worst
Always with the: It's the cheapest shop.   After a superlative use in (a place) or of (a group): the best shop in town · the youngest of my brothers.
Practise: Complete with the superlative.  1 Souq Al-Hamidiyah is ______ (cheap) place in Damascus.  2 This is ______ (good) café in my area.  3 It's ______ (expensive) phone in the shop.  4 February is ______ (short) month.  5 That was ______ (bad) film of the year!
Useful phrases: Where's the best place to…? · It has the biggest choice. · The quality isn't always the best. · True. · That sounds perfect. · You always know the best places!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 5.6 the before a consonant = /ðə/: the cheapest. Before a vowel sound = /ðiː/: the easiest · the oldest · the most expensive. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A asks for advice; B answers with superlatives from the card. Then swap.
A | Where's the best place to buy __________?
B | __________ has the biggest choice, but it's the most expensive.
A | What about __________?
B | It's the cheapest, but __________.
A | What's the most popular __________?
B | __________. There's a __________ shop in __________ – it's got the best __________ in the city.
A | Thanks! That sounds perfect.
Card A – buying a bicycle | Card B – buying sweets for a wedding
Big shop in Mezzeh: biggest choice, most expensive · street market: cheapest, bad quality · small shop in Qassaa: best repairs, good prices | Famous shop in the centre: the most famous, most expensive · supermarket: cheapest, not fresh · family shop near the mosque: freshest, best baklava

5.4  Can I pay by card?
Function: paying and using services   ·   Grammar: can / could for requests and possibility; by card / in cash   ·   Pronunciation: numbers and prices
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  Which services do you use? (bank, post office, pharmacy, phone shop, electricity office…) What do you ask there?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Can I pay by card? | Please keep your receipt.
 | 
 | 
b  Where do you go to…? send a parcel → ______  change money → ______  buy medicine → ______  pay your internet bill → ______
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What does Lina want to do at the post office?   A collect a parcel   B send a parcel   C buy stamps
b  Listen again. 1 How long does the parcel take?   2 How does Lina pay?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 5.7  At the post office, Al-Hamra Centre
Clerk | Next, please. How can I help you?
Lina | Hello. I'd like to send this parcel to Dubai, please.
Clerk | OK. Can you put it on the scale? … It's two kilos. What's in it?
Lina | Clothes and some sweets – a present for my friend.
Clerk | Fine. Could you fill in this form, please? Your name and address here, and your friend's address here.
Lina | Of course. How long does it take?
Clerk | Normal post takes about two weeks. Express takes four or five days, but it's more expensive.
Lina | How much is express?
Clerk | One hundred and eighty thousand.
Lina | That's fine. It's her birthday soon. Can I pay by card?
Clerk | I'm sorry, our machine isn't working today. Can you pay in cash?
Lina | Yes, no problem. Here you are.
Clerk | Thank you. Here's your receipt – please keep it. You can follow your parcel online with this number.

Language boxGrammar – can / could for servicesAsking (customer) | Asking politely (staff) | Answering
Can I pay by card? · Could I have a receipt? · Can you send it today? | Can you put it on the scale? · Could you fill in this form, please? | Yes, of course. · I'm sorry, you can't… · Our machine isn't working.
Paying: pay by card · pay in cash · pay online · in instalments · Here you are. · Keep the change. · Here's your receipt.
Services: send a parcel · fill in a form · change money · pay a bill · open an account · collect something · follow / track it online
Practise: Complete with Can I or Can you.  1 ______ pay by card? (customer)  2 ______ sign here, please? (staff)  3 ______ have a bag, please? (customer)  4 ______ wait two minutes, please? (staff)  5 ______ change this money? (customer)
Useful phrases: Next, please. · How can I help you? · What's in it? · How long does it take? · That's fine. · No problem. · Please keep it.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 5.8 Big numbers: one hundred and eighty thousand · two hundred and fifty · a million. Stress: EIGHteen (18) / EIGHty (80), THIRteen (13) / THIRty (30). Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is the customer with the card, B is the clerk. Then swap.
B | Next, please. How can I help you?
A | Hello. I'd like to __________, please.
B | OK. Could you __________, please?
A | Of course. How long does it take? / How much is it?
B | __________.
A | Can I pay __________?
B | Yes, of course. / I'm sorry, you can't. Can you __________?
A | No problem. Here you are.
B | Here's your receipt. Please keep it.
Card A – at the bank | Card B – at the pharmacy
change 200 dollars · fill in a form · takes 10 minutes · card not accepted | buy medicine for a cough · show the prescription · 45,000 SYP · card accepted
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: tick the phrases you hear.
☐ How can I help you? | ☐ I'd like to… | ☐ Could you…, please? | ☐ How long does it take? | ☐ Can I pay by card? | ☐ Here's your receipt.
 4   Your turn – the service circuit   (9 min)
Make four "desks" in the classroom: bank · post office · pharmacy · phone shop. Half the class are staff, half are customers. Customers: visit three desks and do a real task (change money, send a parcel, buy medicine, pay your phone bill). Then swap roles. At each desk, ask about the price and how to pay!
 4R   Report   (6 min)

Listener task: which service was the slowest? Where was there a problem?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.

5.5  It doesn't work
Function: returning something   ·   Grammar: negatives; too + adjective / not + adjective + enough   ·   Pronunciation: doesn't /ˈdʌznt/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What problems do people have with things they buy? What do you say in the shop? What do you write in a complaint?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
It doesn't work. | I would like a refund, please.
 | 
 | 
b  Match the problem: It's broken. · It doesn't work. · It's too small. · There's a hole in it. · It's the wrong colour.  →  a kettle · a shirt · shoes · a screen · an online order
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What is the problem with the kettle?   A It's broken.   B It's too small.   C It's the wrong colour.
b  Listen again. 1 When did Maya buy it?   2 What does she get in the end?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 5.9  At the customer service desk
Maya | Excuse me, I bought this kettle here last week, but it doesn't work.
Assistant | I'm sorry to hear that. What exactly is the problem?
Maya | The water doesn't get hot enough. It's warm, but it never boils.
Assistant | I see. Have you got the receipt?
Maya | Yes, here it is.
Assistant | Thank you. Did you use it with a stabiliser?
Maya | Yes, I did. The problem isn't the electricity – my other things are fine.
Assistant | OK. I can change it for a new one, or you can have your money back.
Maya | I'd prefer a new one, please – but a different model. This one is too small for my family anyway.
Assistant | Of course. This one is bigger and it's only twenty thousand more.
Maya | That's fine. Thank you for your help.
Assistant | You're welcome. I'm very sorry about the problem.

Language boxGrammar – describing problemsNegatives: It doesn't work. · It isn't the right size. · The light doesn't come on. · They don't fit.too + adjective (= a problem: more than good) | not + adjective + enough (= a problem: less than good)
It's too small for my family. · These shoes are too tight. · It's too expensive. | The water isn't hot enough. · The bag isn't big enough. · It isn't strong enough.
⚠ enough comes after the adjective: hot enough, not enough hot. too comes before: too hot.
Problems: It's broken. · There's a hole / a mark / a crack in it. · It's the wrong size / colour. · A piece is missing. · It doesn't fit.
Solutions: change it for a new one · have your money back (a refund) · repair it · give you a discount
Practise: Complete with too or enough.  1 I can't wear these shoes – they're ______ small.  2 The soup isn't hot ______.  3 This bag isn't big ______ for my laptop.  4 The music is ______ loud!  5 He isn't old ______ to drive.
Useful phrases: I bought this here last week. · I'm sorry to hear that. · What exactly is the problem? · Have you got the receipt? · I'd prefer… · I'm very sorry about the problem.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 5.10 doesn't /ˈdʌznt/ – two syllables, clear /z/: It doesn't work. · It doesn't fit. · She doesn't like it. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is the customer with the problem card, B is the assistant. B offers a solution. Then swap.
A | Excuse me, I bought this __________ here __________, but __________.
B | I'm sorry to hear that. What exactly is the problem?
A | It's too __________. / It isn't __________ enough.
B | I see. Have you got the receipt?
A | Yes, here it is.
B | I can __________, or you can __________.
A | I'd prefer __________, please.
Problem card A | Problem card B
a jacket · bought 3 days ago · there's a hole in the pocket · wants a refund | headphones · bought yesterday · one side doesn't work · wants a new pair
 3R   Perform   (5 min)

5.6  She's wearing a blue jacket
Function: saying what people are wearing   ·   Grammar: present continuous (recycle) with clothes   ·   Pronunciation: /dʒ/ jacket, jeans
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What do people wear to work? To a wedding? At home? Is it different in summer and winter? Make a class list.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
He's wearing a dark suit. | Please wear comfortable shoes.
 | 
 | 
b  Wear or carry? a bag · a jacket · an umbrella · glasses · a scarf · a box
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Why is Lina at the airport?   A She's travelling.   B She's meeting a visitor.   C She's collecting a parcel.
b  Listen again. 1 What is Mr Lang wearing?   2 What is he carrying?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 5.11  At the airport
Lina | Hello, Mr Lang? This is Lina Haddad from Medica Trading. I'm at the airport, but I can't see you!
Mr Lang | Hello, Lina! I'm just outside Gate 2. There are a lot of people here.
Lina | What are you wearing?
Mr Lang | I'm wearing a grey jacket and jeans, and I've got glasses. I'm carrying a black suitcase and a laptop bag.
Lina | Hmm, I see two men with grey jackets…
Mr Lang | I'm quite tall, and I'm wearing a red scarf. My wife says it's too bright!
Lina | Ah – I can see you! You're standing next to the coffee shop, right? I'm waving.
Mr Lang | Yes! Are you the lady in the long black coat?
Lina | That's me. Welcome to Damascus, Mr Lang!

Language boxGrammar – wear / be wearingI wear a suit to work every day. (routine – present simple)   Today I 'm wearing jeans. (now – present continuous)wear clothes, shoes, glasses, a watch, perfume · carry a bag, a suitcase, an umbrella (in your hand) · have got glasses / a beardDescribing a person: size (tall, short) + hair + clothes + carrying: He's quite tall, with short dark hair. He's wearing a grey jacket and he's carrying a black suitcase.quite = a little: quite tall. very = a lot: very tall.Practise: Choose the correct form.  1 Rami usually (wears / is wearing) a shirt to work.  2 Look at Maya! She (wears / is wearing) a beautiful dress today.  3 I (carry / 'm carrying) two bags – can you help me?  4 My grandfather always (wears / is wearing) a hat.Useful phrases: What are you wearing? · I can't see you! · I'm quite tall. · I'm waving. · Are you the lady / man in…? · That's me!Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 5.12 /dʒ/ – like "d" + "j": jacket · jeans · January · orange · jumper. Don't say /ʒ/ or /ʃ/! Listen and repeat: He's wearing a jacket and jeans in January.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is at the airport / station; B is meeting them. Describe yourself so B can find you. Use the skeleton and a card. Then swap.
A | Hello? I'm at __________, but I can't see you!
B | I'm just __________. What are you wearing?
A | I'm wearing __________ and __________. I'm carrying __________.
B | Hmm, I see two people with __________…
A | I'm quite __________, and I've got __________.
B | Ah – I can see you! You're standing next to __________, right?
A | That's me!
Card A | Card B
short · long dark hair · black coat, white scarf · carrying a big bag and a plant | tall · glasses, short grey beard · brown jacket, dark trousers · carrying a suitcase and an umbrella
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: draw a quick picture of the person. Compare with the card!
 4   Your turn – who is it?   (9 min)
a  Secretly choose a person in the class. Describe what they are wearing – don't use their name or hair. Your partner guesses. Then swap.
b  Describe what you usually wear to work or college, and what you are wearing today. Is it different? Why?
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner:  Hiba usually wears a long skirt and a jacket to college, but today she's wearing jeans because she has no classes – only our English lesson!
Listener task: who is the most comfortable in class today? Who is the most elegant?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read Lina's message to her colleague Rana at the office. Answer the questions.
Rana – I'm still at the airport. Mr Lang's plane was late, but he's here now and we're on our way.He's quite tall, about fifty, with glasses and short grey hair. He's wearing a grey jacket, jeans and a bright red scarf – you can't miss him! He's carrying a black suitcase and a laptop bag.Please tell Mr Khaled that we'll be at the office at about 11.30. Can you make sure the meeting room is ready? Mr Lang doesn't drink coffee – he prefers tea with no sugar.Thanks! Lina
1  How old is Mr Lang, about?
2  What will make him easy to recognise?
3  What does he like to drink?
Writer's boxA message describing a person: the situation → description (age, size, hair, glasses) → clothes now (He's wearing…) → what they're carrying → practical information (time, a request, a detail).Use quite / very / about: quite tall · about fifty.Order: wearing first, then carrying.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)

 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
is wearing / is carrying (not wearing alone) | 
Adjective order in the clothes description | 
All parts: situation, description, clothes, carrying, practical information | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one description sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
A friend is meeting someone you know at the station or airport, but they have never met. Write a message describing that person (50 words or more) and add two pieces of practical information.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's message as the person meeting them. ★ I can find them!  ✓ Can you add what they're carrying?  ? How tall exactly?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two messages are read aloud. Draw the person as you listen. Compare your drawings!
HomeworkWrite the reply from the person at the station (25+ words): I found him! He was… We're on our way… or I can't see him. Is he wearing…?

5.7  Review & Mediate: How do I order online?
Function: explaining an online order page   ·   Recycling: Units 1–5   ·   Exam practice: A2 Key Reading Part 5, Writing Part 6, Listening Part 4
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready – Unit 5 quiz   (6 min)
In teams. Correct the mistakes! 1 This shoes are too small.  2 My phone is more cheaper than yours.  3 It's the most big shop in town.  4 Is this bag your's?  5 The water isn't enough hot.  6 Today she wears jeans.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Read the online order page.
BEIT STORE – HOW TO ORDERStep | What to do
1  Choose | Click on the product. Choose your size and colour. Click "Add to basket".
2  Basket | Click the basket icon (top right). Check your items. You can change the number or remove an item here.
3  Delivery | Write your name, phone number and address. Choose: standard (3–5 days, 25,000 SYP) or fast (24 hours, 60,000 SYP). Free standard delivery for orders over 500,000 SYP.
4  Payment | Choose: card online or cash on delivery (+10,000 SYP).
5  Confirm | Click "Confirm order". You get a message with your order number. Keep this number.
Returns: you can return an item in 14 days if it isn't used. Call 011 556 7788 first.
b  Books closed. Listen. Lina helps her mother order online. What does her mother worry about?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 5.13  Ordering with Mum
Mum | Lina, I like this jacket on the website, but I don't understand how to buy it.
Lina | It's easy, Mum. First, click on the picture. Now choose your size – you take a medium – and the colour.
Mum | Dark blue. And then?
Lina | Then click "Add to basket". Now click the little basket at the top. Can you see it? That's everything you want to buy.
Mum | Yes. It says one jacket, 480,000.
Lina | Good. Now write your name, phone number and address. Then choose the delivery: standard takes three to five days and costs twenty-five thousand, or fast comes tomorrow for sixty.
Mum | Standard is fine. I'm not in a hurry. But Lina – I don't want to put my card number on the internet.
Lina | You don't have to. Choose "cash on delivery" – you pay the driver. It's ten thousand more.
Mum | Oh, that's better! And if it doesn't fit?
Lina | You can return it in fourteen days, but call them first. And Mum – keep the order number they send you.
Mum | Thank you, habibti. Now I can do it myself next time!

Language box – explaining a website or a processExplain each step with an imperative (Unit 4): Click on the picture. Choose your size. Write your address.Explain the options and what they mean: Standard takes three to five days and costs 25,000.Answer worries with You don't have to… / You can…: You don't have to use a card. You can pay the driver.Useful phrases: It's easy. · First,… Then… Now… · Can you see it? · That's everything you want to buy. · I'm not in a hurry. · …, but call them first. · Keep the order number.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A has the page, B is a person who doesn't use websites much. B asks the questions on the card; A explains. Then swap.
B's questions – set 1 | B's questions – set 2
How do I choose the size? · Where do I see what I'm buying? · How much is delivery? · Is standard delivery ever free? | Can I pay when the driver comes? · How much more is that? · What if the size is wrong? · What must I keep?
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the answers to the four questions.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Think of a real app or website you use (a delivery app, a bank app, a bus booking site, a shopping page). Explain to your partner how to use it, step by step. Your partner is a beginner – they ask at least three questions, including one worry (Is it safe? What if…?).
 4R   Report – mediation   (6 min)
Tell the class what your partner explained:  Tareq explained the Yalla delivery app. First you choose the restaurant, then you add food to the basket. You can pay in cash. The delivery costs about fifteen thousand, and you can follow the driver on the map.
Listener task: which app sounds the easiest? Which do you want to try?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING AND UNIT CHECK
 5   Read – exam practice (A2 Key Reading Part 5)   (7 min)
Read the text. Write ONE word for each gap.

🔊 Track 5.14  Audio script (read after the check)
Samira | Hello! I'm Samira and I work at Moda Clothes in Al-Hamra Centre. My family opened the shop twenty years ago – it isn't the oldest shop in the centre, but we know all our customers! In winter, everyone asks for jackets, and we sell a lot of them, but the thing people buy the most is scarves – they're cheaper and they make a nice present. Our prices this year are a little lower than last year, because we buy from a new company. You can return anything in fourteen days with the receipt, but please don't wear it first! And my advice: come on Sunday morning. Saturday is the busiest day and Thursday evening is terrible – you can't move in here!

Unit 6  Getting Around
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
In this unit you will…6.1  ask how to get to places6.2  buy tickets6.3  ask for travel information (how long, how far)6.4  give reasons and say why you do things6.5  make and answer suggestions6.6  explain signs and rules6.7  Review & Mediate: explain a station announcement

New in this unitAl-Amir Travel – the bus company at the Damascus garage (Damascus → Homs, Aleppo, Latakia).Jamal – a minibus (service) driver on the Mezzeh line.   ·   Nadia – Rami's colleague at the Kafr Sousa school site.

6.1  How do I get to the university?
Function: asking about transport   ·   Grammar: by bus / on foot; take / get / catch   ·   Pronunciation: /əʊ/ go, don't, road
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  How do you travel in your city? How do people travel between cities? Your teacher writes the class's ideas on the board.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I go to work by minibus. | Take the number 5 bus.
 | 
 | 
b  Which word? bus · minibus (service) · taxi · train · car · bicycle · on foot   Which is the cheapest in your city? Which is the fastest?
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Where does Lama want to go?   A to the university   B to the Old City   C to the airport
b  Listen again. 1 How much is the minibus?   2 Why doesn't Lama walk?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 6.1  At the minibus stop
Lama | Excuse me, does this minibus go to the Old City?
Woman | No, it doesn't. This one goes to Mezzeh. You need the Bab Touma service.
Lama | Oh. Where do I get it?
Woman | Cross the road and wait at the second stop. The driver shouts "Bab Touma!" – you can't miss it.
Lama | How much is it?
Woman | Two thousand. Have the money ready – you give it to the driver.
Lama | Thank you. How long does it take?
Woman | About twenty-five minutes, but at this time maybe forty. The traffic is terrible.
Lama | Can I walk?
Woman | You can, but it's a long way – about an hour on foot. And it's very hot today!
Lama | You're right. I'll take the service. Thank you so much!

Language boxGrammar – talking about transportby + transport | on foot | verbs
by bus / minibus / taxi / train / car / bike  ⚠ no the, no a: I go by bus. | I go on foot. / I walk. | take the bus · get the number 5 · catch the train (= be in time for it) · miss the bus
Questions: Does this bus go to the Old City? · How do I get to the university? · Where do I get it? · How much is it?
⚠ get to a place: How do I get to the station?  But: get on / off a bus.
Practise: Complete with by, on, to or –.  1 I go to work ______ minibus.  2 How do I get ______ the airport?  3 She goes ______ foot every day.  4 We usually take ______ the number 7 bus.  5 My brother comes ______ car.
Useful phrases: Does this bus go to…? · Where do I get it? · You can't miss it. · Have the money ready. · The traffic is terrible. · It's a long way. · You're right.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 6.2 /əʊ/ – your lips move from "o" to "u": go · don't · road · slow · nobody · home. Listen and repeat: I don't go home on the slow road.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A asks about transport, B is a local person with the card. Then swap.
A | Excuse me, does this __________ go to __________?
B | No, it doesn't. This one goes to __________. You need __________.
A | Where do I get it?
B | __________.
A | How much is it? And how long does it take?
B | __________. About __________ minutes.
A | Thank you so much!
Card A – to the airport | Card B – to Tishreen Park
airport bus from the garage · 15,000 SYP · 45 minutes · taxi 100,000 and faster | number 7 bus from the main street · 2,000 SYP · 20 minutes · or 30 minutes on foot
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform without the book. Listener task: write the transport, the price and the time.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Tell your partner how you get to this class – every step: on foot, by minibus, by car? How long does each step take? Then ask them about their journey. Then find a new partner and ask how to get from here to their home.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner's journey:  Hiba walks for ten minutes to the main street. Then she takes the Mezzeh service – it costs two thousand. She gets off at the pharmacy and walks for five minutes. It takes about forty minutes.
Listener task: who has the longest journey? Who comes on foot?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read Omar's message to a new colleague. Answer the questions.
Hi Samer! Welcome to TechCare. Here's how to get to the office from the bus garage.Take the Mezzeh service – it leaves from the first platform and costs 2,000. Tell the driver you want Al-Hamra Street. The journey takes about 25 minutes, but in the morning it can take 40, so leave early!Get off at the big pharmacy on the corner. Then walk up Al-Hamra Street for about five minutes. Our building is on the left, opposite a bank. We're on the fourth floor – there's a lift.If the traffic is very bad, a taxi from the garage is about 35,000 and takes 15 minutes.See you on Sunday! Omar
1  Where does the Mezzeh service leave from?
2  Why should Samer leave early?
3  What is the other option?
Writer's boxA travel instructions message: greeting → which transport (+ where it leaves from, price) → how long → where to get off → the last part on foot → another option → friendly ending.Use imperatives (Unit 3): Take… Tell… Get off… Walk…Give a warning about time or traffic: …so leave early!
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Hi ______! Here's how to get to ______________ from ______________.   Take ______________ – it ______________ and costs ______. The journey takes about ______, but ______________, so ______________.   Get off at ______________. Then walk ______________. ______________ is ______________.  6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
by bus / on foot used correctly | 
Each instruction starts with a verb | 
All parts: transport, price, time, where to get off, other option | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Two or three students read one line before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
A friend from another city is coming to visit you. Write a message explaining how to get from the bus garage (or station) to your home or workplace (60 words or more).







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's message as the visitor. ★ Very clear!  ✓ Can you add the price?  ? What if I arrive at night?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two messages are read aloud. Listeners say: could you find the place?
HomeworkWrite the visitor's reply (25+ words): say when you arrive, ask one question about the journey, and ask the person to meet you if you get lost.

6.2  A return to Aleppo, please
Function: buying tickets   ·   Grammar: single / return; I'd like… / How much is…? (recycle)   ·   Pronunciation: sentence stress in prices and times
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What tickets do you buy? (bus, train, cinema, match…) What do you ask when you buy a bus ticket?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
A return to Homs, please. | Ticket: Damascus – Homs · 20,000 SYP
 | 
 | 
b  Match: single · return · platform · seat number · luggage  →  the number of your place · a bag or suitcase · one way only · there and back · where the bus waits
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. When does Rami travel?   A today   B tomorrow morning   C Friday
b  Listen again. 1 Single or return?   2 How much does he pay?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 6.3  At the Al-Amir Travel desk
Clerk | Good morning. Where are you travelling to?
Rami | Aleppo, please. Have you got a bus tomorrow morning?
Clerk | Yes, we have buses at seven, nine and eleven.
Rami | The nine o'clock one, please. A single or a return… how much is a return?
Clerk | A single is forty-five thousand and a return is eighty thousand.
Rami | A return, then. I'm coming back on Sunday.
Clerk | Sunday at what time? The last bus from Aleppo leaves at six.
Rami | Six is fine. Can I change the time later?
Clerk | Yes, but you must call us one day before. Here's your ticket – seat 14, platform 3.
Rami | Thank you. Is there a place for luggage?
Clerk | Yes, under the bus. Please be here twenty minutes early.
Rami | OK. Thanks a lot.

Language boxBuying a ticketCustomer | Clerk
A single to Homs, please. · A return to Aleppo, please. · Two tickets for the nine o'clock bus. · How much is a return? · Can I change the time? · Is there a place for luggage? | Where are you travelling to? · When would you like to travel? · Single or return? · Seat 14, platform 3. · Please be here twenty minutes early.
single = one way (→)   return = there and back (→ ←)
must = it's necessary (a rule): You must call us one day before. (more in Unit 9)
Practise: Put the conversation in order.  ___ A return, please.  ___ Where are you travelling to?  ___ Here's your ticket. Platform 2.  ___ Latakia, please.  ___ Single or return?  ___ Thank you!
Useful phrases: Have you got a bus at…? · The nine o'clock one, please. · …, then. · Can I change it later? · Thanks a lot.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 6.4 Stress the important information: A reTURN to aLEPpo, please. · SEAT fourTEEN, platform THREE. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is the clerk with the timetable, B is the customer with the card. Then swap.

 4R   Report   (6 min)

LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read Rami's booking confirmation message. Answer the questions.
AL-AMIR TRAVEL – BOOKING CONFIRMEDPassenger: Rami Khoury   Booking number: AM-4471Out: Damascus → Aleppo · Saturday 18 October · 09:00 · Platform 3 · Seat 14Back: Aleppo → Damascus · Sunday 19 October · 18:00 · Seat 22Price: 80,000 SYP (return) – PAIDPlease arrive 20 minutes before the bus leaves. 1  What is Rami's seat number on the way back?
2  How much is extra luggage?

Writer's boxA booking confirmation: title → passenger + booking number → Out / Back (place, date, time, platform, seat) → price + paid → rules (arrive early, luggage, changes, refunds).Information lines are short (no full sentences), but the rules at the end are full sentences.Dates: Saturday 18 October. Times: 09:00 or 9 am.

To change your ticket, ______________.
 6P   Check together   (5 min)

All parts: passenger, number, out, back, price, rules |

 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write a booking confirmation for the trip you planned in Stage 4 – for a bus, a train or a flight (50 words or more). Add two rules of your own.






 7P   Respond   (5 min)

6.3  How long does it take?
Function: asking for travel information   ·   Grammar: How long / How far / How often; It takes…   ·   Pronunciation: 13 / 30 stress
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  How long is your journey to work or college? How far is it? Make a class list of questions travellers ask.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
How long does it take? | Journey time: about 2 hours
 | 
 | 
b  Which question? How long…? · How far…? · How often…? · How much…?   → every 20 minutes · 45 kilometres · 90 minutes · 30,000 SYP
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Where is Mr Lang going?   A to Aleppo   B to Palmyra   C to Bosra
b  Listen again. 1 How far is it?   2 How long does the visit take?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 6.5  Planning a day trip
Mr Lang | Lina, I have a free day on Friday. I'd like to see something outside Damascus.
Lina | Then you should visit Bosra. The Roman theatre there is amazing.
Mr Lang | How far is it from Damascus?
Lina | About a hundred and thirty kilometres, in the south.
Mr Lang | And how long does it take?
Lina | About two hours by car, maybe two and a half by bus.
Mr Lang | How often do the buses go?
Lina | Every hour in the morning, I think. But for one day, a taxi is easier. The driver waits for you.
Mr Lang | How much does that cost?
Lina | About two hundred thousand for the whole day. You can share it with someone.
Mr Lang | And how long do people usually stay there?
Lina | Two or three hours is enough for the theatre and the old city. So you can leave at eight and be back at four.
Mr Lang | Perfect. Would you like to come?
Lina | I'd love to! Let's ask Maya too.

Language boxGrammar – travel questionsQuestion | Answer
How long does it take? | It takes about two hours. / About 25 minutes.
How far is it (from Damascus)? | It's about 130 kilometres. / It's 20 minutes away.
How often do the buses go? | Every hour. / Three times a day.
How much does it cost? | It costs about 200,000.
What time does it leave / arrive? | It leaves at 8 and arrives at 10.
It takes + time: It takes two hours by bus.  With a person: It takes me forty minutes to get to work.
leave (= go away from a place) ≠ arrive (= come to a place): The bus leaves Damascus at 8 and arrives in Aleppo at 12.  ⚠ arrive in a city, arrive at a station / the airport.
Practise: Write the question.  1 ______? – It takes about an hour.  2 ______? – It's 45 kilometres.  3 ______? – Every twenty minutes.  4 ______? – It arrives at half past six.  5 ______? – Thirty thousand pounds.
Useful phrases: I have a free day on… · You should visit… · …, I think. · for the whole day · That's enough for… · Would you like to come?
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 6.6 Stress: THIRteen (13) / THIRty (30) · fourTEEN (14) / FORty (40) · fifTEEN (15) / FIFty (50). Listen: is it 13 or 30? Then say the numbers to your partner.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A asks about the trip on the card, B answers. Then swap.
A | I'd like to visit __________. How far is it?
B | About __________ kilometres.
A | How long does it take?
B | About __________ by __________.
A | How often do the buses go?
B | __________.
A | And how much does it cost?
B | __________.
Trip card A – Maaloula | Trip card B – Krak des Chevaliers
56 km north · 1 hour by car · buses every 2 hours · 15,000 single · stay 3 hours | 160 km from Damascus · 2.5 hours by car · no direct bus (go to Homs first) · taxi 250,000 for the day · stay 2 hours
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the distance, the time and the price.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
a  Ask your partner about three real journeys: home → work/college · home → the city centre · your city → another city. Ask How long? How far? How much? How often?
b  Now plan a day trip together to a real place. Agree: where, how, what time to leave, how long to stay, how much it costs.
 4R   Report   (6 min)

Listener task: which trip is the cheapest? Which is the longest?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read Hiba's paragraph about her journey to work. Answer the questions.
My journey to workI live in Jaramana and I work in Mezzeh, so my journey is long. It's about twelve kilometres, but it takes me more than an hour in the morning!First, I walk for ten minutes to the main street. Then I take a service to the city centre – it costs 2,000 and takes about thirty minutes, but in the morning it can take fifty. After that, I change and take a second service to Mezzeh. Finally, I walk for five minutes to my office.I usually leave home at seven and arrive at work at a quarter past eight. The journey home is faster because I leave at four. On Fridays I don't work, so I stay in bed!
1  How far is Hiba's journey?
2  How many times does she change?
3  Why is the journey home faster?
Writer's boxDescribing a journey: where you live and work → total distance and time → the steps (First, Then, After that, Finally) → leave / arrive times → one extra detail (the journey home, Fridays, a problem).Use It takes me… for your own journey and give times and prices for each step.

6.4  I take the bus because it's cheaper
Function: giving reasons and purpose   ·   Grammar: because; to + verb (purpose)   ·   Pronunciation: weak because /bɪˈkəz/, to /tə/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  Why do people choose the bus, a taxi, a car, or walking? Make two lists: good things and bad things about each.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I take the bus because it's cheaper. | I walk to save money and to keep fit.
 | 
 | 
b  Finish the sentences: I take a taxi when ______ · I never drive in the centre because ______ · I walk to ______
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Who drives to work every day?   A Rami   B Nadia   C nobody
b  Listen again. 1 Why doesn't Rami drive to the site?   2 Why does Nadia drive?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 6.7  At the building site in Kafr Sousa
Nadia | Rami, do you want a lift home? I've got the car today.
Rami | Thanks, Nadia, but I take the service because it's much cheaper. And honestly, it's faster at this time.
Nadia | That's true. The traffic on the highway is terrible.
Rami | Why do you drive? You live near the centre, don't you?
Nadia | I drive to take my daughter to school first. The school is in the other direction, so I need the car.
Rami | Ah, I understand. Does she like school?
Nadia | She loves it! But she gets up at six to be there at half past seven. Poor girl.
Rami | I get up at six too – to go running!
Nadia | Running? Why?
Rami | Because I sit in meetings all day. I run to keep fit and to think about my problems!
Nadia | Hmm. I think I prefer the traffic.

Language boxGrammar – because and to + verbbecause + subject + verb (a reason) | to + base verb (a purpose = why)
I take the service because it's cheaper. · She gets up early because her school is far. | I run to keep fit. · I drive to take my daughter to school. · She gets up at six to be there at 7.30.
⚠ to keep fit, not for keep fit, not for to keep fit.  (We say for exercise + noun, but to keep fit + verb.)
Answering Why?: Because it's cheaper. (a full reason) or To save money. (a purpose)
so = the result (opposite order): The school is in the other direction, so I need the car.
Practise: Join the sentences with because, to or so.  1 I walk to work. I want to save money. (to)  2 I don't drive in the centre. There's no parking. (because)  3 It was raining. We took a taxi. (so)  4 She studies English. She wants a better job. (to)  5 I like the train. It's comfortable. (because)
Useful phrases: Do you want a lift? · That's true. · Honestly,… · Ah, I understand. · Poor girl! · I prefer…
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 6.8 Weak forms: because /bɪˈkəz/ · to /tə/: I run to‿keep fit. /tə kiːp/ · …because it's cheaper. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A asks Why…? about each thing on the card. B answers with because or to. Then swap.
A | Why do you __________?
B | Because __________. / To __________.
A | Really? And why don't you __________?
B | Because __________.
A | That's true. I __________ because __________.
Card A – ask about… | Card B – ask about…
walk to work · get up early · use a bicycle in the city | take a taxi at night · study English · live near your work
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write two reasons with because and two with to.
 4   Your turn – the "Why?" game   (9 min)
a  Your partner asks Why? about five true things in your life: your job, your city, your transport, your English class, your free time. Answer every time with because or to – never "I don't know"!
b  Now discuss: what is the best way to travel in your city – and why? Agree on three reasons.
 4R   Report   (6 min)

LESSON B – WRITING

There isn't enough space near my pharmacy, so I spend twenty minutes every morning looking for a place. The second reason is money: petrol, repairs and the car itself are very expensive.But the most important reason is time. I take the service to work because it's faster in the morning traffic. And I walk home in the evening to get some exercise and to think.Of course, a car is better for families or for people who work far away. But for me, in the centre of Damascus, two feet and a service are enough!
1  What is Maya's first reason?
2  Why does she walk home?
3  Who does she think needs a car?
Writer's boxAn opinion post: title + your position → reason 1 → reason 2 → the most important reason → the other side (Of course,…) → a short, strong ending.Order your reasons: The first reason is… The second reason is… But the most important reason is…Use because, to + verb and so in each paragraph.

6.5  Why don't we go on Friday?
Function: making and answering suggestions   ·   Grammar: Let's… / Why don't we…? / Shall we…? / How about + -ing?   ·   Pronunciation: Shall we /ʃəl wi/, Let's /lets/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  How do you and your friends decide where to go? What do you say? What do you write in the group chat?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Let's go to the park! | How about Friday morning?
 | 
 | 
b  Yes or no? Good idea! · I'm not sure. · Why not? · Let's do that. · I'd rather not. · That sounds great!
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What do they decide to do?   A go to Bosra   B go to Maaloula   C stay in Damascus
b  Listen again. 1 Why not Saturday?   2 How do they decide to travel?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 6.9  Planning the trip
Lina | So, Mr Lang has a free day. Shall we take him to Bosra?
Maya | Hmm, Bosra is a long way – about two hours each way. How about Maaloula? It's closer.
Omar | Good idea. And the road is beautiful.
Lina | OK, Maaloula. Let's go on Friday, then.
Maya | Why don't we go on Saturday? On Friday everything is busy and the restaurants are full.
Lina | Saturday is a working day for me, I'm afraid.
Omar | Friday is fine. Let's leave early – at seven – to miss the traffic.
Maya | Seven! Shall we say eight?
Omar | OK, eight. And how about taking my car? Four people, one car – it's cheaper.
Lina | Perfect. And let's take some food with us.
Maya | I'll make sandwiches. Omar, why don't you bring the tea?
Omar | Deal!

Language boxGrammar – suggestionsSuggestion | Form | Example
Let's + base verb | Let's go! | Let's leave at eight.
Shall we + base verb? | question | Shall we take him to Bosra?
Why don't we / you + base verb? | question | Why don't we go on Saturday?
How about + -ing / noun? | -ing! | How about taking my car? · How about Friday?
⚠ How about + -ing: How about going…?, not How about to go.  Let's / Shall we / Why don't we + base verb.
Answering: 😀 Good idea! · That sounds great! · Why not? · Let's do that. · Perfect. · Deal!   😐 Hmm, I'm not sure. · I'd rather not. · …, I'm afraid. · Shall we say…? (= a different suggestion)
Practise: Make suggestions.  1 (go / cinema / tonight) – Let's ______  2 (take / the bus) – Shall we ______?  3 (meet / at six) – Why don't we ______?  4 (go / by taxi) – How about ______?  5 (leave early) – Let's ______
Useful phrases: It's a long way. · It's closer. · …, I'm afraid. · Shall we say eight? · Deal!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 6.10 Shall we /ʃəl wi/ – fast and weak. Let's /lets/ – one syllable. How about /haʊ əˈbaʊt/. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. Plan the day on the card. Rule: you must use a different suggestion form each time, and you must disagree at least once. Then swap cards.
A | Shall we __________?
B | Hmm, I'm not sure. How about __________?
A | Good idea. Let's __________, then.
B | Why don't we __________?
A | __________, I'm afraid. Shall we say __________?
B | Perfect. And let's __________.
A | Deal!
Card A – a day out with a visitor | Card B – a surprise for a colleague
where to go · what time to leave · how to travel · what to take | what present · when to give it · where to meet · who pays

6.6  You can't park here
Function: explaining signs and rules   ·   Grammar: can / can't; must / mustn't (intro)   ·   Pronunciation: can /kən/ vs can't /kɑːnt/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What signs do you see every day – in the street, at work, in a bus? Draw three on the board. What do they mean?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
You can't park here. | NO PARKING
 | 
 | 
b  Match the sign and the meaning: NO ENTRY · NO SMOKING · KEEP OFF THE GRASS · QUIET PLEASE · EXIT · NO PHOTOS
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Where are Lina and Mr Lang?   A at the airport   B at a museum   C at the bus station
b  Listen again. 1 What can't he do inside?   2 What must he do with his bag?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 6.11  At the National Museum
Mr Lang | Lina, what does that sign mean? I can see a camera with a line through it.
Lina | It means "no photos". You can't take photos inside this room because of the old paintings.
Mr Lang | I see. Can I take photos in the garden?
Lina | Yes, you can. The garden is fine – everybody takes photos there.
Mr Lang | Good. And this sign? "Please leave bags at the desk."
Lina | You must leave your bag with the guard. Big bags aren't allowed inside.
Mr Lang | OK. Is my laptop bag too big?
Lina | I think so. Let's ask… Excuse me, can he take this bag inside?
Guard | No, I'm sorry. Bags must stay here. But you can keep your phone and your wallet.
Mr Lang | No problem. And can we eat in the garden?
Guard | You can drink water, but you mustn't eat near the old stones. There's a café outside the gate.
Mr Lang | Thank you. Lina, your English lessons are working – I understood everything!

Language boxGrammar – can / can't, must / mustn't | Meaning | Example
can | it's OK / allowed | You can take photos in the garden.
can't | it isn't allowed | You can't park here.
must | it's necessary – do it! | You must leave your bag at the desk.
mustn't | don't do it! (it's forbidden) | You mustn't eat near the old stones.
All of them + base verb: You must leave…, not must to leave. No -s: He can, not He cans.
Explaining a sign: It means… · It's not allowed. · You have to… (Unit 9) · You can keep…
Practise: Complete with can, can't, must or mustn't.  1 You ______ park here – look at the sign!  2 Children ______ swim here, but an adult must be with them.  3 You ______ show your ticket to the driver.  4 You ______ smoke in the hospital. It's forbidden.  5 ______ I take a photo of you?
Useful phrases: What does that sign mean? · It means… · I see. · Is it too big? · Let's ask. · No problem.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 6.12 can is weak /kən/: You can‿take photos.  can't is strong and long /kɑːnt/: You can't park here. Listen: can or can't? Then practise with your partner.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is a visitor who asks about the signs on the card. B explains with can / can't / must / mustn't. Then swap.
A | What does that sign mean?
B | It means __________. You __________ here.
A | I see. Can I __________?
B | Yes, you can. / No, you can't, because __________.
A | And this one?
B | You must __________.
Signs card A – at the park | Signs card B – on the bus

6.7  Review & Mediate: What did they say?
Function: explaining a station announcement   ·   Recycling: Units 1–6   ·   Exam practice: A2 Key Listening Part 1, Reading Part 1, Writing Part 6
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready – Unit 6 quiz   (6 min)
In teams. Correct the mistakes! 1 I go to work with bus.  2 How long takes it?  3 I walk for save money.  4 How about to go on Friday?  5 You must to show your ticket.  6 The bus arrives to Aleppo at twelve.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Read the departures board at the Damascus bus garage.
DEPARTURES |  |  | 
Destination | Time | Platform | Note
Homs | 12:00 | 3 | ON TIME
Aleppo | 12:30 | 5 | DELAYED – 13:15
Latakia | 13:00 | 2 | PLATFORM CHANGED → now 7
Tartus | 13:30 | 4 | ON TIME
Suwayda | 14:00 | 1 | CANCELLED – next bus 16:00
b  Books closed. Listen. Mr Lang is at the garage. What is the problem with his bus?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 6.13  An announcement at the garage
Announcement | Attention please. The twelve thirty bus to Aleppo is delayed. It will now leave at thirteen fifteen from platform five. The one o'clock bus to Latakia will leave from platform seven, not platform two. Passengers for Suwayda: the two o'clock bus is cancelled. The next bus to Suwayda is at four o'clock. We are sorry for the delay.
Mr Lang | Lina, I understood "Aleppo" and some numbers… What did they say?
Lina | Your bus is late, I'm afraid. It leaves at a quarter past one now, not half past twelve.
Mr Lang | Forty-five minutes! Is the platform the same?
Lina | Yes, platform five – that's the same. But the Latakia bus is moving to platform seven.
Mr Lang | And the woman next to us looks worried.
Lina | Because the Suwayda bus is cancelled. She must wait until four o'clock. Poor woman.
Mr Lang | So we have time for a coffee!
Lina | Good idea. But let's stay near the platform. Sometimes the bus leaves early when the road is clear!

Language box – explaining an announcementSay it in simple words, with the important information first:DELAYED – 13:15 → Your bus is late. It leaves at a quarter past one now.   PLATFORM CHANGED → The platform is different now – it's number seven.   CANCELLED → There's no bus at two. The next one is at four.Compare with what the person expects: …not half past twelve. · That's the same.Useful phrases: What did they say? · Your bus is late, I'm afraid. · It leaves at… now. · It's the same. · …is moving to platform… · She must wait until… · So we have time for…Note: will leave / it will now leave – learn as a phrase here. We study will in Book 2.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A can read the board; B can't (close your book!). B asks about their bus; A explains. Then swap.
B travels to… | B travels to…
Latakia (expects 13:00, platform 2) | Suwayda (expects 14:00, platform 1)
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the new time and platform for each traveller.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Think of a real announcement or message you heard recently: at work, at a bus stop, at the university, in a mosque, on the radio, or a message in a group chat. Tell your partner what it said in your own words. Your partner asks three questions.
 4R   Report – mediation   (6 min)
Tell the class what your partner heard:  At Hiba's university there was an announcement on Sunday. The exams are moving from the big hall to the new building because the hall is closed. Students must bring their cards and arrive thirty minutes early.
Listener task: which announcement is the most important? Which is the most confusing?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.

   Woman | I've got a car, but I leave it at home. There's no parking, so I take the service.
2  Clerk | The ten o'clock bus is full, I'm afraid. The next one is at half past ten.
   Man | Half past ten is fine.

Part 2  ·  Three people  (5 marks)Read about three people. For each question, choose A, B or C.

A  Samira – shop assistant | B  Jamal – minibus driver | C  Hiba – student
Samira works at a clothes shop in Al-Hamra Centre. She starts at ten and finishes at nine, six days a week. She lives near the centre, so she walks to work. Thursday is the busiest day. She loves her job because she meets new people, but her feet hurt in the evening. | Jamal drives a minibus on the Mezzeh line. He gets up at half past four and starts at half past five. He finishes at three and sleeps in the afternoon. The traffic is terrible in the morning, but he knows every small street in the city. On Fridays he doesn't work; he takes his children to the park. | Hiba studies architecture. She lives in Jaramana and her college is in the centre, so her journey takes more than an hour. She takes two services every morning. In the afternoon she works in a café near the college. These days she's studying every night because she has exams.

Part 3  ·  Longer text  (5 marks)Read the text and choose the correct answer A, B or C.

My first week in Damascus – by Lama NasserI'm nineteen and I come from Latakia. Last month I moved to Damascus to study English literature at the university. My sister Maya lives here too, so I'm not alone, but my flat is in Baramkeh and she lives in Mezzeh – about forty minutes away by service.My flat is small but bright. There are two bedrooms, a kitchen and a balcony with some plants. My favourite room is the balcony: I study there every morning because it's quiet and the air is fresh. There isn't a washing machine, but there's a laundry next to the building. The lift doesn't work, so I walk up to the third floor every day. My legs are strong now!The best thing is my neighbour, Umm Ziad. On my first day she knocked on my door with a plate of food and told me everything: where the bakery is (on the corner, opposite the mosque), when the bread is hot (seven o'clock in the morning) and which shop is the cheapest. Now we have tea together on Sundays.The only problem is the noise. My street is near a main road, so I hear cars all night. But I'm happy here. Next month my parents are coming to visit, and I want to show them my city – my new city.

12  Why did Lama move to Damascus?   A to work   B to study   C to live with her sister
13  Where does Lama study in the morning?   A in the kitchen   B at the university   C on the balcony
14  Why does she walk up to the third floor?   A Because the lift is broken.   B Because she wants exercise.   C Because there isn't a lift.
15  What did Umm Ziad do on Lama's first day?   A She invited her for tea.   B She brought her some food.   C She took her to the bakery.
16  What does Lama not like about her flat?   A the size   B the stairs   C the noise

How your teacher marks your speaking (10 marks) | 4–5 marks | 2–3 marks | 0–1 mark
Grammar and vocabulary (5) | Uses Unit 1–6 language well: present simple, there is/are, comparatives, can/must, because/to. Few mistakes. | Some good language, but repeats the same words and makes mistakes that slow the listener. | Very short answers; many mistakes.
Communication and pronunciation (5) | Speaks clearly, answers fully, asks the partner questions, keeps the conversation going. | Answers, but waits for the teacher; sometimes hard to understand. | Needs a lot of help; very hard to understand.

Unit 7  Free Time & Arrangements
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
In this unit you will…7.1  talk about what you can do and what you're good at7.2  talk about your arrangements for this week7.3  say no politely to an invitation7.4  agree a time and a place, and change an arrangement7.5  talk about what you prefer7.6  talk about what's on in your city7.7  Review & Mediate: plan an outing from leaflets

New in this unitAl-Yasmin Clubs – the language centre now has three clubs: photography, cooking and football.Dar Al-Fann Cultural Centre – a small arts centre in the Old City (concerts, films, exhibitions).Ziad – Lina's brother (dentist). His daughter Jana is three.   ·   Sara – a classmate in 4B.

7.1  I'm quite good at photography
Function: talking about abilities and hobbies   ·   Grammar: can; could (past ability); good at + -ing   ·   Pronunciation: can /kən/ and could /kʊd/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What can people in this class do? (swim, drive, cook, draw, play an instrument, fix things…) Your teacher writes the class's ideas on the board.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I can swim, but not very well. | I'm good at drawing.
 | 
 | 
b  Complete: I can ______ very well. · I can't ______ at all. · I'm good at ______. · I'm not very good at ______.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Which club does Omar join?   A cooking   B photography   C football
b  Listen again. 1 What could Omar do when he was a child?   2 What is Sara good at?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 7.1  Club sign-up day at Al-Yasmin
Mr Bassam | Good evening! Three clubs this term: photography, cooking and football. Which one for you?
Omar | I'm not sure. I can cook quite well, so maybe the cooking club isn't for me.
Sara | Then come to the photography club with me!
Omar | But I can't take good photos at all. My pictures are always dark.
Sara | That's why it's a club – you learn! Are you good at anything with a camera?
Omar | Well… when I was at school I could draw quite well. Does that help?
Sara | Of course! Drawing and photography are both about light and shapes.
Omar | OK, you win. What about you? Are you good at photography?
Sara | I'm quite good at taking photos of people, but I'm not very good at buildings. I want to learn.
Omar | And can you use the editing programs?
Sara | No, I can't. But you work with computers – you can teach me!
Omar | Deal. Mr Bassam, two for the photography club, please!

Language boxGrammar – can, could, good atcan (now) | could (in the past) | good at + -ing
I can swim. · She can't drive. · Can you cook? – Yes, I can. | When I was a child I could draw. · I couldn't swim at six. | I'm good at cooking. · He's not very good at drawing.
can / could + base verb: I can swim, not I can to swim. No -s: She can drive, not She cans.
How well? very well · quite well · not very well · not… at all: I can cook quite well. I can't sing at all.
good at + noun or -ing: good at English · good at speaking English.  ⚠ Not good in.
Practise: Complete with can, can't, could, couldn't or good at.  1 I ______ play the oud – my uncle taught me.  2 When I was five I ______ read, but now I read every day.  3 She's very ______ cooking.  4 ______ you drive? – No, I ______.  5 He ______ swim at all – he's afraid of water.
Useful phrases: I'm not sure. · …, so maybe… · That's why… · Does that help? · OK, you win. · Deal.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 7.2 can is weak /kən/ in sentences: I can‿swim.  can't is strong /kɑːnt/. could /kʊd/ – short, like "good". Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is at the sign-up desk, B chooses a club. Use the skeleton and a card. Then swap.
A | Three clubs this term: __________. Which one for you?
B | I'm not sure. I can __________ quite well, so maybe __________ isn't for me.
A | Then come to __________!
B | But I can't __________ at all.
A | That's why it's a club – you learn! Are you good at __________?
B | Well… when I was __________ I could __________.
A | That helps! So, __________?
B | OK, you win!
Card A – you… | Card B – you…
can cook well · can't swim · could run fast at school · want to be fitter | can drive · can't play any instrument · could sing in the school choir · love music
 3R   Perform   (5 min)

AL-YASMIN CLUBS – APPLICATION FORMName: Sara Hamdan   Class: 4B   Club: PhotographyWhy do you want to join this club?I love taking photos, but I only use my phone and I don't really understand how a camera works. 7.2  What are you doing on Friday?
Function: talking about arrangements   ·   Grammar: present continuous for the future   ·   Pronunciation: contractions I'm meeting, we're going
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What have you got in your diary this week? (work, family, appointments, friends…) What do you write in a diary or on your phone?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I'm meeting Omar at seven. | Thu 6.30 – dentist
 | 
 | 
b  Which are arrangements (you decided with another person) and which are plans in your head? I'm seeing the dentist at five. · I want to clean the flat. · We're having lunch with my parents. · Maybe I'll read a book.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. When can they meet?   A Thursday   B Friday   C Saturday
b  Listen again. 1 What is Maya doing on Thursday evening?   2 Who is coming on Saturday?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 7.3  A busy week
Lina | Maya, are you free this week? I want to see that new film.
Maya | Let me look at my diary… I'm working late on Tuesday and Wednesday.
Lina | What about Thursday?
Maya | On Thursday I'm going to the photography club after class – I'm taking my sister. Sorry!
Lina | Hmm. Friday?
Maya | On Friday morning I'm having lunch with my aunt, but I'm free in the evening. What time does the film start?
Lina | There are two: six o'clock and half past eight.
Maya | The six o'clock one is better for me. Is Omar coming?
Lina | No, he isn't. He's playing football with the club on Friday evening.
Maya | And on Saturday my parents are arriving from Latakia, so Friday really is the only day!
Lina | Friday at six, then. I'm booking the tickets tonight.

Language boxGrammar – present continuous for the future (arrangements)We use am / is / are + -ing for things we have already arranged with other people, with a time word:+ | – | ?
I'm meeting Omar at seven. | He isn't coming on Friday. | Are you doing anything on Thursday?
My parents are arriving on Saturday. | We aren't going to the club this week. | What are you doing at the weekend?
Time words: tonight · tomorrow · on Friday · this week · at the weekend · next month
Compare: I work on Fridays. (a routine – Unit 2)  /  I'm working this Friday. (this week only – an arrangement)
Practise: Make sentences about the future.  1 (I / meet / my manager / at 10) ______  2 (she / not come / tonight) ______  3 (what / you / do / tomorrow?) ______  4 (we / have / dinner / with Ziad / on Friday) ______  5 (they / arrive / on Saturday) ______
Useful phrases: Are you free…? · Let me look at my diary. · What about…? · I'm free in the evening. · …is better for me. · …, then.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 7.4 Say the contraction, not two words: I'm meeting /aɪm ˈmiːtɪŋ/ · we're going /wɪə ˈɡəʊɪŋ/ · they're arriving. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. Don't show your diary! Find a day when you are both free. Use the skeleton. Then swap diaries and do it again.
A | Are you free this week? I want to __________.
B | Let me look at my diary… I'm __________ on __________.
A | What about __________?
B | On __________ I'm __________. Sorry!
A | Hmm. __________?
B | I'm free in the __________. What time?
A | __________ at __________, then!
Diary A | Diary B
Mon: working late · Tue: dentist 6.30 · Wed: free · Thu: cooking club 6–8 · Fri: free evening · Sat: cousin's wedding | Mon: free · Tue: free evening · Wed: mother arriving from Homs · Thu: free · Fri: football 5–7, free after 8 · Sat: working

LINA – THIS WEEKSun: work 8–4 · evening: Jana's birthday at Ziad's house 🎂Mon: work 8–4 · gym 6.00Tue: work 8–6 (end-of-month accounts) 😩Wed: work 8–6 · Mum coming for dinnerThu: work 8–4 · ENGLISH CLASS 5.30Fri: free! · 6.00 film with Maya
