2  What is happening on Sunday evening?
3  What is Lina doing tonight about the film?
Writer's boxA "my week" message: greeting → the busy days (with reasons) → a special day → the free day → your arrangement → a friendly ending.Use the present continuous for arrangements and because for reasons.A diary uses short notes (work 8–4), but a message uses full sentences.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Hi ______! Here's my week. I'm ______________ on ______ and ______ because ______________. On ______ I'm ______________. ______________ is ______________ as usual. So ______ is perfect for ______________. I'm ______________ tonight. See you ______________!
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters (days!) and full stops | 
am / is / are + -ing for arrangements | 
A time word in each sentence (on Friday, this week…) | 
All parts: busy days + reasons, special day, free day, arrangement | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write a message to a friend or a relative about your real week (50 words or more). Say what you're doing on at least four days and suggest one day to meet.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
★ Your week is so busy!   ✓ Can you add what time?   ? Are you free on…?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two messages are read aloud. The class says which day sounds the hardest.
HomeworkWrite the friend's reply (25+ words): say your own week, accept or change the day, and agree a time.

7.3  I'd love to, but…
Function: saying no politely   ·   Grammar: but / because with excuses; I'd love to, but…   ·   Pronunciation: warm, falling intonation in refusals
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  When is it difficult to say no? (a wedding, a family lunch, your manager, a neighbour…) What do people say? What do they write?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I'd love to, but I'm working. | Thank you so much for the invitation…
 | 
 | 
b  Good or bad? "No." · "I'd love to, but I can't." · "I'm busy." · "I'm really sorry – I'm away that weekend." · "Maybe."   Which answers would make you sad?
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What is the invitation?   A a wedding   B a birthday dinner   C a trip
b  Listen again. 1 Why can't Omar come?   2 What does he offer to do?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 7.5  A difficult no
Hiba | Omar, my cousin is getting married on the 24th. Would you like to come? It's in Homs.
Omar | Oh, thank you so much! I'd love to, but I'm afraid I can't. My brother is arriving from Dubai that weekend – I only see him once a year.
Hiba | Oh, that's a shame. But of course, family first!
Omar | I'm really sorry, Hiba. I was looking forward to meeting your family.
Hiba | Don't worry. There's always the next wedding – I have a big family!
Omar | Ha! Listen, can I do something else? I can take the photos at the engagement party next month.
Hiba | Really? That's very kind of you!
Omar | Of course. I'm learning at the photography club – your family can be my practice!
Hiba | Perfect. And bring your brother to the party if he's still here!

Language boxSaying no politely – the four steps1 Thank | 2 Say no | 3 Give a reason | 4 Keep the friendship
Thank you so much! · That's very kind of you. | I'd love to, but I'm afraid I can't. · I'm really sorry… | My brother is arriving that weekend. · because I'm working. | Maybe next time. · Can I do something else? · Have a wonderful day!
⚠ Never only "No" or "I'm busy" – in English (and in Arabic!) that sounds rude. Always thank + reason.
Notice: the reason is often an arrangement (7.2): I'm working that day. My brother is arriving.
Answering a "no": That's a shame. · Don't worry. · Of course – family first! · Maybe next time.
Practise: Make polite refusals with a reason.  1 (a wedding / you're travelling) ______  2 (dinner on Thursday / you have class) ______  3 (football on Friday / your knee hurts) ______  4 (a trip on Saturday / you're working) ______
Useful phrases: I was looking forward to… · There's always the next… · Can I do something else? · if he's still here
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 7.6 Start high and warm on Oh, thank you so much! then go down ↘ on …but I'm afraid I can't. A flat voice sounds like you don't care. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A invites, B refuses politely using all four steps. Then swap.
A | __________ is / are __________ on __________. Would you like to come?
B | Oh, thank you so much! I'd love to, but I'm afraid I can't. I'm __________.
A | Oh, that's a shame.
B | I'm really sorry. I was looking forward to __________.
A | Don't worry. Maybe next time.
B | Listen, can I __________ instead?
A | That's very kind of you!
Card A – invitations | Card B – invitations
your sister's graduation party, Saturday · a picnic on Friday · a football match tonight | your son's birthday, Thursday · a trip to Maaloula on Friday · dinner at your new flat
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: tick the four steps. Did B do all of them?
☐ 1 Thank | ☐ 2 Say no | ☐ 3 Reason | ☐ 4 Keep the friendship
 4   Your turn – you can't say yes to everything!   (9 min)
Everyone writes two invitations on paper (real or invented: a wedding, a dinner, a trip, a match, a party). Walk around and invite people. Rule: you can only say yes twice – you must refuse all the other invitations politely, with a different reason each time!
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class:  I invited six people to my barbecue. Hiba and Fadi are coming. Tareq can't come because he's visiting his parents in Suwayda, and Rasha is working. Everybody was very polite – nobody just said no!
Listener task: whose refusal was the most polite? Whose excuse was the funniest?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read Omar's written reply to the wedding invitation. Answer the questions.
Dear Hiba,Thank you very much for inviting me to your cousin's wedding on the 24th. It was very kind of you to think of me.I'm really sorry, but I'm afraid I can't come. My brother Khaled is arriving from Dubai on the same weekend, and I only see him once a year. I was looking forward to meeting your family and seeing Homs again.I hope the day is beautiful and that everyone dances until the morning! Please give my best wishes to your cousin and her husband.If it's useful, I'd be happy to take the photos at the engagement party next month – just tell me the date.With best wishes,Omar
1  When is the wedding?
2  What two things does Omar wish them?
3  What does he offer?
Writer's boxA written refusal is longer and warmer than a spoken one: Dear + name, → thank them → say no + the reason → what you'll miss → good wishes for the day → an offer or a next time → With best wishes, → name.Use I'm afraid, I was looking forward to… and I hope… – these make a refusal kind.Never write only two lines for an important invitation (a wedding, a graduation): the length shows respect.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Dear ______,   Thank you very much for inviting me to ______________. It was very kind of you ______________.   I'm really sorry, but I'm afraid I can't come. ______________. I was looking forward to ______________.   I hope ______________. Please ______________.   If it's useful, I'd be happy to ______________.   With best wishes,   ______
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
All four steps: thank, no, reason, keep the friendship | 
A reason with an arrangement (I'm …-ing) or because | 
Good wishes + an offer or next time | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read your refusal sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write a polite refusal (60 words or more) to a real or invented invitation you can't accept: a wedding, a graduation, a work trip, a party, a family lunch.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's letter as the person who invited them. ★ What a kind letter!  ✓ Can you add…?  ? Can you come to…instead?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two letters are read aloud. The class decides: is the writer still a good friend after this letter? Why?
HomeworkWrite the reply to your partner's refusal (25+ words): say it's OK, say you'll miss them, and suggest another time or accept their offer.

7.4  Can we make it seven?
Function: agreeing and changing an arrangement   ·   Grammar: prepositions of time and place (recycle); Can we make it…?   ·   Pronunciation: at /ət/, in /ɪn/, on /ɒn/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  Where do you meet friends? (a café, the centre, outside a shop, a gate…) Why do people change arrangements?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Can we make it seven? | See you at 7 outside the cinema.
 | 
 | 
b  at, in or on?  ___ Friday · ___ half past six · ___ the morning · ___ the weekend · ___ 24 October · ___ night
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Who needs to change the time?   A Lina   B Maya   C nobody
b  Listen again. 1 What is the new time?   2 Where do they decide to meet?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 7.7  Changing the plan
Maya | Lina, I'm really sorry – can we change the film to half past eight?
Lina | Oh! Is everything OK?
Maya | Yes, but the pharmacy is busy on Friday evening and my colleague is ill. I can't leave at five.
Lina | No problem. Can you be there at eight?
Maya | Let's say a quarter past eight, to be safe. Can we make it the late film?
Lina | OK, the half past eight one. I'll change the tickets.
Maya | Thank you! Where shall we meet?
Lina | How about outside the cinema, next to the ticket office? It's on the first floor of the centre.
Maya | Perfect. And if I'm late, don't wait outside – go in and I'll find you.
Lina | OK. Row G, I think. I'll send you the seat numbers.
Maya | You're a star. See you on Friday!

Language boxAgreeing and changing an arrangementSuggesting a time / place | Changing it | Confirming
What time shall we meet? · Where shall we meet? · How about outside the cinema? | Can we make it seven? · Can we change it to Saturday? · Let's say a quarter past, to be safe. | OK, seven, then. · Perfect. · See you on Friday! · I'll send you…
at / in / on (recycle, Unit 2): at 8.30, at night · in the morning / the evening · on Friday, on 24 October · at the weekend
Places to meet: outside the cinema · next to the ticket office · in front of the mosque · at the main gate · on the first floor
Practise: Complete with at, in, on or outside.  1 Let's meet ______ half past six.  2 I'm free ______ the evening.  3 The wedding is ______ 24 October.  4 Wait for me ______ the bank.  5 I'm busy ______ the weekend.
Useful phrases: Is everything OK? · No problem. · …, to be safe. · Don't wait – go in. · You're a star!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 7.8 Weak prepositions: at‿eight /ət eɪt/ · in‿the morning · on‿Friday. The important word is the time, not the preposition. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A has an arrangement; B needs to change it. Agree a new time and a place. Then swap.
B | I'm really sorry – can we change __________ to __________?
A | Oh! Is everything OK?
B | Yes, but __________.
A | No problem. Can you be there at __________?
B | Let's say __________, to be safe.
A | OK. Where shall we meet?
B | How about __________?
A | Perfect. See you on __________!
Card A – the arrangement | Card B – your problem
dinner at a restaurant, Thursday 7 pm | your meeting finishes at 7.30 · you want Friday instead
studying together, Saturday 10 am, the library | the library is closed on Saturday · you can only come in the afternoon
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the new day, time and place.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
a  Arrange a real meeting with your partner (coffee after class, studying together, a walk). Agree the day, the time and the exact place.
b  Now your teacher gives you a problem (your boss needs you · your child is ill · the bus is cancelled). Phone your partner and change the arrangement. Agree a new time and place.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class:  Hiba and I are meeting on Sunday to study. First we said ten o'clock at the library, but the library opens at eleven on Sundays, so we changed it. Now we're meeting at eleven in the café next to the centre.
Listener task: how many pairs changed their plan? What was the most common problem?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the three short messages. Answer the questions.
1 · Lina → Maya (Thursday evening)Hi Maya! Just confirming Friday: the film starts at 8.30, so let's meet at 8.15 outside the cinema, next to the ticket office (first floor). I've got the tickets – Row G, seats 11 and 12. 🎬2 · Maya → Lina (Friday, 5.40 pm)Lina, I'm so sorry – a delivery arrived late and I'm still at the pharmacy. I'm leaving in ten minutes, so I'll be about fifteen minutes late. Please don't wait outside – go in and I'll find you. Sorry!! 🙏3 · Lina → Maya (Friday, 5.45 pm)No problem at all! I'm inside, Row G on the left. The film starts with adverts anyway, so you won't miss anything. Take your time. ☕
1  What exact place do they agree to meet?
2  Why is Maya late?
3  What does Lina tell her not to worry about?
Writer's boxThree useful messages: confirming (day, time, exact place, extra information) · saying you're late (sorry + reason + how late + what the other person should do) · answering (don't worry + where you are + something kind).Be exact about the place: not at the cinema but outside the cinema, next to the ticket office.When you're late, always say how many minutes – and never only "sorry".
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
1 Confirming: Hi ______! Just confirming ______: ______________ starts at ______, so let's meet at ______ ______________. ______________.
2 Late: ______, I'm so sorry – ______________. I'm leaving ______________, so I'll be about ______ minutes late. Please ______________.
3 Answer: No problem! I'm ______________. ______________. Take your time.
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
at / in / on correct | 
The place is exact (next to…, outside…, on the … floor) | 
The late message says how many minutes and what to do | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one message before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write the three messages for a real arrangement of your own (50 words or more in total): confirm it, then say you're late with a reason, then write the kind answer.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)

 7R   Share   (4 min)

HomeworkWrite a fourth message for your partner's arrangement (25+ words): the next day, say thank you for the evening and suggest doing it again.

7.5  I prefer the cinema to TV
Function: talking about preferences   ·   Grammar: prefer A to B; prefer + -ing; I'd rather   ·   Pronunciation: I'd rather /aɪd ˈrɑːðə/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  Cinema or TV? Café or home? Football or films? Ask three classmates and write the class's favourite.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I prefer coffee to tea. | Most students prefer the café.
 | 
 | 
b  Choose for you: tea or coffee · summer or winter · the sea or the mountains · staying at home or going out
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What are they deciding?   A what film to see   B where to go after the film   C what to eat
b  Listen again. 1 What does Maya prefer?   2 What do they decide?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 7.9  After the film
Lina | That was a good film! Shall we eat something? There's a nice restaurant on the top floor.
Maya | Hmm. Honestly, I'd rather not eat in the shopping centre. I prefer small places to big restaurants.
Lina | Me too, actually. What about the café in Bab Touma?
Maya | The one with the garden? Yes! I prefer sitting outside, even in October.
Lina | Then let's go. Do you prefer walking or taking a taxi?
Maya | I'd rather walk – it's only fifteen minutes and the evening is beautiful.
Lina | Perfect. And what do you prefer – a full dinner or just mezze?
Maya | Mezze, definitely. I prefer eating a little of many things.
Lina | You're speaking my language. Let's go!

Language boxGrammar – preferencesprefer + noun **to** noun | prefer + **-ing** | I'd rather + **base verb**
I prefer small places to big restaurants. · She prefers tea to coffee. | I prefer sitting outside. · He prefers walking to taking a taxi. | I'd (would) rather walk. · I'd rather not eat here.
⚠ prefer A to B, not prefer A than B.  ⚠ I'd rather walk, not I'd rather to walk or I'd rather walking.
Negative: I'd rather not… (= a polite no): I'd rather not eat here.
Asking: Do you prefer X or Y? · Which do you prefer? · Would you rather walk or take a taxi?
Practise: Correct the mistakes.  1 I prefer the sea than the mountains.  2 I'd rather to stay at home.  3 She prefers to read than watching TV. (use -ing)  4 I'd rather not going out tonight.  5 Do you prefer tea or you prefer coffee?
Useful phrases: Honestly,… · Me too, actually. · …, definitely. · even in October · You're speaking my language!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 7.10 I'd rather /aɪd ˈrɑːðə/ – don't forget the /d/! Compare: I rather ✗ / I'd rather ✓. And prefer – the stress is on the second syllable: preFER. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. Decide together using the card. You must disagree at least once and use I'd rather at least twice. Then swap.
A | Shall we __________?
B | Honestly, I'd rather not __________. I prefer __________ to __________.
A | Me too, actually. What about __________?
B | Yes! I prefer __________.
A | Do you prefer __________ or __________?
B | I'd rather __________.
A | Perfect. Let's go!
Decide: a free Friday | Decide: a present for a colleague
the park or the Old City · walk or taxi · picnic or restaurant · morning or afternoon | a book or a plant · buy together or alone · give it at work or at a café · with a card or without
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: tick the phrases you hear.
☐ I prefer … to … | ☐ I prefer …-ing | ☐ I'd rather… | ☐ I'd rather not… | ☐ Which do you prefer? | ☐ Me too, actually.
 4   Your turn – this or that?   (9 min)
a  Ask your partner about eight choices and always ask why: tea/coffee · city/village · summer/winter · reading/watching · cooking/eating out · early/late · cat/dog · phone/laptop.
b  Now find three things where you are different. Can you explain why?
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class:  Fadi and I are the same about food – we both prefer eating at home to eating out. But he prefers winter to summer because he hates the heat, and I'd rather have forty degrees than cold hands!
Listener task: find someone with the same preferences as you.   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read Maya's post on the class page. Answer the questions.

I prefer working in the evening to working in the morning, and when I was a student I always studied after midnight.But my job doesn't agree with me! I open the pharmacy at eight, so I'd rather go to bed at eleven – and I usually can't. I lie in bed and think about everything.My sister Lama is the opposite. She prefers getting up at six and studying on her balcony with the first coffee of the day. She says the morning is "quiet and clean". For me, six o'clock is the middle of the night!So: are you a morning person or a night person? And more importantly – how do you wake up?
1  When did Maya study as a student?
2  What does Lama prefer?
3  What two questions does Maya ask the readers?
Writer's boxA preferences post: title as a question → your answer about yourself → a problem or contrast (But…) → another person who is different → a question to the readers.Use prefer + to / -ing and I'd rather – and give a reason for each.Ending with a question gets comments – that's how posts work!

prefer A to B (not than); prefer + -ing |

 6R   Share   (3 min)

 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write a post about your preferences (60 words or more): city or village? cooking or eating out? big family parties or quiet evenings? studying alone or with people? End with a question for your classmates.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)

7.6  What's on this week?
Function: talking about events   ·   Grammar: present simple for timetables and programmes   ·   Pronunciation: dates: the 24th of October
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What happens in your city? (concerts, matches, exhibitions, markets, weddings, religious events…) Where do you find out about them?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
There's a concert on Thursday. | Doors open at 7. Starts at 7.30.
 | 
 | 
b  Say the dates: 1/5 · 3/6 · 12/10 · 21/12 · 24/4
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What does Lama want to go to?   A a concert   B a film   C an exhibition
b  Listen again. 1 What time does it start?   2 How much is a student ticket?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 7.11  Calling Dar Al-Fann
Man | Dar Al-Fann Cultural Centre, good morning.
Lama | Good morning. I'd like to ask about the oud concert. When is it, please?
Man | It's on Thursday the 23rd. The doors open at seven and the concert starts at half past seven.
Lama | How long does it last?
Man | About ninety minutes, with a short break in the middle.
Lama | And how much are the tickets?
Man | Thirty thousand, but students pay fifteen. Bring your university card.
Lama | Great. Do I need to book?
Man | Yes, I recommend it – the hall is small and it fills up quickly. You can book here or on our page.
Lama | Is anything else on this month?
Man | Yes! There's a photography exhibition every day until the 30th – that one's free – and we show an old film every Sunday at six.
Lama | Perfect. Thank you very much!

Language boxGrammar – present simple for programmes and timetablesFor events and timetables we use the present simple, even about the future:The concert starts at 7.30. · The doors open at seven. · The exhibition finishes on the 30th. · The film begins at six.Compare: I'm going to the concert on Thursday. (my arrangement – 7.2)  /  The concert starts at 7.30. (the programme)Asking about events: What's on this week? · When is it? · What time does it start / finish? · How long does it last? · How much are the tickets? · Do I need to book? · Is there a discount for students?Dates: the 23rd of October / October the 23rd · the 1st, the 2nd, the 3rd, the 4th… the 21st, the 22nd, the 23rd, the 30thPractise: Complete with the present simple.  1 The match ______ (start) at four.  2 The museum ______ (not open) on Fridays.  3 What time ______ the concert ______ (finish)?  4 The exhibition ______ (last) two weeks.  5 The doors ______ (open) half an hour before.Useful phrases: I'd like to ask about… · How long does it last? · Do I need to book? · It fills up quickly. · Is anything else on?Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 7.12 Dates: the twenty-third · the first of May · October the thirtieth. The ordinal ending is the important part. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A phones about the event on the card; B has the information. Then swap.
Event card A – football match | Event card B – book fair
Al-Jaish v Al-Wahda · Friday 24th · gates open 3.00, match starts 4.00 · 90 minutes · tickets 25,000 (children 10,000) · no booking, buy at the gate | Damascus Book Fair · every day 10–8 until the 31st · free · talks by writers at 5 pm on Saturdays · café inside · bring a bag for your books!
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the date, the time and the price.
 4   Your turn – what's on in our city   (9 min)
a  Think of a real event you know about: a match, a wedding, a religious event, a market, a concert, a film, a school event. Tell your partner: what, when, where, how much, do you need to book?

 7R   Share   (4 min)
Put the posters on the wall. Walk around and write your name under the event you want to go to. Which event is the most popular?
HomeworkWrite a message to a friend (30+ words) about one event from a classmate's poster: say what it is, when and how much, and invite them.

7.7  Review & Mediate: Let's plan Saturday
Function: planning an outing from leaflets   ·   Recycling: Units 1–7   ·   Exam practice: A2 Key Reading Part 2, Writing Part 6, Speaking Part 2
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready – Unit 7 quiz   (6 min)
In teams. Correct the mistakes! 1 I can to swim.  2 She's good in cooking.  3 What you doing on Friday?  4 I prefer tea than coffee.  5 I'd rather to stay home.  6 The film is starting at eight every day.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Read the three leaflets.
🏛 CITADEL TOUR | 🌳 TISHREEN PARK DAY | 🎨 DAR AL-FANN
Guided tour of the Citadel, in English. | Open every day, 8 am – sunset. | Photography exhibition "Our Streets".
Saturdays at 10 am and 2 pm. | FREE. Big playground for children. | Every day until the 30th, 10 am – 8 pm. FREE.
2 hours. A lot of walking and stairs. | Boats on the lake: 15,000 for 30 min. | Sunday cinema at 6 pm: 10,000.
40,000 SYP · students 20,000. | Café near the main gate. | Small café inside.
Book on 011 221 3344. | Very busy on Fridays. | Closed on Fridays.
b  Books closed. Listen. Lina, Maya and Omar plan Saturday with Mr Lang. What do they decide?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 7.13  Planning Saturday
Lina | So, Mr Lang is free on Saturday. Shall we do the Citadel tour? It's in English.
Omar | Good idea. But it's two hours and there are a lot of stairs. He's nearly sixty.
Maya | He walks a lot, actually – he told me he walks every morning. I think he can do it.
Lina | OK. There are two tours: ten o'clock and two o'clock. Which do you prefer?
Omar | I'd rather go at ten. It's cooler, and then we have the whole afternoon.
Maya | Agreed. And after the tour? The exhibition at Dar Al-Fann is free, and it's very near the Citadel.
Lina | Perfect – and he's interested in photography. What about lunch?
Omar | There's a small café inside Dar Al-Fann, or we can go to Beit Al-Yasmin.
Maya | Beit Al-Yasmin, definitely. He loved the fattoush last time.
Lina | So: the tour at ten, lunch at Beit Al-Yasmin at half past twelve, the exhibition at two. Is that too much?
Omar | It's fine – everything is close. I'm booking the tour now. Forty thousand each.
Maya | And let's meet at the Citadel gate at a quarter to ten, to be safe.

Language box – planning together from informationUse the information, then decide:Saturdays at 10 and 2 → There are two tours. Which do you prefer?   Free → It doesn't cost anything.   Closed on Fridays → We can't go on Friday.Think about the person: He's nearly sixty. · He's interested in photography. · He loved the fattoush.Finish with a summary: So: the tour at ten, lunch at half past twelve, the exhibition at two.Useful phrases: Shall we…? · I'd rather… · Agreed. · …, definitely. · Is that too much? · Everything is close. · …, to be safe.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in groups of three. Plan one day for a visitor using the three leaflets. You must: make at least four suggestions · disagree politely at least once · agree a time and place to meet · give a summary at the end.
Choose your visitor | a) | b) | c)
Your group decides: | a family with two small children | two students with very little money | an older couple who walk slowly
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two groups perform their plan. Listener task: write the plan: time, place, cost. Is it good for that visitor?
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Your partner is visiting your city for one day. Using real places, plan the day together: morning, lunch, afternoon, evening. Think about your partner: what do they like? How much can they spend? Agree everything.
 4R   Report – mediation   (6 min)
Tell the class your partner's plan in your own words:  Fadi planned my day in Aleppo. In the morning we're visiting the citadel because the light is good early. Then we're having lunch at his uncle's restaurant – he says the kebab is the best in the city. The museum is closed on Tuesdays, so we're going to the souq instead.
Listener task: which day sounds the best? Which is the cheapest?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.

Unit 8  The Past
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
In this unit you will…8.1  talk about last weekend8.2  ask questions about past events8.3  say where you were and what you were doing8.4  talk about important events in your life8.5  tell a story in order8.6  react to good news and bad news8.7  Review & Mediate: tell the class a friend's news

Look back!In Units 1–7 you learned some past forms as phrases: I bought this last week · It was delicious · I could draw at school · He said yes.In this unit you learn how the past works – so you can say anything about yesterday, last week or twenty years ago.

8.1  What did you do at the weekend?
Function: talking about last weekend   ·   Grammar: past simple (+ / –), regular and irregular   ·   Pronunciation: -ed endings /t/ /d/ /ɪd/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What do people do at the weekend in your city? Your teacher writes the class's ideas on the board.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I visited my parents. | Last weekend I went to Bludan.
 | 
 | 
b  Say these in the past: _visit → ______ · watch → ______ · study → ______ · stop → _______   And these are different: _go → ______ · have → ______ · see → ______ · eat → _______
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Where did Rami go at the weekend?   A to Homs   B to Maaloula   C nowhere
b  Listen again. 1 What did Omar do on Friday?   2 Why didn't Rami rest?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 8.1  Monday morning at the centre
Omar | You look tired, Rami! What did you do at the weekend?
Rami | I went to Homs to see my family. I arrived on Thursday night and I came back yesterday evening.
Omar | Nice! Did you rest?
Rami | Rest? No, I didn't! On Friday my mother cooked for twenty people. My sister and her children came, my uncle came…
Omar | Ha! And on Saturday?
Rami | On Saturday I painted my parents' kitchen. My father wanted it blue, my mother wanted it white, so we painted it… white and blue.
Omar | Of course you did. What about you – did you sleep all weekend?
Rami | What did YOU do, Omar?
Omar | On Friday I cooked for the photography club – they loved the kibbeh. Then on Saturday we walked in the Old City and took photos for four hours.
Rami | Four hours! Did you get any good ones?
Omar | I took two hundred photos and I liked three. That's photography!

Language boxGrammar – past simple (+ / –)Regular verbs: + -ed | Irregular verbs: learn them! | Negative: didn't + base verb
cook → cooked · visit → visited · walk → walked
live → lived · arrive → arrived (just -d)
study → studied · stop → stopped | go → went · have → had · see → saw · eat → ate
come → came · take → took · buy → bought · say → said
get → got · make → made · do → did · be → was / were | I didn't rest. · She didn't go.
⚠ After didn't, use the base verb: didn't go, not didn't went.
Past time words: yesterday · last night / week / month / year · two days ago · on Friday · in 2019
Same for everybody! I / you / he / she / we / they cooked. No -s in the past. 😀
Practise: Put the verb in the past simple.  1 I ______ (visit) my aunt on Friday.  2 We ______ (go) to the park.  3 She ______ (not / come) to the party.  4 They ______ (take) a lot of photos.  5 He ______ (study) all weekend.  6 I ______ (not / like) the film.
Useful phrases: You look tired! · Nice! · Of course you did. · What about you? · That's photography!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 8.2 -ed has three sounds: /t/ walked, cooked, stopped · /d/ lived, arrived, played · /ɪd/ (an extra syllable!) wanted, visited, painted. Listen and repeat. ⚠ Only verbs ending in t or d get /ɪd/.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. Talk about the weekend on the card. Then swap.
A | You look tired! What did you do at the weekend?
B | I __________. I __________ on __________ and I __________.
A | Nice! Did you __________?
B | No, I didn't! On __________ I __________.
A | And on __________?
B | I __________. What about you?
Weekend card A | Weekend card B
Thu: travelled to Latakia · Fri: swam in the sea, ate fish with cousins · Sat: helped uncle in his shop, came back at night · didn't rest! | Fri: cleaned the whole flat, washed all the clothes · Sat: studied English for three hours, watched two films · didn't go out at all
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform without the book. Listener task: write four past verbs you hear. Are they regular or irregular?
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Tell your partner about your real weekend (or last Friday). Say at least five things, and include one thing you didn't do (I didn't study / I didn't go out). Then change partners and tell it again – faster.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner's weekend:  Hiba went to her grandmother's house in Dummar on Friday. She helped her cook and they ate together. On Saturday she studied for her exam, but she didn't finish. She says she needs another weekend!
Listener task: who had the busiest weekend? Who really rested?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read Omar's message to his mother in Aleppo. Answer the questions.
Hi Mum! Sorry I didn't call on Friday – it was a busy weekend.On Friday I cooked for the English club: fattoush, kibbeh and rice. Twelve people came to our flat! They finished everything and Lina asked for the kibbeh recipe – I told her it's your recipe, of course.On Saturday morning we walked in the Old City with the photography club. I took two hundred photos (and I liked three!). In the afternoon I slept for two hours, and in the evening Rami and I watched the match.I didn't clean the flat, I didn't wash my clothes and I didn't study my English homework. But I was happy!How was your weekend? Say hello to Dad.Omar
1  How many people came to the flat?
2  What did Omar do on Saturday afternoon?
3  Name two things he didn't do.
Writer's boxA weekend message: greeting (+ sorry, if you didn't call!) → Friday → Saturday (morning / afternoon / evening) → what you didn't do → a question → greetings to the family → your name.Start some sentences with the time: On Friday… In the afternoon… – but not every sentence.Three negatives together (I didn't…, I didn't…, I didn't…) are a nice way to end a busy weekend!
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Hi ______! Sorry I ______________ – it was a ______ weekend.   On Friday I ______________. ______________. On Saturday morning ______________. In the afternoon I ______________, and in the evening ______________.   I didn't ______________ and I didn't ______________. But ______________!   How was your weekend? ______________.   ______
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
Past simple: -ed or the irregular form | 
After didn't → base verb (not didn't went) | 
All parts: the days in order, what you didn't do, a question | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Two or three students read one sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write a message to a family member or friend about your real weekend (60 words or more). Include three things you did and two things you didn't do.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
★ That sounds fun!   ✓ Can you add who you went with?   ? Did you…?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two messages are read aloud. Whose weekend was the most relaxing? Whose was the hardest work?
HomeworkWrite the reply from your partner's family member (25+ words): tell them about your weekend and ask one past question (Did you…? What did you…?).

8.2  Where did you go?
Function: asking about past events   ·   Grammar: past simple questions (Did you…? Where did you…?)   ·   Pronunciation: did you /dɪdʒə/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  Your friend comes back from a holiday. What do you ask? Make a class list of six questions.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Where did you go? | How was your trip?
 | 
 | 
b  Make questions: (you / go / where?) · (you / stay / how long?) · (you / like / it?) · (you / go / who with?)
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Where did Maya go?   A to Latakia   B to Tartus   C to Aleppo
b  Listen again. 1 Who did she go with?   2 What didn't she like?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 8.3  Back from the coast
Lina | Maya! You're brown! Where did you go?
Maya | To Latakia, for my father's birthday.
Lina | Lovely! Who did you go with?
Maya | With my sister Lama. We took the Thursday bus.
Lina | How long did you stay?
Maya | Four days. We came back on Monday morning.
Lina | What did you do there?
Maya | Everything and nothing! We swam every morning, we ate too much fish, and I slept nine hours a night.
Lina | That's a real holiday. Did you see your old school friends?
Maya | Yes, I did. We met at a café on Saturday – I didn't see some of them for three years!
Lina | And the journey? Was it OK?
Maya | The journey home was terrible. The bus didn't leave on time and it took five hours. But I don't care – look at my photos!

Language boxGrammar – past simple questionsYes / no questions | Wh- questions | Short answers
Did you rest? · Did she see them? | Where did you go? · Who did you go with? · How long did you stay? · What did you do? | Yes, I did. / No, I didn't.
Yes, she did. / No, she didn't.
⚠ After did, the verb is the base form: Did you go?, not Did you went?  ·  Where did she buy it?, not Where did she bought it?
But with be there is no did: Was it OK? Were you tired? (more in 8.3)
Common questions after a trip: Where did you go? · Who did you go with? · How did you travel? · How long did you stay? · Where did you stay? · What did you do? · Did you like it? · How was the journey?
Practise: Make past questions.  1 (where / you / go / last summer?)  2 (who / she / meet?)  3 (you / like / the film?)  4 (how long / they / stay?)  5 (what / he / buy?)
Useful phrases: You're brown! · Lovely! · Everything and nothing! · That's a real holiday. · I don't care – look at my photos!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 8.4 did you sounds like /dɪdʒə/: Where did‿you go? · What did‿you do? · Did‿you like it? Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. B went on the trip on the card. A asks six questions. Then swap.
A | Where did you go?
B | To __________.
A | Who did you go with?
B | With __________.
A | How long did you stay?
B | __________.
A | What did you do there?
B | We __________ and we __________.
A | Did you __________?
B | Yes, I did. / No, I didn't.
Trip card A | Trip card B
Palmyra · with two cousins · three days · walked in the old city, took photos, drank tea with a family there · didn't swim (no sea!) · the journey: long and hot | Beirut · with your brother · five days · saw the sea, visited a friend, bought presents, ate in a famous restaurant · didn't sleep much · the journey: easy, two hours
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the six questions you hear. Then ask your partner one of them about a real trip.
 4   Your turn – the interview   (9 min)
Think of a real trip you made (a holiday, a visit to family, a work trip, a day out – even a small one). Your partner interviews you with at least eight past questions and writes short notes. Then swap. Then find a new partner and ask about their trip.
Where? | When? | Who with? | How? | How long? | Best thing? | Problem?
 |  |  |  |  |  | 
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner's trip:  Tareq went to Suwayda for his cousin's wedding. He travelled by bus with his wife and they stayed two nights. The best thing was the food – he ate mansaf three times! The problem was the journey home: the bus didn't come and they waited two hours.
Listener task: ask one more question about the trip you liked best.   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read Lina's interview report for the class page. Answer the questions.
Our class holidays: Maya in LatakiaLast week Maya went to Latakia with her sister Lama. It was her father's birthday, and the family wanted everyone together.They took the Thursday bus and stayed four days. "We swam every morning and we ate too much fish," Maya told me. She slept nine hours every night – at home she sleeps five!On Saturday she met her old school friends at a café. She didn't see some of them for three years, so they talked until midnight.The only problem was the journey home. The bus didn't leave on time and it took five hours instead of three. But Maya says it was worth it: "Look at my photos!"
1  Why did the family want everyone together?
2  How is Maya's sleep different at home?
3  How long did the journey home take?
Writer's boxAn interview report: write about the person as he / she, not I → what, where, when, who with → the details → one problem → a short quote at the end.Use quotation marks for the person's own words: "We swam every morning," Maya told me.The past simple is for the story; the present simple is for things that are still true: at home she sleeps five hours.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Our class holidays: ______ in ______________   Last ______ ______ went to ______________ with ______________. ______________.   They ______________ and stayed ______. "______________," ______ told me. ______________.   The only problem was ______________. ______________.   But ______ says it was worth it: "______________."
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
Past simple for the story (he / she – no -s!) | 
Questions and negatives with did / didn't + base verb | 
All parts: where, who with, details, a problem, a quote | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write an interview report about your partner's trip from Stage 4 (60 words or more). Use your notes and add one quote from them.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read the report about you. ★ Good report!  ✓ Small correction: I stayed three days, not two.  ? Why didn't you write about…?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Put the reports together: this is our Class 4B Travel Page. Which trip do you want to make?
HomeworkWrite five questions you want to ask another classmate about their trip, and give them to that person. Answer the five questions you receive (30+ words).

8.3  Where were you?
Function: saying where you were   ·   Grammar: was / were; ago, last   ·   Pronunciation: weak was /wəz/ and were /wə/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  Where were you yesterday at these times? 8 am · 1 pm · 6 pm · 10 pm. Tell the class.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I was at work. | Yesterday at six I was at home.
 | 
 | 
b  was or were?  I ______ · you ______ · he ______ · we ______ · they ______ · the shop ______ · my parents ______
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What is the problem?   A someone is ill   B something is missing   C someone is late
b  Listen again. 1 Where was Omar at seven?   2 Who had the key?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 8.5  The missing camera
Sara | Omar, the club camera isn't in the cupboard! Where is it?
Omar | What? It was there on Monday – I put it back myself.
Sara | Were you here on Tuesday?
Omar | No, I wasn't. I was at work until eight. Why – was the cupboard open?
Sara | Yes, it was. And the key wasn't in Mr Bassam's office.
Omar | Hmm. Who was in the room on Tuesday evening?
Sara | The cooking club. They were here until nine.
Omar | Then let's ask them. Wait – two weeks ago Lina borrowed the camera for her niece's birthday…
Sara | And did she bring it back?
Omar | I think so. But she wasn't at the club last week, was she?
Sara | No, she wasn't! She was ill. I'll call her.
Omar | (two minutes later) Well?
Sara | The camera is in her car. It was there for two weeks!

Language boxGrammar – was / were (the past of be)+ | – | ?
I / he / she / it was at home. | I wasn't (was not) here. | Was she ill? – Yes, she was. / No, she wasn't.
You / we / they were here. | They weren't (were not) late. | Were you here on Tuesday? – Yes, I was. / No, I wasn't.
⚠ No did with was / were: Were you at home?, not Did you were at home?  And: She was ill, not She did ill.
ago and last: two weeks ago · ten minutes ago · last night · last Tuesday · last year  (⚠ not the last year, not before two weeks)
Practise: Complete with was, were, wasn't or weren't.  1 I ______ at the club yesterday – I was at home.  2 ______ you at work on Sunday?  3 They ______ very happy with the photos.  4 The shop ______ open, so we went home.  5 Where ______ your parents last night?
Useful phrases: Where is it? · I put it back myself. · Who was in the room? · Wait – … · I think so. · Well? · It was there for two weeks!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 8.6 was /wəz/ and were /wə/ are weak in sentences: I was‿at home /aɪ wəz ət/ · They were‿here. But in short answers they are strong: Yes, I was. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. Something is missing! A asks, B answers using the card. Then swap.
A | __________ isn't __________! Where is it?
B | It was __________ on __________.
A | Were you here on __________?
B | No, I wasn't. I was __________.
A | Who was __________?
B | __________ was / were __________.
A | Let's ask them!
Card A – the office projector | Card B – your brother's bicycle
last seen: Sunday, meeting room · you: at a client's office all Monday · who was there: the training group, until six | last seen: Friday, outside the building · you: at your sister's house all day Saturday · who was there: your cousin, he borrowed it
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write where the thing was and who had it.
 4   Your turn – the alibi game   (9 min)
Something happened yesterday evening at eight o'clock! (Your teacher decides: all the sugar in the staff room disappeared…)
a  In pairs, agree your alibi: you were together. Where were you? What were you doing? Who else was there? Agree every detail – you have three minutes!
b  Now the pairs split. Another pair interviews you separately: Where were you at eight? Who were you with? What were you wearing? Was the café busy? If your two stories are different, you're guilty!
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class:  Fadi said they were at the café in Shaalan, but Rasha said they were at the café in Mezzeh. Fadi said the café was busy and Rasha said it was empty. They're guilty!
Listener task: which pair has the best alibi? Who is guilty?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read Sara's report to Mr Bassam. Answer the questions.
REPORT – THE CLUB CAMERA   From: Sara Hamdan   Date: 21 OctoberOn Tuesday evening the club camera wasn't in the cupboard. The cupboard was open and the key wasn't in your office.Omar wasn't at the centre on Tuesday – he was at work until eight. The cooking club was in the room until nine, but nobody there saw the camera.Then Omar remembered: two weeks ago Lina borrowed the camera for her niece's birthday. Lina wasn't at the club last week because she was ill, so nobody asked her.I called her this morning. The camera was in her car – it was there for two weeks! She is very sorry and she is bringing it back tomorrow.My suggestion: we need a book for borrowing: name, date and signature.
1  Where was the key?
2  Why didn't anybody ask Lina?
3  What does Sara suggest?
Writer's boxA short report: title + from + date → the problem (what wasn't there) → who was and wasn't there → what you found out → the answer → your suggestion.A report is factual and calm: no "It was terrible!" – just what happened.Use was / were / wasn't / weren't for the situation and the past simple for the actions.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
REPORT – ______________   From: ______________   Date: ______________   On ______________ the ______________ wasn't ______________. ______________ was ______________.   ______ wasn't ______________ – he / she was ______________. ______________ were ______________ until ______.   Then ______________. ______________.   My suggestion: ______________.
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
was / were (no did with be!) | 
wasn't / weren't for the negatives | 
All parts: problem, who was there, what you found, suggestion | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write a short report about a real or invented problem: something missing at work, a delivery that didn't arrive, a room that was double-booked, a machine that wasn't working (60 words or more). End with a suggestion.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's report as the manager. ★ Clear report!  ✓ Can you add the exact time?  ? Who had the key?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two reports are read aloud. The class says: is the suggestion a good one?
HomeworkWrite the manager's reply to your partner's report (25+ words): thank them, say what you will do, and accept or change their suggestion.

8.4  I was born in Homs
Function: talking about life events   ·   Grammar: past simple + dates and years; was born   ·   Pronunciation: years: 1995, 2003, 2019
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What are the important years in a person's life? (born, school, university, first job, marriage, children, moving…) Make a class list.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I was born in 1996. | 2014 – started university
 | 
 | 
b  Say the years: 1987 · 1995 · 2001 · 2010 · 2019 · 2026
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Who is Lina talking to?   A a new colleague   B a job interviewer   C her teacher
b  Listen again. 1 When did she move to Damascus?   2 Why did she change jobs in 2022?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 8.7  A chat at the centre party
Hani | So, Lina, are you from Damascus?
Lina | No, I'm not, actually. I was born in Homs. My family moved here in 2005, when I was seven.
Hani | Ah! So you grew up here.
Lina | Yes. I went to school in Shaalan, and then I studied accounting at Damascus University. I finished in 2019.
Hani | And did you start work immediately?
Lina | Almost. I worked in a small shop for a year – it wasn't my dream job, but I learned a lot. Then in 2020 I got a job at a trading company.
Hani | Are you still there?
Lina | No. In 2022 the company closed, so I moved to Medica Trading. I've been there since then.
Hani | And the English classes?
Lina | I started in September. My company works with foreign suppliers now, so English is important. And honestly – I wanted to do something for myself.
Hani | That's the best reason.

Language boxGrammar – talking about your lifeI was born in Homs / in 1998 / on 3 May.  ⚠ Always was born – not I born, not I am born.Prepositions with time | Examples
in + year, month, season | in 2005 · in September · in the summer
on + day, date | on Friday · on 3 May 2005
at + time | at six o'clock
when + a past sentence | when I was seven · when I finished school
for + a period | I worked there for a year.
then / after that / so | Then in 2020 I got a job. · The company closed, so I moved.
Life verbs: be born · grow up · go to school · study · finish / leave school · start work · get a job · change jobs · move (to) · get married · have children · retire
Practise: Complete with in, on, when, for or was born.  1 I ______ in Latakia.  2 We moved to Damascus ______ 2015.  3 My sister got married ______ 12 June.  4 I lived in Aleppo ______ three years.  5 I started work ______ I finished university.
Useful phrases: …, actually. · So you grew up here. · Almost. · It wasn't my dream job, but… · since then · honestly · That's the best reason.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 8.8 Years: 1987 = nineteen eighty-seven · 1995 = nineteen ninety-five · 2001 = two thousand and one · 2019 = twenty nineteen (or two thousand and nineteen) · 2026 = twenty twenty-six. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. B is the person on the card. A asks about their life. Then swap.
A | Are you from __________?
B | No, actually. I was born in __________. My family moved to __________ in __________.
A | So you grew up there. Where did you go to school?
B | __________. Then I studied __________ at __________.
A | And did you start work immediately?
B | I worked __________ for __________. Then in __________ I __________.
A | And now?
B | Now I __________.
Life card A | Life card B
born in Deir ez-Zor, 1990 · family moved to Damascus 1999 · school in Mezzeh · studied pharmacy · worked in a hospital 2 years · 2016 opened own pharmacy · married, two sons | born in Damascus, 1985 · grew up in Dummar · studied engineering in Aleppo · worked in Dubai 2010–2018 · came back in 2018 · now builds schools · not married
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write four years you hear and what happened in each.
 4   Your turn – your timeline   (9 min)
Draw your own timeline with five or six important years (you can invent or leave things out – it's your choice what you share). Explain it to your partner. They ask three questions. Then swap. Then change partners and tell your life again, shorter and faster.

What happened |  |  |  |  |

Listener task: who travelled the most? Who changed jobs the most?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the short biography on the centre's wall. Answer the questions.
OUR TEACHERS: Huda SalehMs Huda was born in Damascus in 1979 and grew up in the Old City, near Bab Touma. She went to school there and studied English Literature at Damascus University.She finished in 2001 and started teaching in a secondary school the same year. "My first class had fifty-two students," she says. "I learned everything in that room."In 2008 she got a diploma in teaching English to adults, and in 2011 she joined Al-Yasmin Language Centre. She taught here for six years. Then in 2017 she moved to Dubai with her family and worked at an international school.She came back to Damascus in 2023 and she is now the head teacher of our adult programme. In her free time she reads poetry and makes a very good lentil soup.
1  Where did Ms Huda grow up?
2  What happened in 2017?
3  What does she do in her free time?
Writer's boxA short biography: born + grew up → education → first job (+ a quote) → the next steps in order (in 2008… then in 2017…) → now (present simple!) → one personal detail at the end.Use the past simple for the life story and the present simple for now: She is now the head teacher.Years go in order – don't jump backwards and forwards.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)

"______________," he / she says.   In ______ he / she ______________, and in ______ he / she ______________. Then in ______ ______________.   He / She is now ______________. In his / her free time ______________.
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters (names, places, months) and full stops | 
was born (not born alone) | 
in + year, on + date | 
Past for the story, present for now; years in order | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one sentence before and after the fix.

Write a short biography (60 words or more) of your partner (from Stage 4), a person in your family, or a person you admire in your city.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)

8.5  First… then… in the end
Function: telling a story   ·   Grammar: sequencers (first, then, after that, in the end)   ·   Pronunciation: pausing after sequencers
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  Think of a day that started badly and ended well (or the opposite!). What words do we use to tell a story in order?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
First we took the bus… | In the end, everything was fine.
 | 
 | 
b  Put in order: In the end · First · After that · Then · Finally
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. How did the day end?   A badly   B well   C nobody knows
b  Listen again. 1 What was the first problem?   2 Who helped them?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 8.9  The worst (best) day out
Lama | How was your trip to Maaloula?
Maya | Oh, it was a disaster! …But a beautiful disaster.
Lama | What happened?
Maya | Well, first we arrived at the garage at eight and the bus wasn't there. We waited for an hour.
Lama | An hour!
Maya | Then, when we finally arrived, it started to rain – and nobody had an umbrella. After that, Omar dropped his camera in the mud.
Lama | Oh no! Was it broken?
Maya | No, it was fine. But we were wet and cold and hungry, and all the restaurants were full.
Lama | So what did you do?
Maya | An old woman saw us outside her door and she invited us in! She gave us tea and bread and olives, and we sat in her kitchen for two hours.
Lama | That's amazing.
Maya | In the end, it was the best day of the month. We didn't see the monastery – but we made a new friend. Omar took a photo of her, and it's the best photo he took all year.

Language boxGrammar – telling a story in orderFirst, … → Then … → After that, … → Suddenly, … → In the end, … / Finally, …First we arrived at eight. Then it started to rain. After that, Omar dropped his camera. In the end, it was the best day of the month.Making a story interesting: It was a disaster! · Oh no! · What happened? · Suddenly… · You won't believe it… · And then?when + past: when we finally arrived, it started to rain · when I was seven⚠ In the end = finally, after everything (a story).  At the end = at the last part of something: at the end of the film.Practise: Put the story in order.  ___ After that, we walked to the café.  ___ First, I met Hiba at the bus stop.  ___ In the end, we were very late – but it was a great evening.  ___ Then we waited twenty minutes for the service.  ___ Suddenly, it started to rain.Useful phrases: What happened? · Oh no! · Was it broken? · So what did you do? · That's amazing. · You won't believe it!Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 8.10 Pause after the sequencer – it helps the listener: First, || we arrived at eight. · In the end, || it was the best day. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. Tell the story from the pictures on the card. Use all five sequencers. Your partner reacts (Oh no! What happened? And then?). Then swap.
Story card A – pictures | Story card B – pictures
1 A man leaves home with a big cake. | 1 A woman waits at the airport with flowers.
2 He gets on a crowded minibus. | 2 The plane arrives – no sister.
3 The minibus stops suddenly. The cake falls. | 3 She phones; her sister is at the other airport gate.
4 Everybody helps him. | 4 They run to each other and the flowers fall.
5 He arrives at the party with half a cake – everybody laughs. | 5 A child gives the flowers back; everybody is happy.
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform their story. Listener task: tick the sequencers you hear. Which story was the funniest?
☐ First | ☐ Then | ☐ After that | ☐ Suddenly | ☐ In the end
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Tell your partner about a real day that didn't go as you planned – a journey, a day at work, a family event, a day at school. Use all five sequencers. Your partner must react at least three times and ask two questions. Then swap, and then change partners and tell it again.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class your partner's story:  Tareq had a terrible morning last Sunday. First his alarm didn't ring. Then the water wasn't working, so he couldn't shower. After that, he ran to the bus and it left without him. In the end he arrived at the bank at ten – and the manager was late too!
Listener task: whose story was the worst? Whose had the best ending?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Look at the four pictures and read Maya's story. Answer the questions.
1 | 2 | 3 | 4
Two friends get on a bus. It's sunny. They're smiling. | They arrive. It's raining hard. They have no umbrella. | A man drops his camera in the mud. They look at the restaurants – all full. | An old woman invites them into her kitchen. Tea, bread, olives. Everybody is laughing.

A day in MaaloulaLast Friday my friends and I took the bus to Maaloula. The morning was beautiful and we were very happy.But when we arrived, it started to rain and nobody had an umbrella. After that, my friend Omar dropped his camera in the mud, and all the restaurants were full. We were wet, cold and hungry.Then an old woman saw us outside her house. She invited us in and she gave us tea, bread and olives. We sat in her warm kitchen for two hours and she told us about the village.In the end, we didn't see the famous monastery – but it was the best day of the month.
1  How many paragraphs are there?
2  Which paragraph has the problems?
3  How does the writer make the ending interesting?
Writer's boxA picture story (A2 Key Writing Part 7) – write 35 words or more about the pictures:Picture 1 = the beginning (who, where, when) → Pictures 2–3 = the problem → Picture 4 = the end.Use the past simple, the sequencers, and but / so / because to join sentences.A good ending is a surprise or a feeling: …but it was the best day of the month.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)

 6P   Check together   (5 min)

Sequencers: First / Then / After that / In the end | 
The story matches all the pictures; 35+ words | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely – A2 Key Writing Part 7   (12 min)
Write the story of your real day from Stage 4 (60 words or more) – or invent a story from these three pictures: 1 A boy finds a wallet in the street. 2 He runs after a woman. 3 The woman gives him a box of sweets.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)

8.6  Really? That's great!
Function: reacting to news   ·   Grammar: exclamations; That's + adjective   ·   Pronunciation: wide intonation in reactions
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  Good news and bad news – what do you say in each case? Make two lists on the board.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
That's great news! | Congratulations! 🎉
 | 
 | 
b  Good news or bad news? I'm sorry to hear that. · Congratulations! · Oh no! · That's wonderful! · Poor you! · Well done!
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. How many pieces of news are there?   A two   B three   C four
b  Listen again. 1 What happened to Hiba?   2 What is Fadi's bad news?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 8.11  Before the lesson
Hiba | Everyone – I passed my exam! I got 92!
Rasha | Really? That's wonderful! Congratulations, Hiba!
Tareq | Well done! You studied so hard.
Hiba | Thank you! I couldn't sleep last night – and then the result came at seven this morning.
Fadi | That's great news. I need some good news today, honestly.
Rasha | Why? What happened?
Fadi | My car broke down on the highway yesterday. I waited two hours and it cost me a fortune.
Tareq | Oh no! I'm sorry to hear that. Is it OK now?
Fadi | It's in the garage. They said Thursday.
Hiba | Poor you! Listen – I'm buying sweets for everyone today, so at least you get baklava.
Fadi | Ha! Now that IS good news.
Rasha | And my news: my sister had a baby girl on Sunday!
All | Congratulations! That's lovely! Mabrouk!

Language boxReacting to news😀 Good news | 🙁 Bad news | Showing interest
Really? That's wonderful / great / lovely! · Congratulations! · Well done! · That's great news! · I'm so happy for you! | Oh no! · I'm sorry to hear that. · Poor you! · That's terrible. · What a shame! | Really? · What happened? · And then? · Is it OK now? · Tell me everything!
Congratulations! = for success (an exam, a wedding, a baby, a new job).  Well done! = for something the person worked hard for.
⚠ Congratulations always has an s. ⚠ I'm sorry to hear that – not I'm sorry for that.
That's + adjective: That's amazing / terrible / strange / interesting.  What a + noun: What a shame! What a surprise!
Practise: Choose a reaction.  1 "I got the job!" → ______  2 "My grandmother is in hospital." → ______  3 "We're getting married in June!" → ______  4 "I lost my phone." → ______  5 "I finished the book – 400 pages!" → ______
Useful phrases: I need some good news today. · It cost me a fortune. · They said Thursday. · at least… · Now that IS good news.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 8.12 Reactions need a big voice – start high and fall: REALly? That's WONderful! A flat voice sounds bored or even rude. Listen and repeat – then say them again, bigger!
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A reads a piece of news from the card. B reacts and asks two questions. Then swap.
A | I've got some news – __________!
B | Really? That's __________! __________!
A | Thank you! __________.
B | What happened exactly?
A | __________.
B | __________?
News card A | News card B
😀 Your brother got a job in Dubai. | 😀 You passed your driving test (third time!).
🙁 Your laptop stopped working before an exam. | 🙁 Your flight was cancelled and you missed a wedding.
😀 Your neighbours had twins. | 🙁 Someone took your bicycle from outside the building.
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform – with big voices! Listener task: tick the reactions you hear. Did they sound real?
☐ Really? | ☐ That's wonderful! | ☐ Congratulations! | ☐ Oh no! | ☐ I'm sorry to hear that. | ☐ Poor you!
 4   Your turn – the news circle   (9 min)
a  Write two pieces of news on a piece of paper: one real (something that happened to you or your family recently) and one invented (something big and surprising!).
b  Walk around. Tell your news to four classmates. React to their news, and ask two questions each time. At the end, guess: which of your partner's news was invented?
 4R   Report   (6 min)

LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the two replies. Answer the questions.
1 · Rasha → HibaHiba!! Congratulations!! 🎉 92 is amazing – and you did it with a full-time job. I know how hard you studied: all those nights in the café with your books. Your mother must be so proud. Let's celebrate properly on Thursday – and this time I'm paying!2 · Tareq → FadiFadi, I'm really sorry to hear about the car. Two hours on the highway in this weather – what a terrible day. I hope the garage finishes it quickly and that it isn't too expensive. If you need a lift to work before Thursday, just call me – I pass your building every morning at a quarter to eight. Don't worry about asking; it's no problem at all.
1  What does Rasha remember about Hiba's studying?
2  What does Tareq offer?
3  Which reply is longer – and why?
Writer's boxA reply to good news: react (Congratulations!) → say why it's special (something you remember about them) → a plan to celebrate.A reply to bad news: react (I'm sorry to hear that) → show you understand → hope for the future → an offer of help → don't worry about asking.For bad news, the offer of help is the most important part. A short "Sorry!" isn't enough.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Good news reply: ______! Congratulations! ______________ is amazing – and ______________. I know ______________. Let's ______________!
Bad news reply: ______, I'm really sorry to hear about ______________. ______________ – what a ______________. I hope ______________. If you need ______________, just ______________. Don't worry about asking.
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
The right reaction for good / bad news | 
Something personal (what you remember or understand) | 
A plan to celebrate OR a real offer of help | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)

 7   Write freely   (12 min)

 7P   Respond   (5 min)

 7R   Share   (4 min)

HomeworkWrite a reply to the reply (25+ words): thank your classmate and accept their offer or their invitation.

8.7  Review & Mediate: Sami says he…
Function: telling the class a friend's news   ·   Recycling: Units 1–8   ·   Exam practice: A2 Key Writing Part 7 (picture story), Reading Part 3, Listening Part 5
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready – Unit 8 quiz   (6 min)
In teams. Correct the mistakes! 1 I go to Homs last week.  2 Did you went to the party?  3 She didn't came.  4 Where you were yesterday?  5 I born in 1995.  6 I'm sorry for that.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Read the message Omar received.
From Sami (12 messages!)Omar!! You won't believe my week. On Sunday I had my interview at the new company – the one in Mezzeh. There were four people in the room and I was so nervous I forgot my own phone number 😂Anyway, they called me on Tuesday… I GOT THE JOB! I start on the first of November. More money, and it's fifteen minutes from my house!The bad news: on Wednesday I went to tell my parents and my old car broke down – again. The mechanic says it needs a new engine. So now I have a new job and no car 🚗💀Tell everyone at the club. And tell Lina I'm sorry – I can't come to the dinner on Friday because I'm moving my things to the new office. Next time, I promise!
b  Books closed. Listen. Omar tells the class. What two things does he say about Sami's car?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 8.13  Telling the class
Omar | Everyone, I've got news from Sami – you remember Sami, he was in our class last year.
Lina | Of course! How is he?
Omar | He had an interview on Sunday at a company in Mezzeh, and on Tuesday they called him – he got the job!
Rasha | That's wonderful! Congratulations to him!
Omar | He starts on the first of November. More money, and it's only fifteen minutes from his house.
Lina | He was so worried about that interview.
Omar | He says he was so nervous he forgot his own phone number!
Hiba | Ha! And is everything else OK?
Omar | Not everything. On Wednesday his car broke down again – it needs a new engine. So he has a new job and no car.
Hiba | Oh no! Poor Sami.
Omar | And Lina – he's sorry, but he can't come to the dinner on Friday. He's moving his things to the new office.
Lina | Never mind. Let's all call him tonight and say congratulations.

Language box – telling someone else's newsChange I to he / she and keep the past simple:"I had my interview on Sunday." → He had his interview on Sunday.   "I got the job!" → He got the job!   "My car broke down." → His car broke down.For things that are still true, use the present: "I start on the first of November." → He starts on the first of November.Introduce the news: I've got news from… · He says… · He told me…Pass on a message: He's sorry, but he can't come. · He says hello to everyone.⚠ He says + -s (Unit 2!) and He said (past) are both correct – but don't mix: He say.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A reads the message on the card (don't show it!). B listens and then tells it back as news: She says she… Then swap.
Message card A | Message card B
"I finished my thesis on Monday! I worked on it for two years." | "We moved to the new flat on Saturday. The lift doesn't work!"
"The defence is on the 20th and I'm terrified." | "My husband carried everything up five floors – twice."
"Can you all come? It's at the university at 10." | "Come and see it on Friday – but bring strong legs!"
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform the "telling back". Listener task: was everything correct? What did they forget?
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Think of real news from someone in your life (family, a friend, a colleague) from the last month – good or bad. Tell your partner. They react, ask three questions, and then tell you their news. Change partners and tell your partner's news to the new person – as he / she.
 4R   Report – mediation   (6 min)
Tell the class the news you heard from your second partner about their first partner:  Hiba told me about Rasha's news. Rasha's sister had a baby girl on Sunday in Jaramana. The baby's name is Jouri. Rasha visited them on Monday and she says the baby looks like her father!
Listener task: did the news change on the way? That's how rumours start!   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING AND UNIT CHECK
 5   Read – exam practice (A2 Key Reading Part 3)   (7 min)
Read the text and choose the correct answer A, B or C.
The day I became a photographer – by Omar DarwishI bought my first camera eight years ago, in a second-hand shop in Aleppo. It was old and heavy and the man wanted too much money for it, but my mother said, "Buy it. You look at the world like a photographer." I was twenty-seven and I knew nothing about photography.For two years the camera stayed in its bag. Then in 2020 I moved to Damascus for work, and I didn't know anybody here. At the weekend the flat was very quiet, so I started walking in the Old City with the camera. I took terrible photos – dark, grey, boring. But I met people: a man who makes wooden boxes, a woman who sells spices, three boys who wanted to be in every picture.Last year I joined the photography club at my English centre. In the first lesson the teacher looked at my photos and said nothing for a long time. Then she said, "Your photos of buildings are bad. Your photos of people are good. Why?" I said I don't know. She said, "Because you like people, not buildings. Now stop photographing buildings."It was the best advice I ever got.

1  Why did Omar buy the camera?   A It was cheap.   B His mother encouraged him.   C He studied photography.
2  What did he do with the camera for the first two years?   A nothing   B He photographed Aleppo.   C He sold it.
3  Why did he start taking photos in Damascus?   A for his work   B because his weekends were empty   C because he joined a club
4  What does he say about his first photos?   A They were good.   B They were bad, but he met people.   C He lost them.
5  What did the teacher tell him?   A to photograph people   B to photograph buildings   C to leave the club
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
