Unit 9  Work & Study
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
In this unit you will…9.1  describe what you have to do in your job or studies9.2  explain the rules of a workplace9.3  ask for permission at work9.4  talk about your skills and experience9.5  take and leave phone messages9.6  write polite requests to colleagues9.7  Review & Mediate: pass on a phone message

New in this unitTechCare – Omar's IT company in Al-Hamra Street (manager: Mr Fouad).Medica Trading – Lina's company in Mezzeh (manager: Mr Khaled; her colleague: Rana).Nour – a new trainee at TechCare, in her first week.

9.1  I have to check the machines
Function: describing job duties   ·   Grammar: have to / has to   ·   Pronunciation: have to /hæftə/, has to /hæstə/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What do you do every day at work or at college? What is the first thing you do? Your teacher writes the class's ideas on the board.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I have to check my emails first. | I'm responsible for the stock.
 | 
 | 
b  Which jobs? Who has to: wear a uniform · work at night · use a computer all day · talk to customers · carry heavy things
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Who is Nour?   A a customer   B a new trainee   C the manager
b  Listen again. 1 What does Omar have to do every morning?   2 What doesn't Nour have to do?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 9.1  First day at TechCare
Omar | Welcome, Nour! Let me explain the work. Every morning I have to check the machines in the workshop – which ones are ready, which ones are waiting for parts.
Nour | Do I have to do that too?
Omar | No, you don't. In your first month you're responsible for the small repairs: phones, chargers, keyboards.
Nour | OK. And the customers?
Omar | Rami on the desk talks to them. But you have to write a note for every repair – what the problem is, what you did, and how long it took.
Nour | Every repair? Even a charger?
Omar | Even a charger. Mr Fouad reads all the notes on Thursday.
Nour | And what time do I have to start?
Omar | We open at half past eight, but the technicians have to be here at eight – we prepare the machines first.
Nour | Do I have to work on Saturdays?
Omar | Only one Saturday a month. And you don't have to come on Fridays – nobody does!

Language boxGrammar – have to / has to (something is necessary)+ | – | ?
I / you / we / they have to write a note. | I don't have to work on Fridays.
(= it isn't necessary – I can if I want) | Do you have to work on Saturdays?
– Yes, I do. / No, I don't.
He / she has to be here at eight. | She doesn't have to wear a uniform. | Does he have to start at eight?
– Yes, he does. / No, he doesn't.
have to + base verb: I have to check…, not have to checking, not have to checks.
⚠ don't have to = it isn't necessary (you can choose).  It is not the same as mustn't = don't do it! (see 9.2)
Job words: be responsible for · check · prepare · repair · write a report / a note · deal with customers · work shifts
Practise: Complete with the correct form of have to.  1 She ______ wear a uniform in the hospital.  2 I ______ (–) work on Fridays.  3 ______ you ______ travel for your job?  4 He ______ be at the site at seven.  5 We ______ (–) pay for the training – the company pays.
Useful phrases: Let me explain the work. · You're responsible for… · Even a charger? · Nobody does!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 9.2 have to = /hæftə/ (with an f sound!) · has to = /hæstə/ (with an s sound). I have to check. · She has to start at eight. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is the experienced worker, B is new. Explain the job on the card. Then swap.
A | Welcome! Let me explain the work. Every morning I have to __________.
B | Do I have to do that too?
A | No, you don't. You're responsible for __________.
B | OK. And __________?
A | __________ does that. But you have to __________.
B | What time do I have to start?
A | We open at __________, but you have to be here at __________.
B | Do I have to work __________?
A | Yes, you do. / No, you don't.
Job card A – a pharmacy | Job card B – a hotel reception
morning: check the fridge temperature · new person: the shelves and the prices · the pharmacist talks to patients · write the delivery book every day · open 8.00, staff 7.45 · Saturdays yes, Fridays afternoon only | morning: read the night book · new person: the keys and the breakfast list · the manager deals with problems · wear a uniform · open 24 hours, shifts 7–3, 3–11, 11–7 · one weekend a month free
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform without the book. Listener task: write two things the new person has to do and one thing they don't have to do.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Tell your partner about your real job or studies: what do you have to do every day? What do you have to do once a week or once a month? What don't you have to do (lucky you!)? Your partner asks three questions. Then swap and change partners.
Every day | Every week | Every month | I don't have to…
 |  |  | 
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner's work:  Rasha has to prepare her lessons every evening and she has to write reports every month. She has to be at school at half past seven. She doesn't have to wear a uniform, but she has to wear something smart.
Listener task: whose job has the most duties? Who starts the earliest?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the job description from the TechCare file. Answer the questions.
TECHCARE – JOB DESCRIPTION   Position: Junior Technician   Reports to: Omar DarwishMain dutiesThe junior technician is responsible for small repairs: phones, chargers, keyboards and cables. He or she has to write a repair note for every job, with the problem, the solution and the time.The junior technician also has to keep the workshop clean and check the small parts box every Thursday.HoursTechnicians have to be in the workshop at 8.00, thirty minutes before we open. The junior technician has to work one Saturday a month. Nobody works on Fridays.Not part of this jobThe junior technician doesn't have to deal with customers at the desk, and doesn't have to answer the company phone.
1  What must be in every repair note?
2  What happens every Thursday?
3  Name two things this person doesn't have to do.
Writer's boxA job description: position + who you report to → main duties (is responsible for… has to…) → hours → not part of this job (doesn't have to…).Write about the position, not a person: The junior technician has to… (he / she + -s!).The "not part of this job" section stops arguments later – always include it!
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
______________ – JOB DESCRIPTION   Position: ______________   Reports to: ______________   Main duties: The ______________ is responsible for ______________. He or she has to ______________. He or she also has to ______________.   Hours: ______________.   Not part of this job: The ______________ doesn't have to ______________.
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
has to + base verb (he / she!) | 
doesn't have to for the things that aren't necessary | 
All parts: position, duties, hours, not part of the job | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Two or three students read one duty before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write a job description for your own job, a job you know, or a job you would like (60 words or more). Include at least four duties and two things the person doesn't have to do.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's description as someone applying for the job. ★ This sounds interesting!  ✓ Can you add the salary?  ? Do I have to…?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two descriptions are read aloud. Which job does the class want? Which has the hardest hours?
HomeworkWrite a short email to apply for your partner's job (30+ words): say why you want it and ask one question about the duties.

9.2  You mustn't use that door
Function: explaining rules   ·   Grammar: must / mustn't / don't have to   ·   Pronunciation: mustn't /ˈmʌsnt/ (silent t!)
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What rules are there at your work, your college or your building? Which rule is the most annoying? Make a class list.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
You mustn't smoke in the building. | Staff must wear ID badges.
 | 
 | 
b  Necessary, forbidden or free? wear a seat belt · park in front of the gate · come to the meeting · bring your own lunch
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What does Nour do wrong?   A she arrives late   B she uses the wrong door   C she breaks a machine
b  Listen again. 1 Why is that door a problem?   2 What must she do with her badge?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 9.3  The rules at TechCare
Mr Fouad | Nour! Not that door, please.
Nour | Oh! I'm sorry. Is it broken?
Mr Fouad | No, but you mustn't use the back door. It's the emergency exit – it has to stay closed. Everyone comes in through reception.
Nour | I understand. Sorry, it's my second day.
Mr Fouad | Don't worry. Let me tell you the important rules. First, you must wear your badge – always, even in the workshop.
Nour | It's here, in my bag…
Mr Fouad | In your bag is not on your shirt! Second, you mustn't take customers' machines out of the building. Never – not even to help a friend.
Nour | Of course not.
Mr Fouad | Third, you must write your name in the parts book when you take something from the box.
Nour | And the kitchen? Do I have to bring my own lunch?
Mr Fouad | No, you don't have to – there's a restaurant next door. But you mustn't eat near the machines. That one is serious.
Nour | Badge on, back door no, machines stay here, name in the book, no food in the workshop. Did I forget anything?
Mr Fouad | No! And welcome to TechCare.

Language boxGrammar – must / mustn't / don't have toForm | Meaning | Example
must + base verb | It's necessary. Do it! | You must wear your badge.
mustn't + base verb | It's forbidden. Don't do it! | You mustn't use the back door.
don't have to + base verb | It isn't necessary. You can choose. | You don't have to bring lunch.
⚠ The big difference:  You mustn't take the machines home. (forbidden!)  ≠  You don't have to bring lunch. (it's your choice)
must has no -s and no do: He must wear it. · Must I wear it? (or more usually: Do I have to wear it?)
In writing, rules often use must and mustn't. When we speak, we often use have to and can't.
Practise: mustn't or don't have to?  1 You ______ smoke here – it's a hospital.  2 You ______ come on Friday; the office is closed.  3 You ______ tell anybody the password.  4 You ______ wear a tie, but you can if you want.  5 Visitors ______ enter the workshop.
Useful phrases: Let me tell you the important rules. · First,… Second,… Third,… · Of course not. · That one is serious. · Did I forget anything?
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 9.4 mustn't = /ˈmʌsnt/ – don't say the first t: mus-nt. Compare: You must wear it. /məst/ (weak) and You mustn't. (strong). Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A explains the rules on the card to B, who is new. B asks two questions and repeats the rules at the end. Then swap.
A | Let me tell you the important rules. First, you must __________.
B | OK. And __________?
A | You mustn't __________. Never.
B | Do I have to __________?
A | No, you don't have to. But you mustn't __________.
B | So: __________, __________, __________. Did I forget anything?
Rules card A – a laboratory | Rules card B – a children's nursery
must: wear a white coat and glasses · mustn't: eat or drink inside, touch other people's samples · don't have to: work after 4 | must: wash your hands when you arrive, sign the children in and out · mustn't: take photos of the children, give any food without asking · don't have to: wear a uniform
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write one must, one mustn't and one don't have to rule you hear.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
a  Explain the real rules of your workplace, college or building to your partner. Give at least two things people must do, two things they mustn't do and one thing they don't have to do.
b  Now discuss: which of your partner's rules is the most sensible? Which rule would you change?
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner's rules:  At Fadi's workshop everybody must wear boots, and they mustn't use the machines alone. They don't have to wear a uniform. Fadi says the best rule is "no phones near the machines" – he saw an accident last year.
Listener task: which place has the strictest rules? Which rule would you change?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the page from the TechCare staff handbook. Answer the questions.
TECHCARE – STAFF RULES1 Badges. All staff must wear their badge during working hours, in the office and in the workshop.2 Doors. Everyone must enter through reception. Staff mustn't use the back door; it is an emergency exit and must stay closed.3 Customers' machines. Machines mustn't leave the building for any reason. If a repair needs a special part, tell your manager.4 Parts. You must write your name and the date in the parts book when you take anything from the box.5 Food. You mustn't eat or drink near the machines. Please use the kitchen. You don't have to bring lunch – there is a restaurant next door.6 Phones. Personal phones must be on silent in the workshop. You don't have to leave them at reception, but please don't answer calls while you are repairing.Thank you for helping us work safely. – Fouad Al-Saleh, Manager
1  Where must staff wear their badge?
2  What must you do before you take a part from the box?
3  What is the rule about personal phones?
Writer's boxWritten rules: numbered sections with a short title (Badges, Doors, Food…) → the rule with must / mustn't → sometimes a reason or what to do instead.Add Please… and a thank-you line at the end so the rules don't sound angry.Give the reason for the strange rules: it is an emergency exit. People follow rules they understand.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
______________ – RULES   1 ______________. All ______________ must ______________.   2 ______________. You mustn't ______________, because ______________.   3 ______________. You must ______________.   4 ______________. You don't have to ______________, but please ______________.   Thank you for ______________. – ______________
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
must / mustn't / don't have to + base verb | 
A reason for at least one rule | 
Numbered sections with titles + a friendly ending | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one rule before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write the rules for a real place: your workplace, a shared flat, a computer room, a family car, a children's playroom (60 words or more). Write five numbered rules with at least one reason.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's rules as a new person there. ★ Clear and fair!  ✓ Can you add why for rule 3?  ? What happens if I forget?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Put the rules on the wall. Which place would you like to work in? Which rule is the funniest?
HomeworkWrite a short message to your partner (25+ words): say which rule you don't understand, and suggest one new rule for their place.

9.3  Is it OK if I leave early?
Function: asking for permission   ·   Grammar: Can I…? Could I…? Is it OK if I…?   ·   Pronunciation: polite, rising then falling intonation
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What do you ask your manager or teacher for? (leave early, take a day off, change a shift, use a room…) What do they answer?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Could I leave early today? | Dear Mr Khaled, I would like to ask…
 | 
 | 
b  Order them from least polite to most polite: I'm leaving early. · Can I leave early? · Could I possibly leave early? · Is it OK if I leave early?
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What does Lina ask for?   A a day off   B to leave early   C a different desk
b  Listen again. 1 Why does she need it?   2 What does she offer to do?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 9.5  In Mr Khaled's office
Lina | Mr Khaled, have you got a minute?
Mr Khaled | Yes, Lina, come in. What is it?
Lina | Is it OK if I leave at three on Thursday? My niece has a small operation and I'd like to be at the hospital with my brother.
Mr Khaled | Of course. I hope everything goes well. Is it serious?
Lina | No, thank you – it's small, but she's only three.
Mr Khaled | Then of course you must go. What about the supplier report?
Lina | I'll finish it on Wednesday evening, and Rana has all the files if anybody needs them.
Mr Khaled | Perfect. And could you tell Rana about the Thursday call too?
Lina | Yes, of course. One more thing – could I possibly take Friday morning as well, if the operation is late?
Mr Khaled | Friday isn't a working day, Lina.
Lina | (laughs) Of course. I'm tired already!

Language boxGrammar – asking for permissionLess formal | More polite | Most polite / a big request
Can I leave early? | Could I leave early? · Is it OK if I leave early? | Could I possibly take Friday off? · Would it be possible to change my shift?
Is it OK if I + present simple: Is it OK if I leave at three?  ·  Could I + base verb: Could I take Thursday off?
Answers: 😀 Of course. · Yes, that's fine. · No problem.   🙁 I'm afraid not. · Sorry, not this week – we have the audit. · Can we talk about it tomorrow?
Good manners at work: give a reason, and say how you will solve the problem: I'll finish the report on Wednesday · Rana has all the files.
Practise: Make polite requests.  1 (leave at four today / dentist)  2 (take Monday off / family wedding)  3 (change my shift with Fadi / exam)  4 (use the meeting room at two / online interview)
Useful phrases: Have you got a minute? · What is it? · I hope everything goes well. · One more thing… · Of course you must go.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 9.6 Permission questions go up then down: Is it OK if I leave at three? ↗↘  A flat voice sounds like you are telling, not asking! Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A asks the manager (B) for the things on the card. Always give a reason and a solution. B says yes to one and no to one. Then swap.
A | __________, have you got a minute?
B | Yes, come in. What is it?
A | Is it OK if I __________? __________.
B | Of course. / I'm afraid not this week, because __________.
A | I'll __________, and __________ has everything if anybody needs it.
B | Perfect. / Then let's say __________ instead.
A | Thank you very much.
Requests card A | Requests card B
leave at 2 on Sunday (your son starts school) · take a week off in December (your brother is coming from abroad) | change your Saturday shift with a colleague (an exam) · work from home on Wednesday (the plumber is coming)
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: did the person give a reason and a solution? Did the manager say yes or no?
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Think of three real things you would like to ask your manager, your teacher or your family for. Ask your partner (they are the manager / teacher / parent). They must say yes to one, no to one, and "let's talk about it" to one – with reasons. Then swap.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class:  Hiba asked me for three days off in December to visit her sister in Beirut. I said yes, because she finished the project early. She also asked to change her Tuesday class, but I said no – the room isn't free.
Listener task: who asked the most politely? Whose reason was the strongest?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read Lina's email to her manager. Answer the questions.
To: k.ahmad@medicatrading.sy   Subject: Request – leaving early on Thursday 23rdDear Mr Khaled,I am writing to ask if I could leave the office at 3 pm on Thursday 23 October. My niece has a small operation that afternoon and I would like to be at the hospital with my family.I will finish the supplier report on Wednesday evening and send it to you before I leave. Rana has all the files, and she can join the call with the Turkish supplier at 4 pm.If Thursday is difficult, I could work on Saturday morning instead.Thank you very much for considering my request.Best regards,Lina Haddad · Accounts · Medica Trading
1  What exactly is Lina asking for?
2  What three things has she organised?
3  What does she offer if the answer is no?
Writer's boxA request email: a clear subject line (Request – what, when) → Dear Mr / Ms + family name, → the request (I am writing to ask if I could…) → the reason → how you solved the problem → an alternative → thank you → Best regards, → name + department.Work emails are more formal than messages: write I am, I will, I would like – not I'm, I'll, I'd.Never ask without offering a solution. That is the difference between a good request and a problem for your manager.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Subject: Request – ______________   Dear ______________,   I am writing to ask if I could ______________ on ______________. ______________.   I will ______________ before I leave. ______________ has ______________ and ______________.   If ______________ is difficult, I could ______________ instead.   Thank you very much for considering my request.   Best regards,   ______________
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
Formal forms (I am / I will / I would like) | 
A clear subject line with the date | 
All parts: request, reason, solution, alternative, thanks | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read your request sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write a real request email to a manager, a teacher or an office (60 words or more): time off, a change of shift, a day to study, working from home, a different class time.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's email as the manager. ★ Well organised!  ✓ Can you add who will cover your work?  ? Can you do it another day?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two emails are read aloud. The class decides: would the manager say yes?
HomeworkWrite the manager's reply to your partner's email (30+ words): say yes, no or "yes, but…", give a reason, and say what the person must do before they go.

9.4  I can use Excel and I worked as a cashier
Function: talking about skills and experience   ·   Grammar: can + past simple (recycle)   ·   Pronunciation: word stress in long words
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What skills do employers want? (languages, computers, driving, working with people…) Which do you have? Make a class list.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I can use Excel quite well. | Skills: Arabic, English, Excel, driving licence
 | 
 | 
b  Skill or experience? I worked in a hotel for two years. · I can type fast. · I studied accounting. · I speak three languages.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What job is Nour applying for?   A a permanent job at TechCare   B a job at a hotel   C a university place
b  Listen again. 1 What did she do before TechCare?   2 What can't she do yet?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 9.7  The interview
Mr Fouad | So, Nour, your training finishes next month. Tell me about your experience before TechCare.
Nour | I studied IT at the institute for two years and I finished in June. Before that, I worked in my uncle's phone shop for three summers.
Mr Fouad | What did you do there?
Nour | I sold phones and accessories, and I changed screens and batteries. I learned a lot about customers – some of them were… difficult!
Mr Fouad | Ha! And your computer skills?
Nour | I can repair hardware quite well, and I can install Windows and Linux. I can use Excel for the parts lists, but I'm not very good at it yet.
Mr Fouad | And English? Our manuals are in English.
Nour | I can read technical English quite well. Speaking is harder – but I started classes at Al-Yasmin in September.
Mr Fouad | Good. One last question: what can you do that your colleagues can't?
Nour | I think… I listen. People explain their problem badly, and I ask questions until I understand. My uncle taught me that.
Mr Fouad | That's a very good answer.

Language boxTalking about skills and experienceSkills now (can) | Experience (past simple) | Level
I can use Excel. · I can drive. · I can read technical English. | I worked in a shop for three summers. · I studied IT for two years. · I learned a lot about customers. | quite well · very well · not very well · I'm not very good at it yet · Speaking is harder.
for + a period: for two years · for three summers   in + year: in June
Be honest and positive: I'm not very good at it yet, but I started a course. – this is much better than "I can't".
Useful interview questions: Tell me about your experience. · What did you do there? · What are your computer skills? · What can you do that others can't?
Practise: can or past simple?  1 I ______ (work) in a hotel last summer.  2 I ______ (use) Photoshop quite well.  3 She ______ (study) at the institute for two years.  4 He ______ (drive), but he hasn't got a car.  5 We ______ (learn) a lot in that job.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 9.8 Stress in long words: exPErience · TECHnical · comPUter · acCOUNTing · uniVERsity · INterview · qualifiCAtions. Listen and repeat.

🔊 Track 9.9  A call for Mr Khaled
Lina | Good morning, Medica Trading.
Caller | Good morning. Could I speak to Mr Khaled Ahmad, please?
Lina | I'm afraid he's in a meeting until twelve. Who's calling, please?
Caller | This is Yusuf Haddadin from Delta Medical in Amman.
Lina | Sorry, could you spell your family name?
Caller | H-A-D-D-A-D-I-N. Haddadin.
Lina | Thank you. Can I take a message?
Caller | Yes, please. It's about the order of December. We can't send the full quantity this month – only half. I need to know if that's OK, or if he prefers to wait until January.
Lina | I see. So: half the quantity this month, or everything in January.
Caller | Exactly. Could you ask him to call me back today? It's a little urgent.
Lina | Of course. What's your number?
Caller | Zero-zero-nine-six-two, seven-nine-five, four-one-two, eight-double-three.
Lina | Let me read that back: 00962 795 412 833.
Caller | That's right. Thank you very much.
Lina | You're welcome. I'll give him the message as soon as he finishes.

Language boxOn the phone at workAnswering | The person isn't free | Taking the message
Good morning, [company]. · How can I help you? | I'm afraid he 's in a meeting / isn't in the office today. · He'll be back at… | Who's calling, please? · Could you spell that? · Can I take a message? · What's your number? · Let me read that back. · I'll give him the message.
I'll (= I will) for a decision now: I'll give him the message. · I'll call you back. · I'll tell her.
Asking for action: Could you ask him to call me back? · Could you tell her the meeting is at three? · Please ask him to send the file.
Numbers on the phone: 0 = "zero" or "oh" · 33 = "double three" · say the number in small groups: 0944 – 318 – 260. Always read it back!
Practise: Complete the phone phrases.  1 Who's ______, please?  2 Could you ______ that?  3 Can I ______ a message?  4 I'm ______ he's in a meeting.  5 Let me ______ that back.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 9.10 Say names and numbers slowly, in groups, and read them back: H-A-D-D-A-D-I-N · 00962 — 795 — 412 — 833. Listen and repeat. Then practise with your own number.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs, back to back (you can't see your partner – like a real phone call!). A answers the phone, B is the caller on the card. A writes the message. Then swap.
A | Good morning, __________.
B | Could I speak to __________, please?
A | I'm afraid __________. Who's calling, please?
B | This is __________ from __________.
A | Could you spell your family name? … Can I take a message?
B | Yes, please. __________. Could you ask him / her to __________?
A | Of course. What's your number?
B | __________.
A | Let me read that back: __________. I'll give him / her the message.

Writer's boxA message slip has eight parts: For · From · Company · Date and time · Message · Action · Phone · Taken by.The Message says *what happened*; the Action says *what the person must do*. Never mix them!Write the time of the call – your manager needs to know if it was two hours ago or two minutes ago.Use bold or capitals for the most important word: only half, call him back today.

9.6  Could you send me the file?
Function: polite requests at work   ·   Grammar: Could you…? Would you mind…?; Thanks in advance   ·   Pronunciation: Could you /kʊdʒə/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What do you ask colleagues for every week? (a file, a signature, help, information, a change of time…) What do you write in those emails?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Could you send me the file? | Thanks in advance!
 | 
 | 
b  Which is the most polite? Send me the file. · Can you send me the file? · Could you send me the file, please? · Would you mind sending me the file?
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. How many things does Rana ask Omar for?   A one   B two   C three
b  Listen again. 1 What is the problem with the laptop?   2 When can Omar come?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 9.11  A call between two companies
Omar | TechCare, good afternoon.
Rana | Hello, this is Rana from Medica Trading. Is that Omar?
Omar | Yes, speaking. How can I help?
Rana | We've got a problem with one of the laptops – the one in the accounts office. It turns on, but the screen stays black.
Omar | Hmm. Could you try one thing before I come? Press and hold the power button for ten seconds, then start it again.
Rana | OK, wait… No, the same.
Omar | Fine. I'll come tomorrow morning at nine.
Rana | Thank you. And would you mind bringing a spare screen? Lina needs that laptop for the end-of-month accounts.
Omar | No problem. Could you send me the model number before five? It's on a sticker under the laptop.
Rana | Of course. I'll send it by email in ten minutes.
Omar | Perfect. One more thing – could you ask reception to make me a visitor badge? Last time I waited twenty minutes at the door!
Rana | Ha! I remember. I'll do it today. Thanks in advance, Omar.

Language boxGrammar – polite requests at workRequest | Form | Example
Could you + base verb | the normal polite request | Could you send me the file?
Would you mind + -ing | extra polite (a bigger favour) | Would you mind bringing a spare screen?
Could you ask + person + to + verb | a request through someone | Could you ask reception to make a badge?
⚠ Would you mind bringing…? – -ing, not to bring.  And the answer is confusing! Would you mind…? → No, not at all = yes, I'll do it. 😀
Softening a request: Could you possibly…? · If you have time… · before five, if possible · Sorry to ask, but…
Thanking in advance: Thanks in advance. · Thank you for your help. · I appreciate it.
Practise: Make the requests more polite.  1 Send me the report. → ______  2 Change the meeting to three. → ______  3 Help me with this machine. → ______  4 Tell Mr Fouad I called. → ______
Useful phrases: Yes, speaking. · How can I help? · Could you try one thing before I come? · No problem. · One more thing… · I remember!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 9.12 Could you /kʊdʒə/ and Would you /wʊdʒə/ – fast and linked: Could‿you send me the file? Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A phones B with the problem on the card and makes three requests (use a different form each time!). Then swap.
B | __________, good afternoon.
A | Hello, this is __________ from __________.
B | Yes, speaking. How can I help?
A | We've got a problem with __________. __________.
B | Could you try __________ before I come?
A | OK, wait… __________.
B | Fine. I'll come __________.
A | Thank you. And would you mind __________? One more thing – could you ask __________ to __________?
B | No problem. Thanks in advance!
Problem card A | Problem card B
the office printer prints everything grey · you need 60 copies for a meeting tomorrow at 10 · ask: come today, bring ink, send the price first | the air conditioning in the shop is very noisy · customers are complaining · ask: come before the weekend, bring a quiet machine to show you, call before coming

☐ Could you…? | ☐ Would you mind …-ing? | ☐ Could you ask … to…? | ☐ Could you possibly…? | ☐ Thanks in advance.

9.7  Review & Mediate: Mr Saleh called
Function: passing on a phone message   ·   Recycling: Units 1–9   ·   Exam practice: A2 Key Reading Part 5, Writing Part 6, Listening Part 2
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready – Unit 9 quiz   (6 min)
In teams. Correct the mistakes! 1 I must to wear a badge.  2 She have to work on Saturday.  3 You don't must smoke here.  4 Would you mind to send the file?  5 Can I to leave early?  6 He doesn't has to come.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Read the message Nour wrote during a very busy morning.
message on a piece of paper, in a hurry:Mr Saleh — Zenith — 11.40machine 3 — not working again — same problem as last monthhe is angry!!! waited 2 weeks last timewants technician TODAY or tomorrow morning latestif nobody comes he will call another companytel 0988 442 150 — ask for him NOT the office
b  Books closed. Listen. How does Omar tell Mr Fouad about this message?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 9.13  Passing on the message
Omar | Mr Fouad, have you got a minute? There's a message from this morning.
Mr Fouad | Go ahead.
Omar | Mr Saleh from Zenith called at twenty to twelve. Machine three isn't working again – he says it's the same problem as last month.
Mr Fouad | Again? We repaired it in September.
Omar | That's the problem. He isn't happy: he says he waited two weeks last time.
Mr Fouad | I understand. What does he want?
Omar | He wants a technician today, or tomorrow morning at the latest. And he said that if nobody comes, he'll call another company.
Mr Fouad | Hmm. We mustn't lose Zenith – they have eight machines. Who's free?
Omar | Nour and I can go this afternoon. We have to finish two small repairs first, but we can be there at three.
Mr Fouad | Do that. And call him now, don't wait – tell him you're coming at three.
Omar | Of course. He asked for him directly, not the office – his number is 0988 442 150.
Mr Fouad | Good. Thank you, Omar.

Language box – passing on a messageTurn the notes into full sentences, in this order:1 Who / when: Mr Saleh from Zenith called at twenty to twelve.2 The problem: Machine three isn't working again – he says it's the same problem as last month.3 The feeling (carefully!): He isn't happy: he says he waited two weeks last time.  (Not "He is angry!!!" – be professional.)4 What he wants: He wants a technician today, or tomorrow morning at the latest.5 The warning: He said that if nobody comes, he'll call another company.6 How to contact him: He asked for him directly, not the office – his number is…Useful phrases: Have you got a minute? · There's a message from this morning. · Go ahead. · He says… · He asked for… · at the latest
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A has the notes on the card (don't show them!). A passes the message to B (the manager) in full sentences, in the right order. B asks two questions and decides what to do. Then swap.

Dr Samar — Al-Noor Clinic — 9.15 | Umm Yazan — building owner — 4.50 pm
delivery came — 3 boxes missing | water pump broken — no water on floors 4–6

A  Grammar and vocabulary. Choose the correct answer.
1  I ___ wear a uniform at work.   a) have   b) have to   c) has to
2  She ___ work on Fridays – the office is closed.   a) mustn't   b) doesn't have to   c) don't have to
3  You ___ smoke here. It's forbidden.   a) mustn't   b) don't have to   c) haven't to
4  ___ I leave at three today?   a) Could   b) Must   c) Have
5  Would you mind ___ me the file?   a) send   b) to send   c) sending
6  Could you ask him ___ me back?   a) call   b) to call   c) calling
7  I ___ in a phone shop for three summers.   a) work   b) worked   c) have to work
8  "Can I take a ___?" – "Yes, please. Tell him Samar called."   a) message   b) note   c) call

🔊 Track 9.14  Audio script (read after the check)
Receptionist | Good afternoon, Medica Trading.
Caller | Good afternoon. Could I speak to Mr Khaled, please?
Receptionist | I'm afraid he's with a client. Can I take a message?
Caller | Yes, please. This is Samar Khoury – that's K-H-O-U-R-Y – from Zenith Pharma.
Receptionist | Thank you, Ms Khoury. What's the message?
Caller | It's about our order. We asked for forty boxes, but we only need thirty now. Could you ask him to call me back before five o'clock? After five I'm travelling.
Receptionist | Of course. What's your number?
Caller | It's 0944 762 310.
Receptionist | Let me read that back: 0944 762 310. I'll give him the message.
C  Writing. Write an email to a colleague (35+ words). Ask them for two things you need for a meeting, say when you need them, and thank them.





I can… (Unit 9)  —  tick: 😀 well  🙂 OK  😐 not yet☐ describe what I have to do in my job or studies☐ explain rules with must / mustn't / don't have to☐ ask for permission politely, with a reason and a solution☐ talk about my skills and experience☐ answer the phone, take a message and read a number back☐ write polite requests to colleagues☐ pass on a message in full sentences, in the right order☐ write a job description, staff rules, a request email, a skills profile, a message slip and a work email

Unit 10  Health
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
In this unit you will…10.1  say how you feel and describe symptoms10.2  give and ask for advice10.3  make an appointment10.4  read and explain the instructions on a medicine box10.5  talk about healthy habits10.6  say you're sorry someone is ill, and wish them well10.7  Review & Mediate: explain a medicine label to a relative

ImportantIn this unit you learn the English words people use about health: how to describe how you feel, how to book an appointment, and how to read the instructions on a box.Your doctor and your pharmacist decide about medicine – not this book and not your classmates! When our characters have a real problem, they always ask a professional. Do the same.

New in this unitDr Samar Khalil – a doctor at Al-Noor Clinic, Mezzeh.   ·   Al-Shifa Pharmacy – Maya's pharmacy (Unit 2).

10.1  I've got a sore throat
Function: describing symptoms   ·   Grammar: have got + illness; My … hurts   ·   Pronunciation: /ɜː/ hurt, worse, nurse
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  How many parts of the body can you name in English in two minutes? Your teacher writes them on the board.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I've got a headache. | I can't come to work today.
 | 
 | 
b  Match: head · throat · stomach · back · tooth  →  You eat with it. · You think with it. · You sit on a chair and it hurts. · You speak with it. · You feel it after a big meal.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Where is Rami?   A at the doctor's   B at the pharmacy   C at home
b  Listen again. 1 How long has he had the problem?   2 What does Maya tell him to do?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 10.1  At Al-Shifa Pharmacy
Maya | Rami! You don't look well. What's the matter?
Rami | I've got a terrible sore throat. And my head hurts.
Maya | Oh dear. How long have you had it?
Rami | Since Sunday. Three days.
Maya | Have you got a temperature?
Rami | I don't know. I feel hot, then cold.
Maya | Hmm. Are you coughing?
Rami | A little, at night. And I'm very tired – I slept twelve hours yesterday.
Maya | Rami, listen to me as a pharmacist, not as a friend. Three days with a temperature isn't a simple cold. You should see a doctor today.
Rami | It's just a cold, Maya. I've got a lot of work…
Maya | Dr Samar at Al-Noor Clinic is open until six. Go, please. If it's nothing, you'll be happy. If it isn't, you'll be happier.
Rami | (laughs) OK, OK. I'm going.

Language boxGrammar – talking about how you feelhave got + a / an | My … hurts | I feel + adjective
I've got a headache / a sore throat / a cold / a temperature / a cough / a stomach ache / toothache (no a). | My head hurts. · My back hurts. · My feet hurt. (plural – no -s!) | I feel tired / hot / cold / dizzy / sick (= I want to vomit). · I don't feel well.
⚠ I feel well (an adjective after feel), not I feel good for health.  ⚠ My head hurts, not My head is hurt or I have pain in my head (possible, but not natural).
Asking: What's the matter? · How do you feel? · Have you got a temperature? · How long have you had it? – Since Sunday. / For three days.
Parts of the body: head · eye · ear · nose · mouth · tooth (teeth) · throat · neck · shoulder · arm · hand · finger · back · stomach · leg · knee · foot (feet)
Practise: Complete.  1 I've got ______ sore throat.  2 My legs ______ (hurt) after the match.  3 She's got ______ toothache.  4 I don't feel ______.  5 How long ______ you had it? – ______ Monday.
Useful phrases: You don't look well. · Oh dear. · I feel hot, then cold. · Listen to me as a pharmacist, not as a friend. · It's just a cold.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 10.2 /ɜː/ – a long sound: hurt · worse · nurse · first · Thursday · heard. Listen and repeat: My back hurts and it's getting worse.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A doesn't feel well, B asks questions. B always ends by sending A to a doctor or pharmacist. Then swap.
B | You don't look well. What's the matter?
A | I've got __________. And my __________ hurts.
B | Oh dear. How long have you had it?
A | Since __________.
B | Have you got __________?
A | __________.
B | I think you should __________.
A | OK, I'm going.
Symptom card A | Symptom card B
bad stomach ache · since yesterday evening · can't eat · feels dizzy | toothache · for a week · worse at night · the side of the face is swollen
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform without the book. Listener task: write the symptoms and how long. Was the advice good?
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
a  Think of a time when you weren't well (a cold, a bad back, a headache after a long day – nothing private you don't want to share). Tell your partner: what was the matter, how long it lasted, what you did.
b  Now your partner describes symptoms and you ask four questions. Change partners and repeat.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner:  Last winter Fadi had a bad cough for two weeks. He didn't sleep well and he felt tired all day. In the end he went to a doctor, and after that he was fine.
Listener task: who waited the longest before they asked for help?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the two messages. Answer the questions.
1 · Rami → Mr Fouad (his manager)Dear Mr Fouad,I'm sorry, but I can't come to work today. I've had a sore throat and a temperature since Sunday, and this morning I feel worse. I'm going to see a doctor at eleven.The two laptops for Medica Trading are finished and they're on the shelf with the notes. Nour knows about the printer at the school; she can finish it today.I'll call you after the doctor and tell you about tomorrow.Sorry again,Rami2 · Rami → OmarOmar – I'm not coming in today, I feel terrible 🤒 Sore throat since Sunday + temperature. Maya shouted at me until I agreed to see a doctor! Can you tell Nour the printer parts are in the blue box? Thanks – and don't come near me this week 😷
1  Who is the first message for? Who is the second for?
2  Which message is more formal? Give two examples.
3  What does Rami organise in both messages?
Writer's boxA message to say you're ill: sorry + you can't come → what's wrong (short! nobody needs the details) → what you're doing about it → your work (what is finished, who can help) → when you'll be in touch.Formal (manager): Dear Mr…, I'm sorry, but I can't… I'll call you…   Informal (a friend): short sentences, emojis, jokes.The most important part is the work section. A good "I'm ill" message makes your colleague's day easier, not harder.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Dear ______________,   I'm sorry, but I can't come to ______________ today. I've had ______________ since ______________, and this morning ______________. I'm going to ______________.   ______________ is / are finished and ______________. ______________ knows about ______________.   I'll ______________.   Sorry again,   ______
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
I've got / I've had … since / for | 
The work section: what's finished, who can help | 
All parts: sorry, what's wrong, what you're doing, work, contact | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Two or three students read one sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write two messages for the same day (60 words or more in total): one formal to your manager, teacher or client, and one informal to a colleague or friend. You can't come today.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's formal message as the manager. ★ Thank you for organising everything.  ✓ Can you add who has the keys?  ? Do you need more days?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two pairs of messages are read aloud. What is different between the formal and the informal one?
HomeworkWrite the manager's reply (25+ words): say get well, answer one question, and tell them not to worry about the work.

10.2  You should drink more water
Function: giving and asking for advice   ·   Grammar: should / shouldn't; Why don't you…?   ·   Pronunciation: should /ʃʊd/ – silent l
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What advice do people in your family give when someone is ill? (tea with lemon, soup, rest, go to the doctor…) Make a class list – the funny ones too!
We SAY… | We WRITE…
You should drink more water. | Why don't you take a day off?
 | 
 | 
b  Good advice or bad advice? You should sleep more. · You shouldn't go to the doctor – it's expensive. · You should drink water. · You shouldn't sit at the computer for six hours.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What is Hiba's problem?   A she can't sleep   B her back hurts   C she has a cold
b  Listen again. 1 Why does her back hurt?   2 What does Rasha say about the doctor?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 10.3  In the centre café
Hiba | Ouch.
Rasha | What's wrong?
Hiba | My back. I sit at the computer from eight until four and then I study in the evening.
Rasha | That's ten hours on a chair! You should stand up every hour.
Hiba | I forget.
Rasha | Then why don't you put an alarm on your phone? Every hour: stand up, walk, drink some water.
Hiba | That's a good idea, actually.
Rasha | And you shouldn't study on your bed. My sister did that for a year and her back was terrible.
Hiba | But my desk is full of boxes!
Rasha | Then that's your homework this weekend: empty the desk. 😀
Hiba | Ha! OK. And if it doesn't get better?
Rasha | Then you should see a doctor. Don't wait a month like my sister – she needed six weeks of exercises in the end.

Language boxGrammar – giving adviceshould / shouldn't + base verb | Why don't you + base verb? | Other ways
You should stand up every hour. · You shouldn't study on your bed. | Why don't you put an alarm on your phone? (a friendly suggestion) | It's a good idea to… · Have you tried …-ing? · If I were you, I'd… (learn as a phrase)
should + base verb, no to and no -s: She should rest, not She should to rest or She shoulds.
Asking for advice: What should I do? · Do you think I should see a doctor? · What do you think?
⚠ should is advice, not an order: You should rest. (a friend)  /  You must rest. (the doctor!)
Practise: Give advice with should or shouldn't.  1 I'm always tired. → ______  2 I've got a sore throat. → ______  3 I can't sleep at night. → ______  4 My eyes hurt after work. → ______  5 I drink six coffees a day. → ______
Useful phrases: What's wrong? · Ouch! · That's a good idea, actually. · And if it doesn't get better? · Don't wait a month.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 10.4 should /ʃʊd/ – the l is silent! shouldn't /ˈʃʊdnt/. Listen and repeat: You should rest. You shouldn't work so much.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A has the problem on the card, B gives three pieces of advice – using a different form each time. Then swap.
B | What's wrong?
A | __________. __________.
B | You should __________.
A | __________.
B | Then why don't you __________? And you shouldn't __________.
A | That's a good idea, actually.
B | And if it doesn't get better, you should __________.
Problem card A | Problem card B
You're always tired. You sleep five hours, drink a lot of coffee and look at your phone in bed. | Your eyes hurt every evening. You work on a computer all day and the light in your office is bad.
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the three pieces of advice. Which one would you follow?
 4   Your turn – the advice circle   (9 min)
a  Write one real problem on a piece of paper – something everyday: I'm always late, I can't stop looking at my phone, I never have time to exercise, I eat too fast, I don't sleep enough. (No private health problems!)
b  Give your paper to the person on your right. Read their problem and write one piece of advice. Pass again. After four papers, give them back. Read all the advice you got – which is the best?
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class:  My problem was "I never have time to exercise". Fadi said I should walk to work. Hiba said I shouldn't take the service for short journeys. Rasha asked why I don't exercise with my children. I think Rasha's advice is the best – my children have a lot of energy!
Listener task: which piece of advice is the most practical?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the problem and the answer from a magazine page. Answer the questions.
ASK OUR READERSThe problem: "I work in an office from eight until five. When I come home I'm so tired that I just eat and watch my phone until midnight. Then I sleep badly and the next day is the same. I'm thirty-one and I feel sixty. How can I change this?" – T., DamascusOne reader answers:Dear T.,I had exactly the same problem two years ago, so I understand you.First, you should change one small thing, not everything. I started with a walk: twenty minutes after dinner, every day. It isn't a lot, but it was the beginning.Second, you shouldn't take your phone to bed. Put it in the kitchen. The first week is difficult and after that you sleep like a child.Why don't you also ask a friend to walk with you? It's easier with somebody, and you can talk about your day.If you still feel tired after a month, you should see a doctor – sometimes being tired is not about habits.Good luck! It gets better. – R., Homs
1  What does the reader say about changing things?
2  Where should T. put his phone?
3  What should T. do if nothing changes after a month?
Writer's boxAn advice letter: Dear…, → show you understand (I had the same problem…) → advice 1 (First, you should…) → advice 2 (Second, you shouldn't…) → a friendly suggestion (Why don't you…?) → when to get real help → a short, warm ending.Order your advice: First… Second… And…Good advice is small and possible: twenty minutes after dinner, not "change your whole life".Always end with when to see a professional – a friend's advice has limits.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Dear ______,   I had ______________, so I understand you.   First, you should ______________. ______________.   Second, you shouldn't ______________. ______________.   Why don't you ______________?   If ______________ after ______________, you should ______________.   Good luck! ______________.
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
should / shouldn't + base verb | 
Advice in order: First… Second… Why don't you…? | 
An ending that says when to get real help | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one piece of advice before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Choose a real everyday problem (yours, a classmate's from Stage 4, or an invented one) and write an advice letter (60 words or more) with three pieces of advice.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's letter as the person with the problem. ★ I'm going to try this!  ✓ Can you add how you started?  ? What if I can't…?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two letters are read aloud. Which advice would the class really follow?
HomeworkWrite the thank-you reply from the person with the problem (25+ words): say which advice you will try first and why.

10.3  I'd like to make an appointment
Function: making an appointment   ·   Grammar: I'd like to…; dates and times (recycle)   ·   Pronunciation: dates and times on the phone
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What appointments do people make? (doctor, dentist, hairdresser, garage, bank, visa office…) What do you say when you phone?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I'd like to make an appointment. | Tuesday 28th, 10.30 – Dr Samar
 | 
 | 
b  Put in order: Is Tuesday at ten OK? · Good morning, Al-Noor Clinic. · I'd like to make an appointment, please. · Perfect, thank you. · Can I take your name?
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. When is Rami's appointment?   A today at 11   B tomorrow at 11   C Tuesday at 11
b  Listen again. 1 Why can't he come at 9.30?   2 What must he bring?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 10.5  Phoning Al-Noor Clinic
Receptionist | Good morning, Al-Noor Clinic.
Rami | Good morning. I'd like to make an appointment with Dr Samar, please.
Receptionist | Of course. Is it urgent?
Rami | I've had a sore throat and a temperature since Sunday.
Receptionist | Then let's find something today. We have half past nine or eleven o'clock.
Rami | I can't come at half past nine – I'm at a site in Kafr Sousa. Eleven is good.
Receptionist | Eleven o'clock, then. Can I take your name?
Rami | Rami Khoury. K-H-O-U-R-Y.
Receptionist | Thank you. And your phone number?
Rami | 0988 204 517.
Receptionist | Perfect. Please bring your ID card, and come ten minutes early – you have to fill in a form the first time.
Rami | OK. How much is the visit?
Receptionist | Sixty thousand for the first visit. We take cash only, I'm afraid.
Rami | That's fine. Thank you very much.
Receptionist | See you at eleven, Mr Khoury. Get well soon.

Language boxMaking an appointmentYou | The receptionist
I'd like to make an appointment with…, please. · Is it possible to come today? · Can I come on Tuesday? · How much is the visit? · Is Tuesday at ten OK? | Is it urgent? · We have 9.30 or 11 o'clock. · Can I take your name? · Could you spell that? · Please bring your ID. · Come ten minutes early. · Get well soon.
I'd like to + base verb (polite – Unit 4): I'd like to make an appointment.
Changing or cancelling: I'd like to change my appointment. · I'm afraid I can't come on Tuesday. · Could I come on Thursday instead?
at / on / in (recycle, Unit 2 and 7): at eleven o'clock · on Tuesday · on 28 October · in the morning
Practise: Complete.  1 I'd like ______ make an appointment.  2 Can I come ______ Thursday?  3 The appointment is ______ half past three.  4 Please come ten minutes ______.  5 I'm afraid I can't ______ on Monday.
Useful phrases: Is it urgent? · Let's find something today. · …, then. · We take cash only, I'm afraid. · Get well soon.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 10.6 On the phone, say dates and times slowly and clearly, and repeat them back: Tuesday the twenty-eighth, at half past ten. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs, back to back. A phones the place on the card, B is the receptionist. Make the appointment and write it down. Then swap.
B | Good morning, __________.
A | Good morning. I'd like to make an appointment __________, please.
B | Of course. We have __________ or __________.
A | I can't come at __________ because __________. __________ is good.
B | Can I take your name? … And your phone number?
A | __________.
B | Please bring __________ and come __________ early.
A | How much is it?
B | __________.
Card A – the dentist | Card B – the garage
toothache for a week · free Tue after 4, Thu morning · bring your old X-rays · 80,000 · card accepted | the car makes a noise · they have Monday 8 am or Wednesday 2 pm · leave the car for 3 hours · check is free, repairs extra
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform (back to back). Listener task: write the day, the time, the price and one thing to bring.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
a  Think of a real appointment you need to make (doctor, dentist, garage, hairdresser, office, bank). Phone your partner and make it. They invent the times – and there's always a problem: your time isn't free!
b  Now phone again – you have to change the appointment. Give a reason and agree a new time.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class:  I made an appointment at the garage for Monday at eight, but then I had to change it because I'm working. Now it's Wednesday at two. I have to leave the car for three hours and the check is free.
Listener task: who had the most difficult receptionist?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the appointment card and the message. Answer the questions.
AL-NOOR CLINIC – APPOINTMENT CARD |

Doctor | Dr Samar Khalil
Date | Thursday 23 October
Time | 11.00 am
Please bring | ID card · old reports (if any)

My name is Rami Khoury and I have an appointment with Dr Samar on Thursday 23 October at 11 am.I'm afraid I can't come at that time: my company has an urgent site meeting that morning. My phone number is 0988 204 517.Thank you very much.
1  What must Rami bring, and when must he arrive?
2  When must you call to change an appointment?
3  When is Rami not free?
Writer's boxAn appointment card has: patient · professional · date · time · what to bring · a note · how to change it.A message to change an appointment: your name + the appointment you have → you can't come + the reason → when you ARE free (this is the important part!) → sorry → your phone number.Never write only "I can't come on Thursday". Always give the times you can come – it saves two more messages.

Thank you very much.
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters (days, months) and full stops | 
I'd like to… / Could I…? for the polite request | 
The times you ARE free | 
All parts: name, appointment, reason, free times, sorry, phone | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write two things (60 words or more in total): (a) an appointment card for a real or invented place (clinic, dentist, garage, office) and (b) a message changing an appointment.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's message as the receptionist. ★ Easy to help you!  ✓ Can you add your phone number?  ? Is Sunday at four OK?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two messages are read aloud. Which one can the receptionist answer with one short reply?
HomeworkWrite the receptionist's reply (25+ words): offer a new day and time, confirm what to bring, and ask them to confirm.

10.4  Take one tablet twice a day
Function: understanding instructions   ·   Grammar: imperatives + frequency; before / after / with   ·   Pronunciation: /s/ and /z/ in plurals
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What information is on a medicine box? What words do you see? Make a class list.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Take it after meals. | Twice a day for five days
 | 
 | 
b  What do they mean? twice a day · three times a day · every eight hours · before meals · with food · at night
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Who is Maya explaining the instructions to?   A Rami   B an older customer   C a child
b  Listen again. 1 How many times a day?   2 What must he not do?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 10.7  At the pharmacy counter
Abu Samir | Maya, my daughter brought this from the doctor, but the writing is very small. I can't read it.
Maya | Let me explain, Abu Samir. The doctor says one tablet, twice a day.
Abu Samir | Twice – morning and evening?
Maya | Exactly. Morning and evening, and always after food, not before. The box says "after meals".
Abu Samir | And for how many days?
Maya | Seven days. This is important: you have to finish all of them, even if you feel better after three days.
Abu Samir | Why? If I'm well, why do I continue?
Maya | Because the doctor chose seven days for a reason. If you stop early, the problem can come back.
Abu Samir | I understand. And the other box?
Maya | That one is for the cough – one spoon at night, only if you need it. Shall I write everything on the boxes for you?
Abu Samir | Please. In big letters!
Maya | (writing) Big letters. "ONE TABLET – MORNING AND EVENING – AFTER FOOD – 7 DAYS." And here: "ONE SPOON AT NIGHT IF YOU COUGH."
Abu Samir | God bless you, my daughter.

Language boxUnderstanding instructions on a boxHow much | How often | When
one tablet · two tablets · one spoon · 5 ml · one capsule | once a day · twice a day · three times a day · every eight hours · when you need it | before meals · after meals · with food · on an empty stomach · at night · in the morning
Other words on the box: for 7 days · finish the course · keep in the fridge · keep away from children · do not use after [date] · ask your doctor or pharmacist
Explaining to somebody: The doctor says… · The box says… · It means… · This is important: … · Shall I write it for you?
⚠ twice a day = two times a day. every eight hours = three times in 24 hours (morning, afternoon, night).
Practise: What does it mean? Write it in your own words.  1 "1 tablet 3 × daily after meals"  2 "2 × 5 ml at night"  3 "Every 8 hours for 5 days"  4 "Do not take on an empty stomach"
Remember: only a doctor or pharmacist decides what medicine you take and how much. In English class we only learn to read and explain the words on the box.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 10.8 Plurals: /s/ tablets, weeks · /z/ days, pills, spoons · /ɪz/ doses, boxes. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A has the label on the card. B is a relative who can't read the small letters. Explain it in simple words, then write it in big letters. Then swap.
Label card A | Label card B
ANTIBIOTIC 500 mg | COUGH SYRUP
1 capsule every 8 hours | 10 ml three times a day
after meals · for 6 days | after food · maximum 5 days
finish the course · do not take with milk | keep in the fridge · do not drive after taking it
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the instructions in big letters as the explainer says them. Compare with the card – is anything missing?
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
a  Think of instructions you have to explain to someone in your life: a medicine box, a machine, a form, a school letter, a bank message. Explain it to your partner in simple words.
b  Your partner asks three questions – including one you can't answer. What do you say? (I don't know – we should ask the pharmacist / the office.)
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class:  Hiba explained a letter from her son's school. The children have to bring sports clothes every Tuesday and a swimming towel on Thursdays. Parents have to sign the paper and send it back before Sunday. She didn't know if they need a swimming hat, so she's going to ask the teacher.
Listener task: what was the hardest thing to explain?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the instruction leaflet. Answer the questions.
COLDEX – FOR COLDS AND SORE THROATS   Tablets, 500 mgHow to take it: Adults: one tablet three times a day, after meals. Do not take more than three tablets in 24 hours.For how long: Up to five days. If you do not feel better after five days, stop and see a doctor.Important:• Do not take this medicine on an empty stomach.• Do not take it with other cold medicines.• Keep it away from children.• Do not use after the date on the box.Ask your doctor or pharmacist before you take this medicine if you are pregnant, if you take other medicines, or if you have any questions.Al-Shifa Pharmacy · Al-Ward Street, Mezzeh · 0933 427 815
1  How many tablets a day maximum?
2  What should you do after five days if you don't feel better?
3  Who should you ask before taking it?
Writer's boxAn instruction leaflet: name + what it's for → How to take it (how much · how often · when) → For how long → Important (a short list of *do not*…) → when to ask a professional → contact.Use imperatives: Take… Do not take… Keep… Each instruction is one short line.The most important line is the last one: ask your doctor or pharmacist. Real leaflets always have it.

Capital letters and full stops |

 7P   Respond   (5 min)

10.5  I try to walk every day
Function: talking about healthy habits   ·   Grammar: try to / want to / need to + base verb; enough / too much   ·   Pronunciation: to /tə/ in verb + to
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What do people do to stay well? (walk, sleep, drink water, eat vegetables, see friends…) What makes it difficult? Make two lists.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I try to walk every day. | I don't drink enough water.
 | 
 | 
b  enough or too much?  I sleep five hours – I don't sleep ______.  ·  I drink six coffees – that's ______ coffee.  ·  I sit all day – I don't move ______.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What are they doing?   A a class survey   B a doctor's visit   C a job interview
b  Listen again. 1 What does Tareq try to do?   2 What is Lama's problem?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 10.9  The class health survey
Hiba | OK, question one: do you sleep enough?
Tareq | No! I need to sleep seven hours, but I sleep five. My son wakes up at five.
Lama | I sleep nine hours. Sometimes ten.
Hiba | Ten! Question two: do you exercise?
Tareq | I try to walk home from the bank twice a week – it's forty minutes.
Lama | I want to go to the gym, but I don't have time. I'm a student – I study all evening.
Hiba | Hmm, "I don't have time" is what everybody says.
Lama | I know, I know. The truth is I spend too much time on my phone.
Hiba | Me too, honestly. Question three: water?
Tareq | I don't drink enough. I drink coffee – five cups a day.
Hiba | Five! And you can't sleep…
Tareq | Ah. Maybe that's the problem, not my son.
Lama | (laughing) Write that in the report!

Language boxGrammar – verb + to + base verb; enough / too muchtry / want / need / hope / decide + **to** + base verb | enough / not enough | too much / too many
I try to walk every day. · I want to go to the gym. · I need to sleep seven hours. · I decided to stop smoking. | I don't sleep enough. · I don't drink enough water.
(enough after a verb, before a noun) | too much + uncountable: too much coffee / time / sugar
too many + countable: too many cups / hours / sweets
⚠ I want to go, not I want go or I want going.  ⚠ I don't drink enough water, not enough of water.
Healthy-habit words: exercise · walk · sleep well · drink water · eat vegetables · take a break · spend time with people · stress
Practise: Complete.  1 I try ______ (walk) every morning.  2 He drinks ______ coffee – six cups!  3 I don't eat ______ vegetables.  4 She needs ______ (take) a break.  5 You spend ______ hours on your phone.
Useful phrases: The truth is… · Me too, honestly. · "I don't have time" is what everybody says. · Maybe that's the problem.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 10.10 to is weak /tə/: I try to‿walk /traɪ tə wɔːk/ · I need to‿sleep · I want to‿go (often "wanna" in fast speech – but say want to in class!). Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A asks the survey questions, B answers using the card – and always gives a reason or an excuse. Then swap.
A | Do you sleep enough?
B | No. I need to sleep __________, but I sleep __________, because __________.
A | Do you exercise?
B | I try to __________. / I want to __________, but __________.
A | Do you drink enough water?
B | __________. I drink too much __________.
A | What do you want to change?
B | I'd like to __________.
Survey card A | Survey card B
sleeps 6 hours (works late) · walks at the weekend only · drinks tea, not water · wants to swim again | sleeps 8 hours · no exercise at all (no time, three children) · drinks a lot of water · wants to walk with a friend
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write one healthy habit and one problem for each person.
 4   Your turn – the real class survey   (9 min)
In groups of four, ask each other five questions about everyday habits: sleep · walking or exercise · water · breaks at work · time outside. Write the answers. Be honest – and kind!

Listener task: what is the biggest problem in this class? What is the best habit?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the class survey report. Answer the questions.
Class 4B – How healthy are we?This week we asked twelve students about their everyday habits. The results are interesting – and a little worrying!Sleep. Nobody sleeps enough. Most students sleep between five and six hours, and only two people sleep seven. The main reasons are work, study and children.Exercise. Half of the class try to walk every day, usually to or from work. Three people go to a gym. Four people do no exercise at all, and they all gave the same reason: "I don't have time."Water and coffee. Most students don't drink enough water, but they drink a lot of coffee – one student drinks five cups a day!What we want to change. Everybody wants to spend less time on their phone, and eight students want to walk more.Our conclusion: we don't need a gym. We need to walk, to drink water and to put our phones in another room.
1  How many students sleep seven hours?
2  What reason did the four people give?
3  What is the conclusion?
Writer's boxA survey report (recycle Unit 2): who you asked → a section for each question (with a title in bold) → what people want to change → a conclusion.Report words: nobody · most students · half of the class · three people · everybody · only two people.  ⚠ Nobody sleeps, Everybody wants – with -s!A good conclusion is one clear sentence that comes from the results, not from your opinion.

10.6  Get well soon!
Function: showing sympathy   ·   Grammar: fixed expressions; I hope…   ·   Pronunciation: warm, gentle intonation
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What do people say when someone is ill? What do they say to the family? What do they bring? Make a class list.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Get well soon! | I hope you feel better soon.
 | 
 | 
b  To the ill person or to the family? Get well soon. · How is he today? · I hope you feel better. · Is there anything I can do? · Take care of yourself.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Where are they?   A at the office   B at Rami's flat   C at the hospital
b  Listen again. 1 What did the doctor say?   2 What do they bring?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 10.11  Visiting Rami
Omar | Come in, come in. He's on the sofa, like a king.
Lina | Rami! How are you feeling?
Rami | Better, thank you. Much better than Thursday.
Maya | And what did the doctor say?
Rami | That you were right, Maya. It wasn't a simple cold. She gave me something and told me to rest for five days.
Maya | Five days! And are you resting?
Rami | Omar took my laptop and hid it.
Omar | It's true. It's in the kitchen, behind the rice.
Lina | Good man. We brought you some soup – my mother made it, so it will probably cure everything.
Rami | Thank you so much. Really.
Maya | And this is from the class. Everybody signed it.
Rami | (reading) "Get well soon – we need you for the football club." Ha!
Lina | Is there anything you need? Shopping? The pharmacy?
Rami | No, Omar is doing everything. Just… don't tell Mr Fouad how well I look.
Omar | Too late. I sent him a photo.

Language boxShowing sympathyTo the person who is ill | To their family | Offering help
How are you feeling? · I'm sorry to hear you're ill. · Get well soon! · I hope you feel better soon. · Take care of yourself. · Don't come back to work too early! | How is he / she today? · I hope he gets better soon. · Please give her my best wishes. · If you need anything, call me. | Is there anything I can do? · Do you need anything from the pharmacy? · Shall I bring you…? · I can do your shopping.
Get well soon is for a card or a message. When you speak, people usually say I hope you feel better soon.
Answering: Thank you so much. · That's very kind of you. · I'm feeling much better, thank you. · Much better than yesterday.
⚠ I hope you feel better (not I hope you will feeling). · Get well, not Get good.
Practise: What do you say?  1 Your colleague has flu. → ______  2 Your neighbour's mother is in hospital. → ______  3 Your friend had an operation and is at home. → ______  4 Your teacher has lost her voice. → ______
Useful phrases: Like a king! · Much better than Thursday. · Good man. · Is there anything you need? · Too late!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 10.12 Sympathy needs a soft, warm voice and a falling tone: I'm so sorry to hear that. ↘ · I hope you feel better soon. ↘ Not fast, not loud. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is visiting or phoning, B is the person on the card. Use three sympathy phrases and one offer of help. Then swap.
A | How are you feeling?
B | __________, thank you. __________.
A | I'm sorry to hear that. What did the doctor say?
B | __________.
A | Is there anything I can do? __________?
B | That's very kind of you. __________.
A | Take care of yourself. And __________!
Card A – you are… | Card B – you are…
at home with flu for three days · feeling a little better · you're bored · your family is doing the shopping | your mother is in hospital after a small operation · she's fine · you're tired because you stay with her every evening
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: tick the phrases you hear. Did the voice sound warm?
☐ How are you feeling? | ☐ I'm sorry to hear that. | ☐ I hope you feel better soon. | ☐ Is there anything I can do? | ☐ That's very kind of you. | ☐ Take care of yourself.

a  Think of someone real who was ill or had a difficult time recently (a relative, a neighbour, a colleague). Tell your partner what happened and what people said and did.
