10.7  Review & Mediate: What does the box say?
Function: explaining instructions to a relative   ·   Recycling: Units 1–10   ·   Exam practice: A2 Key Reading Part 3, Writing Part 6, Listening Part 3
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready – Unit 10 quiz   (6 min)
In teams. Correct the mistakes! 1 I have headache.  2 My feet hurts.  3 You should to rest.  4 I'd like make an appointment.  5 I don't drink enough of water.  6 I hope you will feeling better.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Read the two boxes from the pharmacy.
BOX 1 · THROAT TABLETS | BOX 2 · VITAMIN D
One tablet every 4 hours | One capsule a week
Maximum 6 tablets a day | Take it with food (not on an empty stomach)
Suck slowly – do not swallow whole | The same day every week (e.g. Friday)
For 3–4 days only | Keep away from light
If the pain continues, see a doctor | Ask your doctor before you start
b  Books closed. Listen. Maya explains the two boxes to her grandmother. What does she write on the calendar?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 10.13  Explaining to Teta
Teta | Maya, habibti, the doctor gave me two boxes and I understood nothing.
Maya | Don't worry, Teta. Let's do them one by one. This box is for your throat.
Teta | How many?
Maya | One tablet every four hours – so about four or five a day, but never more than six.
Teta | And do I swallow it with water?
Maya | No! This is important: you suck it slowly, like a sweet. Don't swallow it whole.
Teta | Ah. Like a sweet. And for how many days?
Maya | Three or four days only. If your throat still hurts after that, we go back to the doctor – we don't buy more.
Teta | Fine. And the other one? It's very big!
Maya | That one is easy: one capsule a week. Not every day – once a week.
Teta | Once a week! I'll forget.
Maya | That's why we choose a day. Friday? Everybody remembers Friday. And you take it with food, after lunch.
Teta | Friday after lunch. Write it on my calendar, please.
Maya | (writing) Here: big letters on every Friday. "VITAMIN – AFTER LUNCH."
Teta | God bless your hands.

Language box – explaining instructions to somebody1 One thing at a time: Let's do them one by one. This box is for…2 Say the number simply: every four hours – so about four or five a day3 Warn clearly: This is important: … · Never more than six. · Don't swallow it whole.4 Compare with something they know: You suck it slowly, like a sweet.5 Make it easy to remember: Let's choose a day. Friday? · I'll write it on your calendar.6 Say when to go back: If it still hurts after four days, we go back to the doctor.⚠ When you don't understand something on a box, the answer is always: ask the pharmacist. Never guess for somebody else.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A has the instructions on the card; B is a relative who finds them confusing. Explain, using the six steps in the Language box. Then swap.
Instructions card A – eye drops | Instructions card B – a blood-pressure machine
2 drops in each eye | Sit quietly for 5 minutes first
3 times a day | Put the band on your arm, above the elbow
Do not touch the eye with the bottle | Press the button and do not talk
Throw the bottle away 4 weeks after opening | Write the two numbers in the book, every morning
If your eye is red after 2 days, see a doctor | Take the book to the doctor every month
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the instructions in big simple letters, as the explainer would write them. Did they forget anything?
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Think of real instructions you have explained (or should explain) to someone in your family: a machine, a form, a school letter, a bank app, a bus timetable, a box from the pharmacy. Explain them to your partner using the six steps. Your partner plays the relative – and asks the difficult questions!
 4R   Report – mediation   (6 min)
Tell the class what your partner explained:  Tareq explained the bank app to his father. First you open the app and you put your number. The code comes by message and it only works for two minutes. His father always forgets the code, so Tareq wrote the steps on a card and put it in his wallet.
Listener task: whose explanation was the clearest? What made it clear?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING AND UNIT CHECK
 5   Read – exam practice (A2 Key Reading Part 3)   (7 min)
Read the text and choose the correct answer A, B or C.
The pharmacist in the middle – by Maya NasserPeople think a pharmacist only gives out boxes. In my pharmacy, I spend half my day explaining them.Many of my customers are older people. They come with two or three boxes from the doctor and very small writing. Sometimes they come with a bag of old medicines from last year, and they ask me which one is for which problem. I always do the same thing: I take them one by one, I explain in simple words, and I write big letters on the box with a marker.The hardest part is not the words – it's the habits. One man told me he stops his medicine when he feels better, after two or three days. His daughter buys it, he feels better, he stops, and three weeks later he is ill again. We talked about it for twenty minutes. Now he brings the empty box to show me that he finished it.My grandmother is the same. She is eighty-one and she says, "I took medicine for forty years before you were born." But she lets me write on her calendar, and that is enough.A pharmacist is not only a person who sells medicine. Often we are the person between the doctor and the family – and we have time to explain.

1  What does Maya spend a lot of time doing?   A selling medicine   B explaining instructions   C calling doctors
2  Why do older customers find the boxes difficult?   A The writing is very small.   B They don't speak English.   C The boxes are old.
3  What does Maya do with a marker?   A She writes on her calendar.   B She writes big letters on the box.   C She writes a letter to the doctor.
4  What was the problem with the man?   A He took too many tablets.   B He stopped when he felt better.   C He forgot to buy his medicine.
5  What does Maya say about her grandmother?   A She refuses all help.   B She accepts the calendar idea.   C She can't read.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Write the note Maya puts in her grandmother's bag (use the two boxes from Stage 2).
TETA – YOUR TWO BOXES   Box 1 (throat): One tablet every ______ hours. Never more than ______ a day. ______________ it slowly, like a sweet – do not ______________. For ______ days only.   Box 2 (vitamin): One capsule ______ a week – ______! Take it ______ food, after ______. I wrote it on your calendar.   Important: if your throat ______________ after four days, ______________.   Call me any time, Teta. ❤ Maya · 0933 427 815
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗

 7   Write freely – exam practice (A2 Key Writing Part 6)   (10 min)

 ✓   Unit 10 check   (15 min)
A  Grammar and vocabulary. Choose the correct answer.
1  I've got ___ sore throat.   a) –   b) a   c) the
2  My back ___ when I sit for a long time.   a) hurt   b) hurts   c) is hurt
3  You ___ rest for a few days.   a) should   b) should to   c) shoulds
4  ___ you take a day off?   a) Why don't   b) Why not you   c) Why you don't
5  I'd like ___ an appointment.   a) make   b) to make   c) making
6  Take one tablet ___ a day: morning and evening.   a) two   b) twice   c) second
7  I don't drink ___ water.   a) enough   b) too much   c) too many
8  I hope you ___ better soon.   a) feel   b) will feeling   c) feels
B  Listening (A2 Key Listening Part 3 style). 🔊 Track 10.14 Listen to a pharmacist talking to a customer. Choose A, B or C.
1  How many tablets a day?   A one   B two   C three
2  When must the customer take them?   A before food   B with food   C on an empty stomach
3  How many days?   A three   B five   C seven
4  What must the customer not do?   A drive   B drink coffee   C stop early
5  What should she do if she doesn't feel better?   A take more tablets   B come back to the pharmacy   C see a doctor
🔊 Track 10.14  Audio script (read after the check)
Pharmacist | Right, let me explain this one. It's one tablet in the morning and one in the evening – so two a day, not three.
Customer | Two. With water?
Pharmacist | With water, and always with food – never on an empty stomach, because it isn't comfortable for the stomach.
Customer | And for how long?
Pharmacist | The doctor wrote five days. This is the important part: finish all five days. Many people stop after two days because they feel better, and then the problem comes back.
Customer | I understand. Can I drive?
Pharmacist | Yes, this one is fine for driving. And if you don't feel better after the five days, don't buy another box – go back to the doctor.
Customer | Thank you very much.
C  Writing. Write a message to a friend or colleague who is ill (35+ words). Say you're sorry, ask how they are, and offer one concrete piece of help.

Unit 11  Travel & Holidays
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
In this unit you will…11.1  talk about your holiday plans11.2  book a hotel room11.3  ask about facilities and compare hotels11.4  talk about the weather11.5  write a holiday message11.6  recommend places to a visitor11.7  Review & Mediate: explain a tourist leaflet

11.1  We're going to spend a week in Latakia

c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 11.1  Planning the trip
Maya | So it's decided: we're going to spend a week in Latakia next month.
Lina | A whole week! Who's going?
Maya | Me, Lama, and you if you come. Omar is going to join us for the weekend only – he can't take a week off.
Lina | And Rami?
Maya | Rami says he's going to work. He isn't going to take a holiday this year.
Lina | Of course he isn't. What are we going to do there?
Maya | Nothing! That's the plan. We're going to swim every morning, eat fish, and sleep.
Lina | I love this plan. Where are we going to stay?
Maya | My parents' house is small, so we're going to book a hotel near the sea. I'm going to call two of them tomorrow.
Lina | How are we going to travel?
Maya | By bus. It's four hours, but it's cheap and I can sleep.
Lina | Perfect. I'm going to ask Mr Khaled for the days tomorrow morning.

Language boxGrammar – be going to (plans and decisions you made before now)+ | – | ?
I 'm going to book a hotel. · She 's going to join us. · We 're going to travel by bus. | He isn't going to take a holiday. · We aren't going to fly. | Are you going to stay a week? – Yes, I am. / No, I'm not.  ·  What are you going to do? · Where are we going to stay?
going to + base verb: I'm going to book, not I'm going to booking or I going to book (don't forget am / is / are!).
Compare the three futures you know:
We 're going to book a hotel. = a plan or decision (this unit)  ·  I 'm meeting Maya at seven. = an arrangement with a person, with a time (Unit 7)  ·  The bus leaves at eight. = a timetable (Unit 7)
Future time words: tomorrow · next week / month / summer · in August · at the weekend · soon
Practise: Make sentences with going to.  1 (I / visit / my cousins / next month) ______  2 (she / not take / a holiday) ______  3 (what / you / do / in the summer?) ______  4 (we / travel / by bus) ______  5 (they / not fly / it's too expensive) ______
Useful phrases: So it's decided. · Who's going? · A whole week! · That's the plan. · Of course he isn't.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 11.2 In fast speech going to = /ˈɡənə/ ("gonna"): What are you gonna do? Listen and repeat – but write going to always! Then say it slowly and clearly: We're going to swim every morning.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. Plan the trip on the card together. Use going to at least six times. Then swap.
A | So it's decided: we're going to __________ in __________.
B | Who's going?
A | __________. __________ is going to join us for __________ only.
B | What are we going to do there?
A | We're going to __________ and __________.
B | Where are we going to stay?
A | We're going to __________.
B | And how are we going to travel?
A | __________.
Trip card A | Trip card B
three days in Bludan in August · you + two cousins · your brother joins on Friday · walk in the mountains, drink tea outside, sleep · stay in your uncle's house · travel by car | a weekend in Aleppo in the spring · you + your wife/husband · nobody else · see the citadel, eat kebab, visit the souq · a small hotel near the centre · travel by bus
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform without the book. Listener task: write four plans you hear with going to.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Tell your partner about a real plan: your next holiday, a trip to see family, a free weekend, or something you want to do next year. Say at least five things with going to, including one thing you aren't going to do. Your partner asks three questions. Then change partners and tell it again, faster.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner's plan:  Fadi is going to visit his family in Tartus in August. He's going to stay for ten days with his parents. He's going to swim every day and help his father in the shop. He isn't going to take his laptop – he says he needs a real holiday.
Listener task: whose plan sounds the best? Who has no plans at all?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read Maya's message to the group. Answer the questions.
Latakia – the plan 🌊Hi everyone! Here's the plan for next month, so you can ask for your days off.We're going to travel on Saturday 15 November on the morning bus (it leaves at eight and arrives at about twelve). We're going to come back on Saturday 22nd in the evening.We're going to stay in a small hotel near the sea – I'm going to call two hotels tomorrow and tell you the prices. It's about 300,000 a night for two people, I think.In the week we're going to swim, walk on the sea road, visit my parents one evening, and do absolutely nothing else. Lama is going to show us her favourite fish restaurant.We aren't going to plan every day – that's the point of this holiday!Can everybody confirm by Thursday? I need to book.Maya 🐟
1  When are they going to travel, and when are they coming back?
2  What is Maya going to do tomorrow?
3  What aren't they going to do?
Writer's boxA plan message: a friendly title → why you're writing → travel (date, time, how) → where you're going to stay (+ price) → what you're going to do → what you aren't going to do → what you need from people (confirm by…) → your name.Give dates and times, not just "next month". People can't book days off from "next month".End with one clear action: Can everybody confirm by Thursday?
 6   Write with help   (9 min)

It's about ______ a night.   We're going to ______________, ______________ and ______________. We aren't going to ______________.   Can everybody ______________ by ______________?   ______
 6P   Check together   (5 min)

Capital letters (days, months) and full stops | 
am / is / are + going to + base verb |

 6R   Share   (3 min)
Two or three students read one sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write a plan message to friends or family about a real or invented trip (60 words or more): a holiday, a family visit, a day out, a trip for work or study.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)

11.2  I'd like to book a double room
Function: booking a room   ·   Grammar: I'd like to…; dates (recycle)   ·   Pronunciation: saying dates and numbers clearly
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What do you ask when you book a hotel? What does the hotel ask you? Make two lists.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Have you got a room for three nights? | Booking: 15–22 Nov, 1 double room
 | 
 | 
b  Match: single room · double room · twin room · half board · full board · breakfast included  →  two separate beds · one big bed · one bed for one person · breakfast only · breakfast and dinner · all three meals
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. How many rooms does Maya book?   A one   B two   C three
b  Listen again. 1 How much is a room with a sea view?   2 What does Abu Ziad give them?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 11.3  Phoning Hotel Al-Bahr
Abu Ziad | Hotel Al-Bahr, good morning.
Maya | Good morning. I'd like to book two rooms, please, from Saturday the 15th of November to Saturday the 22nd.
Abu Ziad | Seven nights. What kind of rooms?
Maya | One double and one twin, please. Have you got anything with a sea view?
Abu Ziad | Let me look… Yes, I have two rooms on the second floor, both with a balcony and a sea view.
Maya | How much are they?
Abu Ziad | The rooms with a sea view are 350,000 a night. Without a view they're 280,000. Breakfast is included.
Maya | And for seven nights?
Abu Ziad | For a week I can give you a better price: 320,000 a night for the sea view rooms. That's for both rooms together… no, sorry – that's for each room.
Maya | OK. Is breakfast really included?
Abu Ziad | My wife makes it. Nobody leaves hungry.
Maya | (laughs) Then we'll take the sea view. Can I pay when we arrive?
Abu Ziad | Of course. Can I take your name and a phone number?
Maya | Maya Nasser, 0933 427 815. What time can we check in?
Abu Ziad | From two o'clock. And check-out is at twelve. See you in November!

Language boxBooking a roomYou | The hotel
I'd like to book a room, please. · from the 15th to the 22nd · Have you got anything with a sea view? · How much is it per night? · Is breakfast included? · Can I pay when we arrive? · What time can we check in? | What kind of room? · For how many nights? · Let me look… · I can give you a better price. · Can I take your name? · Check-in is from two, check-out is at twelve.
Rooms: a single room (one person) · a double room (one big bed) · a twin room (two beds) · a room with a balcony / a sea view
Prices: 350,000 a night (= per night) · breakfast included · half board (breakfast + dinner) · full board (all meals)
Dates: from Saturday the 15th to Saturday the 22nd = seven nights. ⚠ Count the nights, not the days!
Practise: Complete.  1 I'd like to book a room ______ three nights.  2 It's 300,000 ______ night.  3 We're staying ______ Monday ______ Friday.  4 Is breakfast ______?  5 What time can we ______ in?
Useful phrases: What kind of rooms? · Let me look… · Nobody leaves hungry. · Then we'll take… · See you in November!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 11.4 Say dates and big numbers slowly and repeat them back: from the fifteenth to the twenty-second · three hundred and twenty thousand a night. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs, back to back. A phones the hotel; B is the receptionist with the card. Book the room and write down the details. Then swap.
B | __________, good morning.
A | Good morning. I'd like to book __________, please, from __________ to __________.
B | __________ nights. What kind of room?
A | __________. Have you got anything with __________?
B | Let me look… __________.
A | How much is it per night?
B | __________. Breakfast __________.
A | Can I pay __________? What time can we check in?
B | Check-in is from __________ and check-out is at __________.
Hotel card A – Hotel Al-Qalaa, Aleppo | Hotel card B – Cham Guesthouse, Bludan
single 180,000 · double 250,000 · with balcony +40,000 | double 200,000 · family room (4 people) 350,000
breakfast included · dinner 60,000 extra | half board only · no single rooms

 3R   Perform   (5 min)

 4   Your turn   (9 min)

b  Phone again – something has changed! (You need one more night / one more person / you have to cancel one room.) Sort it out politely.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class:  I booked a double room in Aleppo from the 3rd to the 6th – three nights, 250,000 a night with breakfast. Then my brother decided to come, so I phoned again and changed it to a family room for 350,000. Check-in is at two.
Listener task: who got the best price? Who had the most difficult hotel?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the booking email and the hotel's reply. Answer the questions.
1 · Maya → Hotel Al-BahrSubject: Booking request – 15–22 November (2 rooms)Dear Sir,I would like to book two rooms at your hotel from Saturday 15 November to Saturday 22 November – seven nights.We would like one double room and one twin room, both with a sea view if possible. We would also like to know if we can pay when we arrive.My phone number is 0933 427 815.Thank you very much.Best regards, Maya Nasser2 · Hotel Al-Bahr → MayaSubject: RE: Booking request – 15–22 November (2 rooms)Dear Ms Nasser,Thank you for your email. If you arrive early, you can leave your bags with us.We look forward to welcoming you.Best regards, Ziad Mansour · Hotel Al-Bahr · Latakia · 041 556 200
1  How many people are travelling?
2  What three questions does Maya ask?
3  What can they do if they arrive before 2 pm?
Writer's boxA booking email: subject with dates → Dear Sir / Madam, → what you want to book (dates + number of nights) → what kind of rooms + how many people → your questions (price, breakfast, payment) → your phone number → Best regards, + name.The hotel's reply: thank you → confirm everything → price and what's included → check-in / check-out → one helpful extra → we look forward to welcoming you.Always write the dates twice: from 15 November to 22 November – seven nights. This stops expensive mistakes!

11.3  Is there a lift?
Function: asking about facilities and comparing   ·   Grammar: Is there…? / Are there…? / Does it have…?; comparatives (recycle)   ·   Pronunciation: linking Is there…
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What is important for you in a hotel? Put these in order 1–8: price · breakfast · Wi-Fi · a view · air conditioning · a quiet room · near the sea / the centre · parking
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Is there a lift? | Facilities: Wi-Fi, parking, restaurant
 | 
 | 
b  Is there…? or Are there…?  ______ a restaurant? · ______ any shops near the hotel? · ______ air conditioning in the rooms? · ______ towels in the room?
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Which hotel do they choose?   A Al-Bahr   B Blue Coast   C they can't decide
b  Listen again. 1 Which hotel is cheaper?   2 Why is the other one better for Lama?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 11.5  Two hotels
Lina | OK, Maya, you called both hotels. Tell us.
Maya | Hotel Al-Bahr first. It's a small family hotel on the sea road. Is there a lift? No. Our rooms are on the second floor.
Lama | Second floor is fine.
Maya | There's a balcony with a sea view, breakfast is included, and it's 320,000 a night.
Lina | And the other one?
Maya | The Blue Coast is newer and bigger. It has a lift, a swimming pool and a restaurant. But it's 480,000 a night and breakfast isn't included.
Lama | Does it have Wi-Fi? I have to send my university work.
Maya | Both have Wi-Fi, but Abu Ziad said, "The internet is good when the electricity is good." Honest man!
Lina | Ha! Is the Blue Coast nearer the centre?
Maya | Yes, it's five minutes from the restaurants. Al-Bahr is quieter, but it's twenty minutes on foot.
Lama | I prefer quiet. And I'd rather spend the money on fish than on a swimming pool.
Lina | Agreed. Al-Bahr. And we can walk twenty minutes – it's a holiday, not a meeting!

Language boxAsking about facilitiesIs there…? (singular) | Are there…? (plural) | Does it have…? / Has it got…?
Is there a lift / a pool / air conditioning / parking? | Are there any shops / restaurants near the hotel? | Does it have Wi-Fi? · Has it got a balcony?
Hotel facilities: a lift · a swimming pool · a restaurant · parking · air conditioning (AC) · heating · Wi-Fi · a balcony · a sea view · a safe · hot water 24 hours
Comparing (Unit 5): It's cheaper than the other one. · The Blue Coast is newer and bigger. · Al-Bahr is quieter. · It's nearer the centre. · The best thing about Al-Bahr is the breakfast.
Practise: Write the questions.  1 (a lift?) ______  2 (any shops near the hotel?) ______  3 (Wi-Fi in the rooms?) ______  4 (parking?) ______  5 (the rooms / air conditioning?) ______
Useful phrases: Tell us. · …first. · Honest man! · I prefer quiet. · It's a holiday, not a meeting!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 11.6 Link the words: Is‿there‿a lift? /ɪzðərə/ · Are‿there‿any shops? /ɑːðərəni/. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A has the two hotels on the card. B asks six questions about facilities, then they choose together – and give two reasons. Then swap roles with the other card.

Where | in the centre, noisy street | 15 min from the centre, quiet garden
Facilities | lift, AC, Wi-Fi, no breakfast | no lift, AC, Wi-Fi, breakfast included, parking

 3R   Perform   (5 min)

HOTEL AL-BAHR, LATAKIA  ⭐⭐⭐  Our review: 8/10Hotel Al-Bahr is a small family hotel on the sea road, twenty minutes on foot from the centre of Latakia. The Mansour family has had it for thirty years, and Abu Ziad knows every guest by name after one day.The rooms are simple but very clean. Most have a balcony and a sea view, and all of them have air conditioning and good hot water. There isn't a lift, so ask for the first floor if you don't want stairs.Breakfast is included and it is the best thing about this hotel: fresh bread, olives, cheese, eggs, and tea on the terrace. Nobody leaves hungry.What isn't here: there's no swimming pool, no restaurant in the evening, and the Wi-Fi is slow when the electricity is weak.Price: from 280,000 SYP a night (320,000 with a sea view).Perfect for: families and people who want a quiet holiday near the sea.  Not for: people who want a modern hotel with a pool.
1  How far is the hotel from the centre?
2  What is the best thing about the hotel?
3  Who is it not for?
Writer's boxA hotel review: name + stars + your score → where it is + who runs it → The rooms → Breakfast / food → What isn't here (be honest!) → Price → Perfect for… / Not for…Use there is / there are and it has for facilities, and comparatives when you compare.A review people trust always says what is missing. "Everything is perfect" helps nobody.

11.4  It's going to rain
Function: talking about the weather   ·   Grammar: going to from evidence; weather adjectives   ·   Pronunciation: /eə/ there, wear, air
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What's the weather like today? What is it like in your city in each season? Make a class weather map.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
It's going to rain. | Tomorrow: sunny, 24°
 | 
 | 
b  Weather words: sunny · cloudy · rainy · windy · hot · cold · warm · cool · wet · dry. Which three describe today?
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What is the problem?   A the weather is bad in Latakia   B the bus is cancelled   C the hotel is full
b  Listen again. 1 What's the weather going to be like at the weekend?   2 What does Lama decide to take?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 11.7  The day before
Lama | Maya, have you seen the sky? Those clouds are very dark.
Maya | I know. It's going to rain, I think.
Lama | In November, in Latakia? Of course it is. I checked the forecast: Saturday and Sunday are going to be wet and windy, and then it's going to be sunny all week.
Maya | So two bad days and five good ones. That's fine.
Lama | But what are we going to do on Saturday if it rains all day?
Maya | We're going to sit on the balcony with tea and watch the sea. Rain on the sea is beautiful.
Lama | You always have an answer. OK – then I'm going to take a jacket and my book.
Maya | Take an umbrella too. And don't take five pairs of shoes like last time.
Lama | That was ONE extra pair!
Maya | It was four. How cold is it going to be?
Lama | About eighteen in the day, twelve at night. Warmer than here.
Maya | Perfect. Sea, rain, tea, books. I can't wait.

Language boxGrammar – going to for predictions with evidenceWhen you can see something now, use going to: Look at those clouds – it's going to rain. · The sky is clear – it's going to be a beautiful day.Talking about the weather:Asking | Answering | Temperature
What's the weather like? · What's it going to be like tomorrow? · How cold is it going to be? | It's sunny / cloudy / windy / wet. · It's raining. (now) · It's going to be cold at the weekend. | It's about eighteen degrees. · Eighteen in the day, twelve at night. · It's warmer than here.
⚠ It's raining = now, it's falling.  It's rainy = that kind of weather.  It's going to rain = not yet, but soon!
Seasons: in spring · in summer · in autumn · in winter
Practise: Complete with going to and the verb.  1 Look at the sky – it ______ (snow)!  2 The clouds are black. I think it ______ (rain).  3 It ______ (be) very hot tomorrow – forty degrees.  4 Take a jacket. It ______ (be) cold tonight.  5 It ______ (not / rain) this week.
Useful phrases: Have you seen the sky? · I checked the forecast. · You always have an answer. · I can't wait.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 11.8 /eə/: there · wear · air · where · chair · their. Listen and repeat: Wear a jacket – there's cold air out there.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A and B are travelling tomorrow. Look at the forecast card and decide what to take and what to do on the bad days. Then swap cards.
Forecast card A – Aleppo, next week | Forecast card B – Bludan, next week
Mon: sunny, 22° | Mon: cold and windy, 8°
Tue: cloudy, 19° | Tue: snow!, 2°
Wed: rain all day, 15° | Wed: sunny but cold, 6°
Thu–Fri: sunny, 24° | Thu–Fri: cloudy, 10°
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write three things they're going to take and one plan for a bad day.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
a  Describe the weather in your city in the four seasons to your partner. Which is your favourite? Why?
b  Now: your partner is coming to visit you next month. What's the weather going to be like? What should they bring? What are you going to do if it rains?
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class:  Tareq says Suwayda is beautiful in spring – warm in the day, cool at night. In winter it's very cold and sometimes it snows. If I visit him in December, I'm going to need a heavy coat, and he says we're going to sit by the stove and eat all day.
Listener task: which city has the best weather? The worst?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the weather page. Answer the questions.
LATAKIA – THIS WEEK |  |  |

And the message Lama sent with it:
Hi everyone! Here's the forecast for our week. 🌦The first two days are going to be wet – Saturday is the worst: rain, wind and only 17 degrees. But from Monday it's going to be sunny all week, between 21 and 23 in the day.So: bring a jacket and an umbrella for the first two days, and your swimming things for the rest of the week. 2  What should people bring?
3  What are they going to do on Saturday?
Writer's boxA weather message: the bad days first (be honest) → the good days (with temperatures) → what to bring → a plan for the bad weather → a friendly ending.Give numbers: only 17 degrees, between 21 and 23. "Quite warm" means nothing to somebody packing a bag!Use going to for the forecast and for your plans.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Hi everyone! Here's the forecast for ______________.   The first ______ days are going to be ______________ – ______ is the worst: ______________. But from ______ it's going to be ______________, between ______ and ______ degrees.   So: bring ______________ for ______________, and ______________ for ______________.   And don't worry about ______. We're going to ______________.
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗

going to + base verb for the forecast |

 7P   Respond   (5 min)

11.5  We're having a great time!
Function: writing a holiday message   ·   Grammar: present continuous (now) + past simple (yesterday)   ·   Pronunciation: contractions in fast speech
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  When you travel, who do you send photos and messages to? What do you write? Make a class list.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
We're having a great time! | Wish you were here! 🌊
 | 
 | 
b  Now or yesterday? I'm sitting on the balcony. · We swam this morning. · The sun is going down. · We ate the best fish.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What is Maya doing while she talks?   A swimming   B sitting outside   C walking to the hotel
b  Listen again. 1 What did they do yesterday?   2 Why is Rami laughing?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 11.9  A video call from Latakia
Rami | Maya! Where are you? I can hear the sea.
Maya | I'm sitting on the hotel balcony. Look – can you see?
Rami | Oh, that's beautiful. And warm?
Maya | Twenty-two degrees. Lama is swimming right now – in November!
Rami | And how is it? Tell me everything.
Maya | It's perfect. We're having a wonderful time. Yesterday we walked on the sea road for two hours and we ate at a tiny fish restaurant – the best fish of my life, Rami. Lama's friend took us.
Rami | Stop. I'm eating a sandwich at my desk.
Maya | (laughing) This morning we visited my parents, and my mother gave us food for three days.
Rami | Of course she did.
Maya | Tonight we're going to the old port. What are you doing?
Rami | I'm working. Then I'm going to work. And after that – more work.
Maya | Rami. Next year you're coming with us. That isn't a question.
Rami | (laughing) Yes, boss.

Language boxGrammar – mixing the tenses in a holiday messageNOW (present continuous) | YESTERDAY / THIS MORNING (past simple) | TONIGHT / TOMORROW (going to / present continuous)
I'm sitting on the balcony. · Lama is swimming. · We're having a wonderful time. | Yesterday we walked on the sea road and ate at a fish restaurant. · This morning we visited my parents. | Tonight we're going to the old port. · Tomorrow we're going to take a boat.
A good holiday message uses all three: what you're doing now, what you did, and what you're going to do next.
Holiday phrases: We're having a wonderful / great time. · The weather is perfect. · It's the best … of my life. · Wish you were here! · See you soon!
Practise: Choose the correct form.  1 Right now I (sit / 'm sitting) in a café.  2 Yesterday we (visit / visited) the old city.  3 Tonight we ('re going / go) to a concert.  4 The sun (shines / is shining) and it's beautiful.  5 This morning I (swam / swim) for an hour.
Useful phrases: Tell me everything. · Stop – I'm at my desk! · Of course she did. · That isn't a question. · Yes, boss.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 11.10 Contractions in fast speech: we're having /wɪəˈhævɪŋ/ · I'm sitting · she's swimming. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is on holiday in the place on the card, B is at home. Make a video call. A must say three things: now, yesterday, tonight. Then swap.
B | Where are you? What can I hear?
A | I'm __________. Look – can you see?
B | How is it? Tell me everything.
A | It's __________. We're having __________. Yesterday we __________ and we __________.
B | __________!
A | This morning we __________. Tonight we're __________.
B | And what's the weather like?
A | __________.
Holiday card A – Aleppo | Holiday card B – Bludan
now: in a café in the old souq | now: on the balcony, mountains everywhere
yesterday: visited the citadel, walked 5 hours | yesterday: walked to the village, drank tea with a family

 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write one thing for now, one for yesterday and one for tonight.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Imagine you are on holiday today – anywhere you like (a real place you know or a dream place). Your partner phones you. Tell them: where you are and what you're doing now, what you did yesterday and this morning, and what you're going to do tonight. Your partner asks four questions. Then swap.
 4R   Report   (6 min)

Listener task: whose holiday sounds the best? Who is doing nothing at all?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.

1  Who are the two texts for? How is the language different?
2  What are they going to do tomorrow?

Writer's boxA holiday message: greeting → where you are and what you're doing NOW → what you DID yesterday / this morning → what you're GOING TO do tonight / tomorrow → a warm line for the reader (We're thinking of you) → your name.A postcard to an older relative is warmer and more complete. A group message is shorter, with jokes and emojis.The best holiday messages give one small detail, not a list: Mum gave us food for three days is better than "we had a nice time".

11.6  You should visit the old city
Function: recommending places   ·   Grammar: should (recycle) + superlatives (recycle)   ·   Pronunciation: stress on the important word
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  A foreign visitor has three days in Syria. Where should they go? Make a class top five.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
You should visit the Old City. | Three days in Damascus: a short guide
 | 
 | 
b  Finish: The most beautiful place in my city is ______. · The best food is ______. · The thing visitors always love is ______.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. When is Mr Lang coming back?   A next month   B in the spring   C next year
b  Listen again. 1 What does Lina recommend for the first day?   2 What does she say about the market?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 11.11  A visitor is coming back
Mr Lang | Lina, good news – I'm coming back to Damascus in April, and this time I'm going to stay five days, not two.
Lina | That's wonderful! April is the best month – warm, but not hot.
Mr Lang | So, tell me honestly: what should I do with five days?
Lina | Two days in Damascus, and then you should go outside the city. Here in Damascus you should start early in the Old City – before nine, when the streets are empty and the light is beautiful.
Mr Lang | And the souq?
Lina | Souq Al-Hamidiyah, yes, but go in the afternoon, when it's full of people. That's the real experience. And eat the ice cream – everybody does.
Mr Lang | What about outside Damascus?
Lina | Maaloula is only an hour away, and Bosra has the most beautiful Roman theatre I've seen. If you have one more day, Krak des Chevaliers is amazing, but it's a long drive.
Mr Lang | And where should I eat?
Lina | Not in the hotel! You should eat in the small places in the Old City. Ask for fattoush and makdous. And shawarma from a street place, not a restaurant.
Mr Lang | You're a very good guide. Shall I book a hotel in the centre?
Lina | Yes – and ask for a room away from the street. Damascus is beautiful, but it isn't quiet!

Language boxRecommending placesRecommending | Warning / practical advice | Giving a reason
You should visit / go / try / eat… · You must see… · Don't miss… · The best thing is… · My favourite place is… | Go early – it's quieter. · Don't eat in the hotel. · Ask for a room away from the street. · It's a long drive. | …because it's the oldest in the city. · …when the light is beautiful. · It's only an hour away.
Superlatives (Unit 5): the best month · the most beautiful theatre · the oldest café · the cheapest place to eat
Good recommendations have three parts: the place + when / how to go + why.  Go to the souq in the afternoon, when it's full of people – that's the real experience.
Practise: Make recommendations with the three parts.  1 (the Old City / early / quiet)  2 (the park / Friday / families and music)  3 (the fish restaurant / book first / very popular)
Useful phrases: Tell me honestly. · That's the real experience. · Everybody does. · You're a very good guide. · …, but it isn't quiet!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 11.12 Stress the important word: Go in the AFternoon. · Don't eat in the HOtel. · It's the BEST month. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is a visitor with the card, B recommends places in their own city. Give at least four recommendations with a reason, and two warnings. Then swap.
Visitor card A | Visitor card B
two days only · loves old buildings and photography · doesn't like crowds · travelling alone | four days · with two children (6 and 9) · likes food and markets · can't walk long distances
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write the four places and one reason for each. Is the advice right for that visitor?
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
a  Your partner is coming to your city or village for three days. Plan it with them: what to see, when to go, where to eat, what to avoid. Give a reason every time.
b  Now your partner does the same for their place. Which trip do you want to make?
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class your partner's recommendations:  Fadi says I should go to Tartus in June, not August, because August is too crowded. I should eat fish at the port, but in a small place, not the big restaurants. And I shouldn't drive – he says the parking is impossible.
Listener task: which place do you want to visit now?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the guide Lina wrote for Mr Lang. Answer the questions.
FIVE DAYS IN SYRIA – a short guide for PeterDay 1 · Old Damascus. Start early – before nine the streets are empty and the light is perfect for photos. Walk down Straight Street to Bab Sharqi. In the afternoon, go to Souq Al-Hamidiyah when it's full of people, and don't leave without the ice cream.Day 2 · Museums and modern Damascus. The National Museum in the morning (closed on Tuesdays!), then walk in Shaalan and have coffee where the students go.Day 3 · Maaloula. Only an hour from the city. Go early, take a hat, and stay for lunch in the village.Day 4 · Bosra. Two hours south. The Roman theatre is the most beautiful I've ever seen. It's hot, so take water.Day 5 · Rest and the market. Buy your presents on the last day, not the first – you don't want to carry them for a week!Eating: small places, not hotels. Fattoush, makdous, shawarma from a street place. Ask the people around you what they are eating and order that.One last thing: people are going to invite you for coffee. Say yes. That's the best part of Syria.
1  Why should Peter start early on Day 1?
2  Why should he buy presents on the last day?
3  What is "the best part of Syria" for Lina?
Writer's boxA short guide: a title with the number of days → a section for each day (place + when/how + why) → a food section → one last personal tip.Each day needs one main place, not five. Visitors who try to see everything see nothing.The best guides include practical warnings: closed on Tuesdays · take water · take a hat · don't carry presents for a week.End with something personal – that's what a guidebook can't say.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)

 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters (places!) and full stops | 
should + base verb; superlatives correct | 
Each day: place + when / how + why | 
A food section and a personal last tip | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)

 7P   Respond   (5 min)

11.7  Review & Mediate: What does the leaflet say?
Function: explaining a tourist leaflet   ·   Recycling: Units 1–11   ·   Exam practice: A2 Key Reading Part 2, Writing Part 6, Listening Part 4
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready – Unit 11 quiz   (6 min)
In teams. Correct the mistakes! 1 I going to visit my uncle.  2 We're going to booking a hotel.  3 Is there any shops near the hotel?  4 Look at the clouds – it will rain now.  5 Yesterday we are walking on the sea road.  6 You should to visit the Old City.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Read the leaflet from the hotel reception.
LATAKIA – BOAT TRIPS WITH ABU JAMALTrip 1 · The coast (2 hours) · Every day at 10:00 and 16:00 · 60,000 SYP per person · children under 10 half priceTrip 2 · Sunset trip (1 hour) · Every day at 18:00 · 40,000 SYP · tea and coffee includedTrip 3 · The island (all day, 7 hours) · Fridays only, 08:00 · 150,000 SYP · bring your own food and waterInformation: All trips leave from the old port, next to the fish market. Book one day before at the hotel reception or call 041 338 900. Minimum 6 people – if there are fewer, we change the trip to the next day. Life jackets for everybody. Trips do not go if the weather is bad – in that case you get your money back.
b  Books closed. Listen. Lama explains the leaflet to her friends. Which trip do they choose, and why?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 11.13  Choosing a boat trip
Lama | OK, I asked at reception. There are three boat trips.
Lina | Tell us.
Lama | The first one goes along the coast. It takes two hours and it's sixty thousand each. There are two every day – ten in the morning and four in the afternoon.
Maya | And the others?
Lama | The second is a sunset trip – only one hour, forty thousand, and they give you tea and coffee. It leaves at six.
Lina | Oh, that sounds beautiful.
Lama | The third is the big one: all day to the island, seven hours, a hundred and fifty thousand. But it's only on Fridays and you have to take your own food.
Maya | Friday is our last full day. Seven hours on a boat…
Lina | With no food unless we carry it. Hmm.
Lama | And one more thing: you have to book one day before, and they need at least six people. If there are fewer, they move the trip to the next day.
Maya | Then the island trip is risky – we're going home on Saturday.
Lina | Let's do the sunset trip. One hour, beautiful light, tea – and if the weather is bad, we get our money back.
Lama | Agreed. I'm going to book it for Wednesday.

Language box – explaining a leaflet and decidingExplain each option with how long · how much · when:2 hours · 60,000 · 10:00 and 16:00 → It takes two hours and it's sixty thousand each. There are two every day.Explain the conditions – this is where people make mistakes:Minimum 6 people → They need at least six people. If there are fewer, they move it to the next day.   Book 1 day before → You have to book one day before.   No trips in bad weather – money back → If the weather is bad, the trip doesn't go and you get your money back.Then decide together with reasons: Then the island trip is risky, because… · Let's do the sunset trip – one hour, beautiful light, and…Useful phrases: I asked at reception. · The first one… the second… the third… · And one more thing: … · …is risky. · Agreed.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in groups of three. A has the leaflet card (don't show it!). A explains the three options; B and C ask questions and then the group decides, with reasons. Then change roles with the other card.
Leaflet card A – Bus tours, Aleppo | Leaflet card B – Cooking courses, Damascus
Tour 1 · City centre, 3 h, daily 9:00, 50,000 | Course 1 · Mezze, 3 h, Saturdays 10:00, 90,000
Tour 2 · Citadel + museum, 5 h, Tue & Thu 9:00, 90,000 (entrance included) | Course 2 · Sweets, 2 h, Mondays 17:00, 70,000
Tour 3 · Villages outside the city, all day, Fridays 8:00, 140,000 (lunch included) | Course 3 · Full day: market + cooking + lunch, Fridays 9:00, 200,000
Book 2 days before · minimum 8 people · no tours in the rain | Book 3 days before · maximum 6 people · you take the food home
 3R   Perform   (5 min)

 4   Your turn   (9 min)

 4R   Report – mediation   (6 min)

The middle one is thirty-five thousand with fifteen gigabytes and free calls at night. The big one is sixty thousand with unlimited internet. I chose the middle one because I don't need unlimited, but five gigabytes isn't enough for me.
Listener task: did your partner explain all the conditions? What did they forget?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.

🔊 Track 11.14  Audio script (read after the check)
Man | Last month we spent a week at a small hotel on the sea road in Latakia – not in the centre, about twenty minutes on foot. We took the bus from Damascus; four hours, but it was cheap and comfortable. The rooms were simple and clean, nothing special, and there isn't a swimming pool, which my children didn't like. But the breakfast! Every morning the owner's wife made fresh bread, eggs, olives and cheese on the terrace, and nobody left the table hungry. That's the thing I remember. The only real problem is the stairs – there's no lift, and our room was on the third floor, which was hard for my mother. Next year we're going back, but we're going to ask for a room on the first floor.

Unit 12  Celebrations & Customs
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
In this unit you will…12.1  describe a celebration in your country12.2  read an invitation card and reply to it12.3  give good wishes and congratulations12.4  ask and give advice about presents12.5  tell the story of a day you remember12.6  explain a custom to a visitor12.7  Review & Mediate: explain an invitation and a programme

In this unit you meet again…Hiba's cousin's wedding in Homs (Unit 7) – Omar photographs the engagement party and the class is invited to the wedding.Mr Peter Lang comes to Damascus in April (Unit 11) and his visit lands on a celebration.   ·   Jana (Ziad's daughter) is four this year.

12.1  What do people do at Eid?
Function: describing a celebration   ·   Grammar: present simple for customs + before / after / when   ·   Pronunciation: weak do in questions
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  Which celebrations do you know in your country? Make a class list: religious days, national days, family days.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
We visit our relatives. | Eid: three days of visits
 | 
 | 
b  Which words go with a celebration? visit · cook · wear · give · buy · clean · invite · wait · sleep · phone. Choose five and say why.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Who is Lina talking to?   A her sister   B a visitor from another country   C her manager
b  Listen again. 1 What do people do the day before?   2 Who visits who first?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 12.1  Explaining Eid to a visitor
Mr Lang | Lina, can I ask you something? My colleague said "Eid Mubarak" to me yesterday, and I didn't know what to answer.
Lina | (laughs) You answer "Eid Mubarak" too! It means something like "a blessed feast".
Mr Lang | And what do people actually do?
Lina | Well, before the Eid everybody cleans the house and buys new clothes, especially for the children. My mother cooks for two days.
Mr Lang | Two days!
Lina | For about forty people. On the first morning, after the prayer, the men come home and we have breakfast together. Then the visits start.
Mr Lang | Who visits who?
Lina | The young people visit the older people first – grandparents, uncles, aunts. When we arrive, we say "Eid Mubarak", we sit, we eat sweets, we drink coffee, and after twenty minutes we go to the next house.
Mr Lang | Twenty minutes? That's a short visit.
Lina | Because there are ten more houses! And the children get money – "eidiyeh". That's the best part when you're seven.
Mr Lang | And when it finishes?
Lina | Three days later everybody sleeps for a week. Come in April – you're going to see all of it.

Language boxGrammar – present simple for customsWe use the present simple for what people always do: Everybody cleans the house. · The young people visit the older people first. · Children get money.Useful subjects for customs: people · everybody · we · the family · the children · nobody.  ⚠ everybody cleans, people clean.Grammar – before / after / when + present simplebefore | after | when
Before the Eid, everybody cleans the house. · Before we visit, we buy sweets. | After the prayer, the men come home. · After twenty minutes, we go to the next house. | When we arrive, we say "Eid Mubarak". · When the visits finish, everybody sleeps!
⚠ Use the present simple after these words, not the future: When we arrive, not when we will arrive.
Celebration words: a celebration · a feast · a holiday · the prayer · visits · sweets · new clothes · presents · to invite · to celebrate
Practise: Join the sentences with the word in brackets.  1 We clean the house. The guests arrive. (before)  2 We eat. We drink coffee. (after)  3 The children see their grandfather. They kiss his hand. (when)  4 My mother cooks for two days. The Eid starts. (before)
Useful phrases: Can I ask you something? · It means something like… · What do people actually do? · That's the best part. · Come in April!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 12.2 In questions, do is short and weak: What d'you do? /wɒdjəduː/ · What do people eat? Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is a visitor from another country; B explains the celebration on the card. Use before, after and when at least once each. Then swap cards.
A | Can I ask you something? What is __________?
B | It's __________. It means something like __________.
A | And what do people actually do?
B | Before __________, everybody __________.
A | And on the day?
B | When __________, we __________. After that, __________.
A | Who visits who?
B | __________.
A | That sounds wonderful. How long does it last?
B | __________.
Celebration card A – Mother's Day (21 March) | Celebration card B – a new baby
before: children buy flowers or a small present; schools prepare songs | before: the family prepares the house; the grandmother comes to help
on the day: families eat together; people phone their mothers; the radio plays old songs | when visitors arrive: they bring sweets or something for the baby; they say "Mabrouk"
after: people put photos on the internet | after: the mother rests, the grandmother cooks for everybody
one day only | the visits continue for two or three weeks
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform without the book. Listener task: write three customs you hear – one with before, one with when, one with after.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Choose a real celebration you know well – a religious feast, a national day, a wedding, a graduation, Mother's Day, or a family custom in your own house. Explain it to your partner: what happens before, what happens on the day, who visits who, what people eat and wear, and what children like most. Your partner asks four questions. Then swap.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class about your partner's celebration:  Nour told me about the olive harvest in her village. Before it starts, the whole family comes from the city, even the cousins who live far away. When they arrive, everybody works – the children carry the small boxes. After the day in the field, they eat together outside, and somebody always plays music. She says it isn't a holiday, but it feels like one.
Listener task: which celebration would you most like to see? Which one is the most similar to yours?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the text from an international website. Answer the questions.
EID AL-FITR IN SYRIA – what a visitor should knowEid al-Fitr comes after the month of Ramadan and it lasts three days. It is the biggest family celebration of the year, and the whole country stops.Before the Eid. In the last days of Ramadan everybody cleans the house, and the markets stay open until very late. Families buy new clothes, especially for the children, and many people make or buy special sweets.The first morning. After the morning prayer, families have breakfast together. Then the visits begin: young people visit older relatives first – grandparents, uncles and aunts.The visits. When guests arrive, they say "Eid Mubarak". They sit for fifteen or twenty minutes, eat sweets and drink coffee, and then they go to the next house. Children receive a small amount of money, which is called "eidiyeh".For a visitor. You don't have to bring a present, but sweets are always welcome. If somebody invites you, say yes – and don't eat too much in the first house!
1  How long does Eid al-Fitr last?
2  Why do people go to the market late at night before the Eid?
3  What advice does the text give to a visitor?
Writer's boxA short text about a celebration: name + when it is + how long it lasts → Before → The day itself → What people do / eat / say → For a visitor (one practical tip).Write the special words in your language and then explain them: "eidiyeh" – a small amount of money for children. Don't leave a word the reader can't understand.Use the present simple all the way through: this is what happens every year.

12.2  You are invited
Function: reading an invitation and replying   ·   Grammar: formal and friendly register; We are pleased to invite you…   ·   Pronunciation: dates and times in a formal reply
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What information must an invitation always give? Make a class list of five things.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
We'd love to come. | RSVP by 15 October
 | 
 | 
b  Friendly or formal? Come at eight! · We are pleased to invite you · See you there 😊 · We look forward to seeing you · Bring the kids
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What is the invitation for?   A a birthday   B a wedding   C a graduation
b  Listen again. 1 Where and when is it?   2 Why is Lina worried about the time?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 12.3  An invitation arrives
Hiba | Look what came today! The cards for my cousin's wedding. One is for the class.
Lina | For all of us? Read it!
Hiba | "Mr and Mrs Fouad Dabbagh are pleased to invite you to the wedding of their daughter Rasha and Mr Samer Khoury, on Friday the 24th of October at six o'clock in the evening, at the Rose Hall, Homs. RSVP by the 17th."
Lina | Rose Hall! That's a beautiful place. And what does RSVP mean?
Hiba | It means "please answer" – they need to know how many people are coming, because of the food.
Lina | Six o'clock in Homs… the bus takes two hours. We have to leave at three.
Hiba | Three at the latest. And nothing in Syria starts at six.
Lina | (laughs) True. But we arrive at six.
Omar | I'm going earlier – I'm photographing the engagement party in the afternoon.
Hiba | So can everybody tell me by Thursday? I have to send one answer for all of us.
Lina | Write "with pleasure" from me. Should we take a present?
Hiba | We'll talk about that. One thing at a time!

Language boxReading an invitation – the five thingsWho | What | When | Where | Answer
Mr and Mrs Fouad Dabbagh | the wedding of their daughter Rasha and Mr Samer Khoury | Friday the 24th of October at six o'clock | the Rose Hall, Homs | RSVP by the 17th
Formal invitation language: We are pleased to invite you to… · on … at … · We look forward to seeing you. · RSVP (= please answer) by …
Friendly invitation language: We're having a party for… · Come at eight! · Bring the children. · Let me know by Thursday. · See you there!
Answering:
Accepting (formal) | Accepting (friendly) | Saying no (always: thank + reason + good wishes)
Thank you for your kind invitation. We will be pleased to attend. · With pleasure. | Of course we're coming! · We'd love to. · Count me in. · Try and stop me! 😄 | Thank you very much for the invitation. I'm afraid I can't come, because… I hope you have a wonderful day.
Practise: Formal (F) or friendly (f)?  1 Come whenever you like. ___  2 We look forward to seeing you. ___  3 Bring the kids! ___  4 We are pleased to invite you. ___  5 Let me know by Thursday. ___
Useful phrases: Look what came today! · What does RSVP mean? · at the latest · One thing at a time!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 12.4 Reading a card aloud: pause at the commas. on Friday the twenty-fourth of October ⏸ at six o'clock in the evening ⏸ at the Rose Hall, Homs. Listen and repeat.

Work in pairs. A has an invitation card and reads it to B. B asks for the missing information and then answers (yes or no). Then swap cards.

Writer's boxA formal reply: Dear… → thank them → yes or no clearly → how many people / when you arrive → good wishes → Best regards + your name (+ who you are).A friendly reply: one or two lines, a joke is fine, but still answer the question (how many of you, and when).Match the register: a card that says We are pleased to invite you gets We will be pleased to attend, not Count me in.Never leave out the number of people. The family is counting chairs and food.

12.3  Congratulations!
Function: good wishes and congratulations   ·   Grammar: I hope… ; Wishing you… ; fixed phrases   ·   Pronunciation: word stress – con·gra·tu·la·tions
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What do you say in English for these? a birthday · a wedding · a new baby · a new job · passing an exam · a new house · a feast
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Congratulations! | Wishing you every happiness
 | 
 | 
b  In your language, what do you say when somebody buys something new, or comes back from a journey? Is there an English phrase for it?
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. How many pieces of good news are there?   A one   B two   C three
b  Listen again. 1 What is Nour's news?   2 What does Ms Huda ask the class to do?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 12.5  Good news in class
Ms Huda | Before we start, we have some news. Nour, would you like to tell them?
Nour | I finished my training at TechCare. From Sunday I'm a junior technician – a real contract.
Class | Congratulations! · Mabrouk! · Well done, Nour!
Omar | Congratulations, Nour. You worked very hard for that.
Nour | Thank you. I was so nervous on Thursday.
Ms Huda | And there's more. Hiba's cousin is getting married on the 24th, and the whole class is invited.
Lina | Congratulations to your cousin! Give her our best wishes.
Hiba | I will. Thank you.
Ms Huda | So, two things to learn today. First: in English we say "Congratulations" for something a person did – an exam, a job, a new house.
Omar | And for a wedding?
Ms Huda | For a wedding we usually say "Congratulations" to the couple and "Best wishes" or "We wish you every happiness". For a new baby: "Congratulations" to the parents.
Lina | And if somebody is ill?
Ms Huda | Then it isn't congratulations – it's "Get well soon". Different box! Now, everybody: write one line for Nour and one for Hiba's cousin.

Language boxGood wishes – choose the right oneOccasion | You say | You write (card or message)
Birthday | Happy birthday! · Many happy returns! | Wishing you a wonderful year. · Have a lovely day!
Wedding / engagement | Congratulations! · Best wishes! | We wish you every happiness. · Congratulations to you both.
New baby | Congratulations! | Congratulations on your beautiful news. · Welcome to the world, little one.
New job / exam / graduation | Congratulations! · Well done! | Congratulations – you worked very hard for this. · Well done, and good luck in the new job.
A feast / holiday | Happy Eid! · Happy New Year! | Wishing you and your family a happy Eid.
⚠ Somebody is ill or sad | Not congratulations! | Get well soon. · I'm very sorry to hear that. (Unit 10)
Grammar – I hope / Wishing you
I hope + present simple: I hope you have a wonderful day. · I hope everything goes well.
Wishing you + noun: Wishing you every happiness. · Wishing you a lovely year.
Congratulations on + noun or -ing: Congratulations on your new job. · Congratulations on passing your exam.
Answering good wishes: Thank you! · Thank you very much. · That's very kind of you. · Thank you – and the same to you! (for a feast)
Practise: What do you say?  1 Your friend passed his driving test. ______  2 Your neighbour has a new baby. ______  3 It's the first day of the Eid. ______  4 Your colleague is in hospital. ______  5 Your cousin is getting married tomorrow. ______
Useful phrases: Would you like to tell them? · You worked very hard for that. · Give her our best wishes. · I was so nervous. · Different box!
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 12.6 Stress: con·gra·tu·LA·tions · HAP·py BIRTH·day · best WISH·es. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A reads a situation from the card; B gives the right good wishes and one extra sentence. A answers. Then swap cards.

Writer's boxA good-wishes card: Dear + name → the good wishes phrase → one personal sentence (what you know about them) → a hope for the future → your name (or "from everybody in…").The personal sentence is what makes a card real: We all know how hard you worked this year. Without it, every card is the same card.Keep a child's card short, warm and a little funny. Keep a wedding or a feast card warm and calm.Never mention a problem in a congratulations card ("at last!", "after all those failed exams"). Save it for another day.

12.4  What should I bring?
Function: asking and giving advice about presents   ·   Grammar: should + Do you think…? ; It's for…   ·   Pronunciation: polite intonation in questions
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  What do people usually give in your country for: a wedding · a new baby · a new house · a birthday · a first visit to somebody's home?
We SAY… | We WRITE…
What should I bring? | Present ideas: sweets, flowers, something for the house
 | 
 | 
b  Is it a good present or not? Why? money · flowers · a plant · sweets · a photo frame · something you made · a voucher
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What do they decide to give?   A money   B something for the house   C flowers
b  Listen again. 1 Why doesn't Omar want to give money?   2 What does Ms Huda suggest?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 12.7  A present from the class
Lina | So – the wedding. What should we bring?
Hiba | Here most people give money in an envelope. It's normal and it really helps a young couple.
Omar | I know, but we're eighteen people. Eighteen small envelopes is strange. Do you think we should give one present from everybody?
Lina | That's better. Something for the house?
Hiba | They have everything, honestly. Her mother bought half of Homs.
Ms Huda | Can I suggest something? Omar is photographing the engagement party. What about a photo album – the pictures from that evening, printed, with a card from all of us?
Lina | Oh, that's lovely.
Omar | I can print twenty or thirty. And I can finish it before the 24th.
Hiba | Nobody else is going to give them that. Everybody gives money or glasses.
Lina | And we can all sign the first page.
Ms Huda | Shall we say two thousand each for the printing?
Omar | Less. Let me check the prices first.
Hiba | Perfect. One present, one card, eighteen names. Much better than eighteen envelopes.

Language boxAsking for advice about a presentWhat should I bring? · What should we get her? · Do you think we should give money? · Is it OK to bring sweets? · What do people usually give here?Giving adviceRecommending | Softening it | Saying no to an idea politely
You should bring something small. · Why don't you give a plant? · What about a photo album? | Maybe flowers? · I think sweets are always safe. · It depends on the family. | I know, but… · Honestly, they have everything. · That's a nice idea, but it's a bit expensive.
What about / Why don't we: What about a photo album? (+ noun) · What about asking her mother? (+ -ing) · Why don't we ask her mother? (+ base verb)
Talking about a present: It's for the house. · It's from all of us. · It's something she can use. · It isn't expensive, but it's personal.
Practise: Complete.  1 What ______ I bring to the party?  2 ______ about a plant for the new flat?  3 Why ______ we give one present from everybody?  4 Do you ______ we should bring the children?  5 It's something ______ he can use every day.
Useful phrases: Can I suggest something? · That's lovely. · Nobody else is going to give them that. · Let me check the prices first. · Much better than…
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 12.8 A question with a falling voice can sound hard. Let your voice go up at the end: Do you think we should give money?↗ Listen and repeat.

 5   Read   (7 min)

Omar: Everybody – about the wedding present. Lina, Hiba and I talked after the lesson and we have an idea. Writer's boxA message suggesting a group present: what the present is → why it's a good idea (two or three reasons) → how much and by when → what to do if you can't pay → how to answer and by when.Always give people a quiet way out: If somebody can't pay this month, that's absolutely fine. Money in a group is a sensitive thing.Finish with one clear action and a date: answer with 👍 by Thursday evening.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Hi everyone! About ______________. What about ______________ from all of us?   I think it's a good idea because ______________ and ______________.   It costs about ______ each. If ______________, that's absolutely fine.   Please answer by ______________ so I can ______________.   ______
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
What about + noun or -ing; Why don't we + base verb | 
Reasons, the cost, and a quiet way out | 
One clear action with a date | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write a group message (60 words or more) suggesting a present for a real or invented occasion: a colleague leaving, a teacher retiring, a wedding, a new baby, a neighbour who helped everybody. Give your reasons and make it easy to say yes.







 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Reply to your partner's message as a member of the group. ★ I'm in – great idea.  ✓ Can you say by when we should pay?  ? Can I give something instead of money?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Two messages are read aloud. Which one would get the fastest "yes" from this class, and why?
HomeworkWrite the message you would send three days later (30+ words): thank the people who answered, say how much money you have, and remind the others politely.

12.5  While we were waiting, the lights went out
Function: telling the story of a day you remember   ·   Grammar: past continuous with when / while   ·   Pronunciation: was / were weak forms
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  Think of a celebration that didn't go as planned – rain, no electricity, somebody late, too much food, not enough chairs. Tell the class in one sentence.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
While we were waiting, the lights went out. | It was the longest ten minutes of the evening.
 | 
 | 
b  Which happened first?  We were eating when my uncle arrived.  ·  When the music started, everybody stood up.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. What happened at the wedding?   A the bride was late   B the electricity went off   C it rained
b  Listen again. 1 What were people doing when it happened?   2 What did Omar do?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 12.9  The night of the wedding
Ms Huda | So! Tell me about Friday. How was the wedding?
Lina | Unforgettable. But not for the normal reasons.
Ms Huda | Oh dear.
Hiba | Everything was perfect at the beginning. We arrived at six, the hall was beautiful, my cousin looked wonderful. And then, while we were waiting for the dinner, the electricity went off. The whole street.
Ms Huda | The whole street!
Lina | Completely dark. Two hundred people, and you could hear one baby crying somewhere.
Hiba | My uncle was making a speech when it happened. He stopped in the middle of a sentence.
Omar | And then somebody started singing. I think it was Rasha's grandmother.
Lina | Everybody joined in. In the dark, two hundred people singing – I will never forget it.
Omar | While they were singing, I ran to the car and brought my camera light. It isn't strong, but it was something.
Hiba | When the lights came back twenty minutes later, nobody was angry. Everybody was laughing.
Ms Huda | And the dinner?
Hiba | Cold. Nobody cared. It was the best part of the evening.

Language boxGrammar – past continuous (was / were + -ing)We use it for a longer action that was happening, and the past simple for the short action that interrupted it.The long action (past continuous) | The short action (past simple)
While we were waiting for the dinner, | the electricity went off.
My uncle was making a speech | when it happened.
While they were singing, | I ran to the car.
Forms: I / he / she / it was waiting · you / we / they were waiting · He wasn't listening. · Were you dancing? – Yes, I was. / No, I wasn't. · What were you doing?
when + past simple (the short action): when the lights came back.   while + past continuous (the long action): while we were waiting.
⚠ Both parts can be past simple if the actions follow each other: When the lights came back, everybody clapped.
Practise: Put the verbs in the right tense.  1 While we ______ (eat), my uncle ______ (arrive).  2 I ______ (take) photos when the music ______ (start).  3 What ______ you ______ (do) when the lights ______ (go) off?  4 She ______ (not listen) – she ______ (talk) to her sister.
Useful phrases: Unforgettable. · But not for the normal reasons. · Oh dear. · He stopped in the middle of a sentence. · Nobody cared. · I will never forget it.
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 12.10 was and were are weak in the middle of a sentence: /wəz/ /wə/ — We were WAITing. He was MAKing a speech. Listen and repeat.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A tells the story on the card using while and when at least twice. B reacts (Oh dear! · Really? · What did you do?) and asks three questions. Then swap cards.
Story card A – the birthday | Story card B – the graduation
6.00 we were decorating the room | 10.00 we were waiting outside the hall
→ the cake fell on the floor | → it started to rain
7.00 the guests were arriving | 11.00 my brother was getting his certificate
→ we were still cleaning the kitchen | → my phone battery died, no photos
8.00 everybody was laughing about it; we bought sweets instead | 12.00 a man we didn't know sent us all his photos
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write two sentences from the story with while or when.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Tell your partner about a real celebration you remember – a wedding, a feast, a birthday, a graduation, a first day at work. Say: where you were and what people were doing, what happened, and how it finished. Use while and when. Your partner asks four questions and reacts. Then swap, and tell a second partner the same story – faster and better.
 4R   Report   (6 min)
Tell the class your partner's story:  Fadi was at his sister's graduation. While the family was waiting outside the hall, it started to rain and everybody ran inside. His brother was getting his certificate when Fadi's phone died, so they have no photos of the moment. But a man they didn't know was filming, and the next day he sent them everything.
Listener task: whose story is the funniest? Whose is the most beautiful?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read Lina's message to her sister. Answer the questions.
The wedding – you missed something 😄Dear Rana,I'm still laughing. You have to hear about Friday.Everything started perfectly. We arrived at six, the Rose Hall was full of flowers, and Rasha looked beautiful.Then, while we were waiting for the dinner, the electricity went off – the whole street. 1  What three things does Lina say about the beginning?
2  What was the uncle doing when the lights went off?
3  How does she finish the story?
Writer's boxA story message: a title that makes them want to read → set the scene (where, when, how it looked) → then the problem (Then, while we were…) → what people did → how it finished → what you think about it now.Set the scene with the past continuous and tell the events with the past simple. That is what makes a story sound like a story.One good detail beats five: he stopped in the middle of a sentence · you could hear one baby crying.Finish with a feeling, not a fact: It was the best wedding I have been to.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)

Then, while we were ______________, ______________. ______________ was ______________ when it happened.   ______________. While ______________, ______________.   When ______________, ______________.   It was ______________.   Love, ______
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
was / were + -ing for the long action; past simple for the short one | 
while + continuous, when + simple | 
All parts: scene, problem, what people did, the end, your feeling | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read your while sentence before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write the story of a day you remember (70 words or more) – a celebration, a journey, a first day, a day when everything went wrong. Set the scene, tell what happened, and finish with how you feel about it now.








 7P   Respond   (5 min)
