HomeworkRewrite one paragraph of your story (30+ words) adding two more past continuous sentences – what other people were doing at that moment.

12.6  It's polite to take your shoes off
Function: explaining a custom to a visitor   ·   Grammar: It's + adjective + to ; You don't have to… but   ·   Pronunciation: don't have to /həftə/
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready   (6 min)
a  A visitor is invited to a family home in your country for the first time. Write five things they should know.
We SAY… | We WRITE…
It's polite to take your shoes off. | Customs: what to do and what to avoid
 | 
 | 
b  True for your country? You should arrive exactly on time. · You must refuse food twice before you accept. · You take a present for the children. · You stay two hours.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Books closed. Listen. Where is Mr Lang going?   A to a restaurant   B to Omar's family home   C to a wedding
b  Listen again. 1 What time should he arrive?   2 What does Lina say about saying no to food?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 12.11  Before the visit
Mr Lang | Omar's family invited me for lunch on Friday. I'm a little nervous – tell me the rules.
Lina | There aren't really rules, but there are customs. First: they said one o'clock, so arrive at about one fifteen.
Mr Lang | Late? In Germany that's terrible.
Lina | Here, exactly on time means the family is still cooking. Fifteen minutes is perfect. Half an hour is also fine.
Mr Lang | Good. And a present?
Lina | You don't have to bring anything, but it's nice to take sweets from a good shop, or something small for the children. Not something expensive – then they feel uncomfortable.
Mr Lang | Shoes? I've heard about the shoes.
Lina | Look at the door. If there are shoes near the door, take yours off. In most homes it's polite to take them off, and nobody will tell you.
Mr Lang | And the food? I can't eat as much as Syrians eat.
Lina | (laughs) That's the difficult part. They will put food on your plate again and again. It's normal to say "no thank you, it was delicious" two or three times – and it's normal for them to continue.
Mr Lang | So how do I stop?
Lina | You put your hand on your heart and smile. It always works. And one more thing: don't leave immediately after the food. Stay for coffee – the coffee is the real visit.

Language boxGrammar – It's + adjective + to + verbIt's polite to take your shoes off. · It's normal to arrive fifteen minutes late. · It's nice to bring sweets. · It isn't usual to leave after the food. · It's better to ask than to guess.Useful adjectives: polite · impolite · normal · usual · nice · important · better · difficult · easyGrammar – you don't have to … but …This is how you explain a custom that is not a rule: You don't have to bring anything, but it's nice to take sweets. · You don't have to stay late, but people usually stay for coffee.It's a rule | It's a custom (not a rule) | Don't do it
You have to take your shoes off in the mosque. | You don't have to bring a present, but it's nice to. · People usually arrive a little late. | It isn't polite to leave straight after the food. · You shouldn't bring something very expensive.
Practise: Make sentences.  1 (polite / take your shoes off) ______  2 (normal / arrive fifteen minutes late) ______  3 (not usual / leave after the food) ______  4 (you don't have to / bring a present / but / nice / sweets) ______
Useful phrases: Tell me the rules. · There aren't really rules, but there are customs. · That's the difficult part. · It always works. · And one more thing…
Pronunciation. 🔊 Track 12.12 have to = /hæftə/ and don't have to = /dəʊnt ˈhæftə/. Listen and repeat: You don't have to bring anything.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in pairs. A is the visitor with the situation card; B explains the customs. Use It's + adjective + to three times and you don't have to… but… once. Then swap.
Situation card A | Situation card B
You are invited to a family home for lunch for the first time. | You are going to a wedding and you know nobody except one friend.
You are visiting a colleague who is ill, at his home. | You are invited to a family's house during a feast.
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two pairs perform. Listener task: write three pieces of advice. Which one would surprise a visitor most?
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
a  Your partner is a visitor from another country who is coming to your home next Friday. Explain everything they need to know: the time, the present, the shoes, the food, the coffee, when to leave.
b  Now ask your partner: what is different in another country they know, or have read or heard about? Find two differences and discuss them – neither one is wrong, they are just different.
 4R   Report – mediation   (6 min)
Tell the class:  I explained a first visit to a Syrian home. I said it's normal to arrive about fifteen minutes late, and it's polite to take your shoes off if you see shoes at the door. You don't have to bring a present, but it's nice to bring sweets. Hiba told me that in some places people arrive exactly on time and the visit is shorter – so a visitor from there might leave right after the food, and here that looks cold.
Listener task: write one custom that is the same everywhere, and one that is different.   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING
 5   Read   (7 min)
Read the page Lina wrote for the centre's website. Answer the questions.
VISITING A SYRIAN HOME – a short guide for our foreign guestsThe time. If the family says one o'clock, arrive at about one fifteen. It's normal to be a little late, and exactly on time usually means the family is still cooking.A present. You don't have to bring anything, but it's nice to bring sweets from a good shop, or something small for the children. It isn't a good idea to bring something very expensive, because then the family feels they have to give something back.At the door. Look at the floor near the door. If you see shoes there, it's polite to take yours off. Nobody is going to tell you.The food. Your hosts will put food on your plate again and again. It's normal to say "no thank you, it was delicious" two or three times, and it's normal for them to continue. Put your hand on your heart and smile – it always works.After the food. Don't leave immediately. It isn't polite to go straight after lunch: stay for coffee and conversation. The coffee is the real visit.And finally. Nobody is going to be angry if you make a mistake. People here are happy that you came.
1  Why shouldn't a visitor bring an expensive present?
2  How does the guide tell you to stop the food politely?
3  Why is the last paragraph important for a nervous visitor?
Writer's boxA customs guide: a short section for each moment (the time · a present · at the door · the food · after the food) → a kind last line.Explain the reason, not just the rule: because then the family feels they have to give something back. A reason is easy to remember; a rule is easy to forget.Use It's normal / polite / nice to… and You don't have to… but…, not You must everywhere. Most customs are not laws.End by taking the pressure off: Nobody is going to be angry if you make a mistake.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
VISITING ______________ – a short guide for ______________   The time. ______________. It's normal to ______________.   A present. You don't have to ______________, but ______________. It isn't a good idea to ______________, because ______________.   ______________. It's polite to ______________.   The food. ______________.   And finally. ______________.
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capital letters and full stops | 
It's + adjective + to + verb (not It's polite taking) | 
You don't have to… but… used at least once | 
A reason for each custom, and a kind last line | 
 6R   Share   (3 min)
Read one custom before and after the fix.
 7   Write freely   (12 min)
Write a short customs guide (70 words or more) for a visitor to your country, your city or your village. Choose one situation: a family home · a wedding · a feast · an office · a market. Give reasons.








 7P   Respond   (5 min)
Read your partner's guide as a nervous visitor. ★ This makes me feel ready.  ✓ Add why, not only what.  ? What happens if I make a mistake?
 7R   Share   (4 min)
Put the guides together: this is our Class 4B guide for visitors to Syria. Which custom is the hardest to explain?
HomeworkWrite about one custom from another country (30+ words) that you find interesting or surprising – and say what a Syrian visitor there should know.

12.7  Review & Mediate: The invitation and the programme
Function: explaining an invitation and a programme to a guest   ·   Recycling: Units 1–12   ·   Exam practice: A2 Key Reading Part 4, Writing Part 6, Listening Part 5
LESSON A – SPEAKING
 1   Get ready – Unit 12 quiz   (6 min)
In teams. Correct the mistakes! 1 When the guests will arrive, we eat.  2 It's polite taking your shoes off.  3 While we ate, the lights went off.  4 Congratulations for your new job.  5 What about to give a photo album?  6 Everybody clean the house before the feast.
 2   Listen and read   (12 min)
a  Read the invitation and the programme that came with it.
AL-YASMIN CENTRE · END-OF-YEAR EVENINGWe are pleased to invite you and your family to our end-of-year eveningon Thursday 19 December at 5.30 pm, in the big hall (second floor).RSVP to Mr Bassam by 12 December · 011 332 4455Programme5.30  Doors open · tea and coffee in the corridor6.00  Welcome from the director (10 minutes)6.15  Certificates – levels A1 and A27.00  Short break · photographs7.20  Certificates – levels B1 and B28.00  Music from the centre's group, and dinnerPlease note: the certificates for each level take about 45 minutes. If you only want to see your own level, come 15 minutes before it starts. Children are welcome. There is no parking in front of the building – use the street behind. Entrance is free, but every family should bring one dish for the dinner.
b  Books closed. Listen. Nour explains the evening to her uncle on the phone. What time does she tell him to come, and why?
c  Books open. Listen and read.
🔊 Track 12.13  Explaining the evening
Uncle | Nour, I got your message. What is this evening exactly?
Nour | It's the centre's end-of-year evening, on Thursday the 19th of December. The doors open at half past five.
Uncle | And it finishes when?
Nour | After eight – there's music and dinner at eight, so about ten, I think.
Uncle | Four hours! I'm not sitting in a hall for four hours.
Nour | (laughs) You don't have to. My level is A2, and the A2 certificates are at a quarter past six. The paper says come fifteen minutes before your level, so if you come at six, that's perfect.
Uncle | Six. Good. Is there a car park?
Nour | No – not in front of the building. You have to use the street behind.
Uncle | Fine. Does it cost anything?
Nour | The evening is free, but every family brings one dish for the dinner. My mother is making kibbeh, so that's our dish – you don't have to bring anything.
Uncle | And can I bring the children?
Nour | Yes, children are welcome. There's a break at seven for photos – bring them then, they'll love it.
Uncle | Six o'clock Thursday. I'll be there. Congratulations, by the way. Your mother told me about the contract.

Language box – explaining an invitation and a programmeGive the five things first: what · when · where · how to answer · how much.Then read the programme for the person, not to them – tell them the time that matters to them: My level is at a quarter past six, and the paper says come fifteen minutes before, so come at six.Explain the practical conditions, which is where people get it wrong:No parking in front → You have to use the street behind.   Entrance free, one dish per family → It's free, but every family brings one dish.   Children welcome → You can bring the children.Say what they don't have to do: You don't have to stay four hours. · You don't have to bring anything – my mother is making our dish.Useful phrases: What is this evening exactly? · It finishes when? · You don't have to. · that's perfect · I'll be there.
 3   Practise   (7 min)
Work in groups of three. A has an invitation card (don't show it!). A explains it to B and C, who ask about the practical things (time, parking, children, money, what to bring). They decide when to arrive and what to take. Then change roles with the other card.
Event card A – A school open day | Event card B – A neighbourhood iftar
Saturday 8 March, 9.00–13.00 | Saturday, after the sunset prayer, in the street
9.00 classrooms open · 10.30 the children sing · 11.30 tea with the teachers · 12.30 photographs | Everybody brings one dish · the tables are ready at 5.30 · the food starts after the prayer
Parents of A2 children: the singing is the important part | Families with children: there is a corner with games
No cars in the school street · free · tea and sweets provided | Free · bring your own chairs if you can · finishes about ten
 3R   Perform   (5 min)
Two groups perform. Listener task: write when they decided to arrive, what they are taking, and one condition they remembered.
 4   Your turn   (9 min)
Think of a real event you know about: a family celebration, a conference or course, a school event, a religious evening, a wedding in your family. Explain it to your partner so completely that they could go without asking anybody else. They must ask about anything you leave out. Then swap.
 4R   Report – mediation   (6 min)
Tell the class what your partner explained and what you decided:  Samir explained his nephew's graduation party. It's on Saturday the 14th at five, at their house in Jaramana. It's free, obviously, but everybody takes something sweet. There's no parking in their street, so you leave the car on the main road and walk five minutes. Children are welcome. I decided I would go at about half past five, because nothing starts at five, and I would take sweets from the shop near my house.
Listener task: did they explain all the conditions? What did they forget – parking, money, what to bring, what time it really starts?   Board clinic: your teacher writes three mistakes from the reports on the board. Correct them together.
LESSON B – WRITING AND UNIT CHECK
 5   Read – exam practice (A2 Key Reading Part 4)   (7 min)
Read the text and choose A, B or C for each question.
A WEDDING IN HOMSLast month our English class went to a wedding together. Our classmate Hiba invited us – her cousin Rasha was getting married – and eighteen of us travelled from Damascus by bus.We discussed the present for two weeks. Here, most guests give money in an envelope, and for a family that is very useful. But eighteen envelopes from one class seemed strange, so our teacher suggested something else: our classmate Omar was photographing the engagement party the week before, so we printed his photographs in an album and we all signed the first page. It cost each of us very little, and nobody else gave them anything like it.The wedding itself was unforgettable, but not for the usual reasons. We arrived at six and the hall was beautiful. Then, while we were waiting for the dinner, the electricity went off – not only in the hall, but in the whole street. Two hundred people sat in the dark. Rasha's uncle was making a speech and he stopped in the middle of a sentence.After a minute somebody started to sing, and then everybody was singing. Omar ran out to his car and came back with his camera light. When the electricity came back twenty minutes later, nobody was angry; everybody was laughing. The dinner was cold and nobody cared.On the bus home at two in the morning, we agreed: we will all forget the flowers and the food, but none of us will forget those twenty minutes in the dark.
1  Why did the class decide not to give money?     A They had no money.   B Eighteen separate envelopes seemed strange.   C The family asked them not to.
2  Who had the idea of the photo album?     A Omar   B Hiba   C the teacher
3  What was Rasha's uncle doing when the lights went out?     A eating dinner   B making a speech   C taking photographs
4  What did Omar do during the twenty minutes?     A He sang with everybody.   B He went to get a light from his car.   C He phoned the electricity company.
5  How did the guests feel when the lights came back?     A angry   B tired   C happy
6  What does the writer say at the end?     A The food was the best part.   B They will remember the dark minutes most.   C They came home early.
 6   Write with help   (9 min)
Write a message to a friend explaining the end-of-year evening (use the invitation and programme in Stage 2).
Hi ______!   The centre's end-of-year evening is on ______________ at ______________, in ______________. You have to answer ______________ by ______________.   Our level gets the certificates at ______________, so come at ______________.   It's free, but ______________. There's no parking ______________, so ______________. You can ______________ the children – there's a break at ______ for photos.   ______
 6P   Check together   (5 min)
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
The five things: what, when, where, answer by, cost | 
The time that matters to your friend, with the reason | 
The conditions: parking, one dish, children | 
Something they don't have to do | 
 7   Write freely – exam practice (A2 Key Writing Part 6)   (10 min)
Your English friend Sam is going to a celebration with your family next week.Write an email to Sam.  In your email:  •  say what the celebration is and when it starts  •  say what he should bring  •  tell him one thing people do that he should know.Write 25 words or more.





 ✓   Unit 12 check   (15 min)
A  Grammar and vocabulary. Choose the correct answer.
1  Before the feast, everybody ___ the house.   a) clean   b) cleans   c) cleaning
2  When the guests ___, we say "welcome".   a) will arrive   b) arrive   c) arriving
3  It's polite ___ your shoes off.   a) take   b) to take   c) taking
4  You ___ bring a present, but it's nice.   a) don't have to   b) mustn't   c) shouldn't
5  While we ___ for the dinner, the lights went off.   a) waited   b) were waiting   c) are waiting
6  My uncle was making a speech when it ___.   a) happened   b) was happening   c) happens
7  Congratulations ___ your new job!   a) for   b) on   c) to
8  What ___ giving one present from everybody?   a) about   b) if   c) we
B  Listening (A2 Key Listening Part 5 style – matching). 🔊 Track 12.14 Ms Huda talks about five people in the class. What is each person's news? Write a letter A–G. There are two extra letters.
People | News

🔊 Track 12.14  Audio script (read after the check)
Ms Huda | Before we finish the year, let's remember the good news in this class. Nour finished her training at TechCare and she has a real contract now – she starts as a junior technician. Hiba went to her cousin Rasha's wedding in Homs last month, and she took all of us with her, which I think is the kindest thing anybody did this year. Lina and her family finally moved into the new flat in Mezzeh – after four months of waiting, she says the kitchen still has no door. End-of-Book Test  ·  Book 1  ·  Units 1–12
Proactive English · A2 · Syria Edition
Information for the studentThere are four papers: Reading (30 marks) · Writing (30 marks) · Listening (20 marks) · Speaking (20 marks). Total: 100 marks.Time: Reading and Writing 70 minutes · Listening about 25 minutes · Speaking 8–10 minutes in pairs.Write your answers on the paper. For the Listening you will hear each part twice.This test is in the format of the Cambridge A2 Key exam. Everything in it comes from Units 1–12 of this book.
Paper | Parts | Marks | Your mark
Reading | 5 | 30 | 
Writing | 2 | 30 | 
Listening | 4 | 20 | 
Speaking | 2 | 20 | 
TOTAL |  | 100 | 

Paper 1  ·  Reading   (30 marks · 40 minutes)
 Part 1   Six short texts. For each one, choose A, B or C.   (6 marks)
1   AL-SHIFA PHARMACY   Closed 1.00–4.00 for lunch. For urgent medicine outside these hours, please go to Al-Noor Clinic, Mezzeh.
A The pharmacy is closed all afternoon.   B You can get urgent medicine somewhere else between one and four.   C The clinic sells the same medicine at any time.
2   Message from Lina to Rana:  "The bus leaves at eight, not half past – they changed it yesterday. I'll be at the station at a quarter to. Don't run, but don't stop for coffee either!"
A The bus time is earlier than before.   B Rana should buy coffee at the station.   C Lina is going to arrive at eight.
3   AL-YASMIN CENTRE – LIBRARY   You may borrow two books for two weeks. Write your name and the date in the book at the desk. Do not take books out of the building without writing them in the book.
A You can't take books home.   B You must write your name and the date before you take a book out.   C You can borrow books for two months.
4   Email from Mr Khaled to Lina:  "Thursday's meeting is now on Friday at ten. If Friday is difficult for you, tell me today and we will keep Thursday."
A The meeting is definitely on Friday.   B Lina can ask to keep the old day.   C The meeting is cancelled.
5   HOTEL AL-BAHR   Check-out is at 12.00. If you leave later, we must charge one more night. You may leave your bags with us until the evening – there is no charge for this.
A You can stay in the room until the evening.   B You pay for your bags after twelve.   C You pay for another night if you stay in the room after twelve.
6   Note on the fridge at the Al-Yasmin kitchen:  "Cooking club – Wednesday only. Please take your food home the same evening. Anything left on Thursday morning goes in the bin. Sorry! – Omar"
A You can keep food in the fridge all week.   B Food left until Thursday will be thrown away.   C The cooking club meets on Thursdays.

 Part 2   Read about three people. For each question, choose A, B or C.   (7 marks)
A  Samir | B  Dalia | C  The Haddad family
Samir is twenty-six and he works in a shop from nine until nine, six days a week. Friday is his only free day, and he cannot come on any other day. He wants English for his work. He learns quickly when he speaks, and he hates writing exercises at home. | Dalia is a university student with free mornings and no money. She reads English well but she is very shy and she never speaks in a big group. She wants to pass an exam next year, and she can study three hours every day. | The Haddads – two parents and three children – are moving to another country in six months. They need everyday English: shopping, the doctor, the school, the neighbours. They can only study together in the evening, after the children eat.

7   Who needs a class that meets on Fridays?     A     B     C
8   Who should join a class with a lot of speaking and almost no homework?     A     B     C
9   Who should start in a very small group of two or three people?     A     B     C
10  Who should look for a course that costs nothing?     A     B     C
11  Who should practise the language of shops, the doctor and the school?     A     B     C
12  Who should study together with their family after dinner?     A     B     C
13  Who has the most time to study every day?     A     B     C

 Part 3   Read the text and choose A, B or C for each question.   (5 marks)
NOUR'S FIRST MONTHWhen Nour started her training at TechCare, she could repair a phone in twenty minutes, but she could not answer the telephone in English. "The first week was terrible," she says. "A customer from Jordan phoned, and I gave the phone to my colleague Rami. I was so ashamed that I went to the English centre the same evening."Her manager, Mr Fouad, gave her two rules: write everything in the parts book, and never promise a day you are not sure about. "The second rule saved me twice," she says. "A customer asked for his machine on Thursday. I said Sunday. It was ready on Friday, and he was happy. If I had said Thursday, he would have been angry on Thursday evening."The hardest part was not the machines. It was the telephone. In the fourth week she took a call from the same Jordanian customer. She wrote the message on the slip – the name, the time, the problem, the number – and read the number back to him twice. "He said thank you, Miss Nour. He remembered my name. I put the slip on Mr Fouad's desk and I sat down and my hands were shaking."Last week she finished the training and got a contract. Her mother asked her what was different now. Nour thought about it and said: "Nothing in my hands. Everything in my mouth."
14  What was Nour's problem in the first week?   A She couldn't repair the machines.   B She couldn't speak English on the phone.   C Her colleague didn't help her.
15  Why did she go to the English centre?   A Her manager told her to.   B A customer complained about her.   C She felt ashamed after a phone call.
16  What does the second rule mean in practice?   A Give a later day than you think.   B Never tell the customer a day.   C Always finish the work on Thursday.
17  What did she do when the Jordanian customer phoned again?   A She gave the phone to Rami.   B She wrote the message and checked the number.   C She asked him to call Mr Fouad.
18  What does Nour mean at the end?   A Her hands are still shaking.   B Her repair work changed, not her English.   C Her English changed, not her repair work.

 Part 4   Choose the best word (A, B or C) for each gap.   (6 marks)
A HOLIDAY IN LATAKIALast November my sister and I spent a week in Latakia. We stayed in a small family hotel (19) ______ the sea road, twenty minutes from the centre. There was no lift, but breakfast was (20) ______ and the owner knew our names after one day.The first two days were wet, so we sat on the balcony and watched the rain. (21) ______ Monday the weather was sunny and warm, and we swam every morning.On the last Wednesday we took a boat trip at (22) ______ . It only lasted an hour and it cost forty thousand, with tea. We had to (23) ______ one day before, because they need at least six people.It was the best week of the year. Next November we are going (24) ______ do exactly the same thing.
19   A  in     B  on     C  at
20   A  included     B  inside     C  free time
21   A  Since     B  Until     C  From
22   A  sunset     B  the sun     C  evening time
23   A  order     B  book     C  ask
24   A  for     B  to     C  that

 Part 5   Write ONE word in each gap.   (6 marks)
AN EMAIL TO A HOTELSubject: Booking request – 3–6 MayDear Sir or Madam,I would (25) ______ to book one double room from Friday 3 May (26) ______ Monday 6 May – three nights.There (27) ______ two of us. Could you tell me how much it is (28) ______ night, and if breakfast is included?We would also like to know (29) ______ there is parking near the hotel, because we are coming (30) ______ car.Thank you very much.Best regards, Rana Khoury

Paper 2  ·  Writing   (30 marks · 30 minutes)
 Part 6   Write a short message. 25 words or more.   (10 marks)
Your English friend Sam is coming to your city next week.Write an email to Sam.  In your email:•  say what the weather is going to be like•  tell him one place he should visit, and why•  ask him one question about his journey.







 Part 7   Look at the three pictures. Write the story. 35 words or more.   (20 marks)
(Your teacher may show you the pictures. If not, the three pictures are described here.)
Picture 1 | Picture 2 | Picture 3
A family is sitting in a big hall at a wedding. Everybody is eating and talking. On the wall there is a clock: 8.00. | The hall is dark. People are holding up their phones for light. One man is standing with his mouth open – he was speaking. | The lights are on again. Everybody is laughing and clapping. An old woman in the middle is singing, and a young man is taking her photograph.

Paper 4  ·  Speaking   (20 marks · 8–10 minutes, in pairs)
 Part 1   The examiner asks you questions. (3–4 minutes)   (8 marks)
Phase 1 – about you. The examiner asks each student:
What's your name? How do you spell your family name?Where do you live? Who do you live with?What do you do – do you work or study?How do you come here – by bus, by car, or on foot? How long does it take?
Phase 2 – tell me about… The examiner chooses two of these for each student. Speak for about 30 seconds each time.
…your family.   ·   …what you do at the weekend.   ·   …a celebration in your family.…a journey you made this year.   ·   …your work or your studies.   ·   …the food you like.
 Part 2   Talk with your partner about the card. (4–5 minutes)   (12 marks)
a  Look at the card together. Say what you think about each idea, and then agree on one.
A VISITOR IS COMING TO YOUR CITY FOR ONE DAY. Where should you take them?1 the old city and the market   ·   2 a park by the river   ·   3 a famous old building outside the city   ·   4 a long lunch with your familyTalk about: how much time each one takes · how much it costs · what the visitor learns · the weather.
b  Then the examiner asks you both:
Which of these do you like best? Why?What should a visitor to your country never do?Do you prefer to travel alone or with other people? Why?

What the examiner is listening forCan you answer the question that was asked? · Can you give a reason every time, not only an opinion?Can you ask your partner something and react to what they say? · Can people understand you, even with mistakes?Do you use the language of this book: should · going to · because · I prefer · It's polite to · while / when?

When you finishYour teacher will give you your marks for the four papers and the "what to do next" advice from the Answer Key.Then: Book 2 – twelve more units, from here to the end of A2.
